# Codex project instructions

`AI.md` is the canonical shared guide for Claude Code and Codex. Read and follow it before coding, debugging, reviewing, or making architectural changes.

Use the local, Git-ignored `.ai/CURRENT_TASK.md` (create it from `.ai/CURRENT_TASK.template.md` when missing), the current Git state, and relevant Serena memories to recover active context. Keep this file as the detailed product/source map; structural changes must update it and the matching Serena memories.

# This is NOT the Next.js you know

This version has breaking changes — APIs, conventions, and file structure may all differ from your training data. Read the relevant guide in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` before writing any code. Heed deprecation notices.

<!-- END:nextjs-agent-rules -->

# Keeping This File Up to Date

**Every agent that makes structural changes to the project MUST update this file before finishing.**
Structural changes include: adding/removing tables, adding routes, adding/removing components, adding server actions, or changing architectural patterns.
If you skip this step, future agents will work from a stale map and make mistakes.

## Serena Memory Sync

Serena is an MCP code-intelligence assistant with its own persistent memory. Its memories must be kept in sync with this file.

**After updating AGENTS.md, also sync Serena:**

1. Activate the project: call `mcp__plugin_serena_serena__activate_project` with `"remnus-app"`.
2. List existing memories: call `mcp__plugin_serena_serena__list_memories` to see what needs updating.
3. Edit stale entries: call `mcp__plugin_serena_serena__edit_memory` for any memory whose content no longer matches AGENTS.md.

**Key memories to check:**

- `conventions` — i18n namespace list and count, coding rules
- `core` — source map, DB tables, component inventory

Do NOT create new Serena memories for every change — prefer editing existing ones. Only create a new memory if it covers a clearly standalone domain not already tracked.

---

# Project Details: Remnus

**Remnus** is the Human-Agent Collaborative Workspace — a **workspace** model where humans and AI agents read/write through the same MCP surface, not a Notion alternative. Users can create standalone pages (title + markdown) and customizable databases (dynamic columns, table/kanban/calendar views) — both living side by side in a unified sidebar. Each database row is also a page with markdown content.

## i18n & Localization

Remnus is fully internationalized using **next-intl v4** (App Router native). All user-facing text is loaded from translation files — **no hardcoded strings in components**.

### Supported Languages

| Code | Language          |
| ---- | ----------------- |
| `en` | English (default) |
| `tr` | Türkçe            |
| `hi` | हिन्दी            |
| `es` | Español           |
| `fr` | Français          |
| `de` | Deutsch           |
| `zh` | 中文 (Simplified)  |
| `ru` | Русский           |

### Locale Resolution (priority order)

1. `NEXT_LOCALE` cookie (user picks via `LanguageSwitcher`, 1-year expiry)
2. `Accept-Language` header (auto OS language detection via `negotiator` + `@formatjs/intl-localematcher`)
3. `en` fallback

**Clean URLs:** `localePrefix: 'never'` — URLs stay as `/db/123`, never `/en/db/123`. All pages live under `src/app/[locale]/`.

**Translation files:** `messages/{locale}.json` — `en.json` is the source of truth, **8 locales** (`en`, `tr`, `hi`, `es`, `fr`, `de`, `zh`, `ru`). **English fallback:** `src/i18n/request.ts` deep-merges a non-default locale's messages onto `en.json`, so any untranslated key gracefully falls back to English instead of showing a raw key name. **37 namespaces:** `Layout`, `Home`, `Auth`, `Admin`, `Workspace`, `WorkspaceSettings`, `Templates`, `Database`, `Dashboard`, `Editor`, `Page`, `Comments`, `IconPicker`, `Errors`, `LanguageSwitcher`, `MobileNav`, `Landing`, `Billing`, `Pricing`, `Contact`, `Download`, `Privacy`, `Updater`, `Sharing`, `UserSettings`, `Install`, `OAuthAuthorize`, `Security`, `Consent`, `Onboarding`, `DemoFeedback`, `Brand`, `Docs`, `Mailing`, `ProspectInvites`, `WhatsNew`, `Recurrence`. (`Docs` = UI chrome for the public **Wiki + Docs** sections; the long-form content bodies stay English-only — see **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog**. `Comments` drives `PageCommentsPanel` — see **Database Tables** → `page_comments`. `Dashboard` drives the dashboard route and its blocks — see **Dashboards**.)

### Rules for All Future Development

**Every new component or server action that surfaces user-facing text MUST follow these rules:**

1. **Client components** — `import { useTranslations } from 'next-intl'` and call `useTranslations('Namespace')` inside the component body.
2. **Server components / layouts** — `import { getTranslations } from 'next-intl/server'` and `await getTranslations('Namespace')`.
3. **Server actions** — same as above; use `getTranslations('Errors')` for error messages returned to the client.
4. **Add all new keys to ALL 8 files** before committing. Missing keys fall back to English (`en.json`) via the deep-merge in `request.ts`, then to the key name.
5. **No hardcoded display strings** — not even English fallbacks like `|| 'Untitled'`. Always use `t('key')`.
6. **Date formatting** — use `useLocale()` (client) or locale from `getRequestConfig` (server) instead of `'en-US'`.
7. **Namespace selection** — pick the closest existing namespace. Create a new one only for a clearly standalone domain (add to all 8 files and document here).

### Agent Task Management & Work Plan

When working on project tasks, agents MUST use the **remnus-mcp** server to interact with the **Work Plan** database:
1. **Querying Tasks:** Retrieve pending tasks (e.g. Sprint-specific) from the "Work Plan" database using the `query_database` tool with a `fields` projection (e.g. `fields: ["Status", "Sprint"]`) to trim columns. Row bodies are omitted by default (opt-in flip, 2026-07-08) — to read ONE task's write-up use `get_page` on that row (`mode: "outline"` first for long write-ups); never bulk-request bodies (`fields: [..., "content"]`) across the whole Work Plan (~15–20k tokens, measured 2026-07 on prod audit logs). To read a specific known set of task IDs (e.g. from a prior `query_database` call) in one round-trip instead of one `get_page` per task, use `get_pages`.
2. **Updating Status:** When starting a task, update its status to `In Progress` (if applicable), and when completed, update its status to `Done` in the database via the `update_page` tool.
3. **Writing Task Outputs:** Upon task completion, write a detailed markdown summary of the changes made, files modified, and test outcomes directly into the task's page content in the database using the `update_page` tool.

## Agent MCP Servers

Three MCP servers are registered **project-scoped** in [.mcp.json](.mcp.json) — a tracked file, so everyone who clones the repo gets them. Claude Code lists project-scoped servers as `⏸ Pending approval` until accepted once in this directory; `/mcp` then runs the OAuth flow per server. Restart the client after editing `.mcp.json` by hand.

| Server       | Endpoint                            | Use it for                                                                                                             |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `remnus_app` | `https://www.remnus.com/api/mcp`    | The production Remnus workspace — Work Plan tasks, pages, databases. See **Agent Task Management & Work Plan** above.    |
| `vercel`     | `https://mcp.vercel.com`            | Production truth for remnus.com: deployments, build logs, runtime logs/errors, Web Analytics, Vercel platform docs.      |
| `posthog`    | `https://mcp.posthog.com/mcp`       | Product analytics for the Remnus PostHog project (EU cloud): events, funnels, error tracking, replays, flags, SQL.       |

**All three authenticate over MCP OAuth — never put a credential in `.mcp.json`.** That file is tracked by git (`.env*` is ignored, `.mcp.json` is not), so an `Authorization: Bearer …` header there would publish a key. PostHog also accepts a personal-API-key header, but that mode exists for clients without OAuth — do not switch this repo to it. Each developer authorizes their own account and picks the Remnus org/project during consent, so access is per-person, not shared. If two PostHog servers show up in a session, the extra one is a personal client-level connector (e.g. a claude.ai connector), not this repo's config.

**Read freely, write deliberately.** Both external servers point at live production systems. Queries, log reads, and analytics lookups need no ceremony; anything that creates, mutates, or spends requires explicit user intent — same rule as every other production system in `AI.md` **Safety rules**.

### Vercel MCP

Remnus deploys to Vercel (see **Cross-Platform Architecture**), so this server is the fastest path to *what production actually did*, as opposed to what the code says it should do.

- **Prod-only failures:** `get_runtime_errors` / `get_runtime_logs` for a live 500, `get_deployment_build_logs` for a failed build, `list_deployments` / `get_deployment` to establish which commit is actually serving traffic. **Precedent:** the `generateStaticParams` × unconditional `headers()`/`cookies()` conflict documented in **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog** built green and only surfaced through a Vercel MCP runtime-error lookup (`DYNAMIC_SERVER_USAGE`) — that class of bug is invisible to `npm run build` and `tsc`.
- **Deploy state:** `list_projects` / `get_project` for env-var names, domains, and framework settings; `get_project_deployment_protection` for preview-protection state. Cron entries and function `memory`/`maxDuration` live in [vercel.json](vercel.json) — read the file for intent, use the MCP to confirm what shipped.
- **Web Analytics:** `get_web_analytics` covers visitors/pageviews at the edge. Product behaviour (funnels, retention, custom events) is PostHog's job, not this one — see below.
- **Docs:** `search_vercel_documentation` for *platform* questions (cron, functions, protection, domains). Next.js *framework* questions still go to `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` per **This is NOT the Next.js you know** at the top of this file.
- **Protected previews:** `get_access_to_vercel_url` / `web_fetch_vercel_url` to read a deployment behind deployment protection.
- **Never call on your own initiative — real money or real production mutation:** `buy_pro`, `buy_credits`, `buy_addon`, `buy_domain`, `get_purchase_quote` follow-through, `deploy_to_vercel`, `update_project_deployment_protection`.

### PostHog MCP

Exposes a **single `exec` tool** that dispatches CLI-style commands rather than one tool per operation:

```text
exec { command: "search <pattern>" }        # find the tool (prefer over `tools`, which dumps everything)
exec { command: "info <tool>" }             # read its schema ONCE, then reuse it
exec { command: "schema <tool> <field>" }   # REQUIRED for any field whose info output carries a `hint`
exec { command: "call <tool> <json>" }      # execute
```

Do not guess a schema, and do not re-run `info` before every call.

- **Schema-first, every time:** before any analytical `call`, confirm the event/property actually exists via `call read-data-schema` — including canonical-looking names like `$pageview`, which vary per team. Remnus's funnel is largely **custom** (`signup`, `mcp_token_created`, `agent_call`, `connect_*`, `oauth_*`, `mcp_token_mint_blocked`) — see **Activation Funnel Analytics** for the authoritative list and where each is emitted.
- **Active environment:** the Remnus project on **EU cloud** (`eu.posthog.com`). The server reports the live project/org ids in its own instructions at session start — read them there instead of hardcoding, and never paste the `phc_` project token into repo files (it belongs in `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN`; the build-time personal key is `POSTHOG_API_KEY`, see **Crash Reporting & Source Maps**).
- **Person-on-events is enabled:** `person.properties.*` on the events table reflects the value at ingest time, not the person's current value. Same person, different values across events — by design, not a bug to work around.
- **Good uses:** verifying a newly instrumented event actually arrives after a deploy (the step that lint/tsc can't cover); querying the activation funnel, retention, or lifecycle; inspecting `$exception` issues from **Crash Reporting & Source Maps** and confirming source maps resolved; pulling a session replay for a reported bug; checking a feature flag's real rollout state; `execute-sql` for anything the typed `query-*` tools can't express.
- **Dashboards/insights are no longer "manual-only":** **Activation Funnel Analytics** deliberately leaves the funnel insight and the agent-add drill-down as "PostHog-side config, not code" — those can now be built through this server. Because that writes into the real Remnus project, do it only when the task asks for it.
- **Writes hit production:** creating or updating dashboards, insights, cohorts, feature flags, experiments, surveys, or annotations changes what the team sees. Explicit user intent required.

## Project Install (`npx remnus init`)

Turns any directory into a Remnus-connected project: **one project = one workspace**. Four pieces, in dependency order.

### 1. Workspace-pinned MCP endpoint

`/api/mcp/w/<workspaceId>` ([src/app/api/mcp/w/[workspaceId]/route.ts](src/app/api/mcp/w/[workspaceId]/route.ts)) alongside the original `/api/mcp`. Both mount the same [handler.ts](src/app/api/mcp/handler.ts) via an `McpEndpoint` descriptor.

**Why it exists:** MCP clients namespace stored OAuth state by a hash of the server URL — Remnus's own launcher does this (`SERVER_URL_HASH` in [mcpb/server/index.js](mcpb/server/index.js)). With one shared URL, two projects on two workspaces collide on one token file and the second silently opens the first's workspace. A per-workspace URL makes that state per-workspace at zero client cost.

**Invariants — do not break these:**
- A token whose workspace differs from the URL gets **403, not 401**. 401 would send the client back through OAuth with a credential that can never satisfy that URL.
- The workspace id is shape-checked (`isWorkspaceIdShape` in [src/lib/mcp/workspaceEndpoint.ts](src/lib/mcp/workspaceEndpoint.ts)) before it reaches a `WWW-Authenticate` header — an unchecked segment carrying CR/LF is header injection. Bad shape → 404, the same answer an unknown workspace gets.
- `/api/mcp` behaviour is frozen. Every already-connected client uses it.
- `verifyBearerToken` skips bcrypt for a secret it has already verified on this instance, by caching `sha256(secret)` keyed on the token row id + stored hash. It caches the *match*, never the auth result: the token row is still read on every request, so revocation and expiry apply immediately. Don't turn it into a token → context cache.
- **Agent access follows membership** ([src/lib/services/agentAccess.ts](src/lib/services/agentAccess.ts)). The same query that reads the token row LEFT JOINs the holder's `workspace_members` row (PAT `created_by` / OAuth `user_id`) and `user` row, and `effectiveAgentScope` decides per request: not a member (and not a site admin) → 401 (`pat_member_gone` / `oauth_token_member_gone` in the log), `viewer` → the session gets `read` whatever scope is stored. A PAT with `created_by = null` (its creator's account was deleted — see `performAccountDeletion`) keeps its stored scope. On top of that, `removeFromWorkspace` / `removeUserFromPool` call `revokeAgentAccess` and a switch to viewer calls `restrictAgentAccessToRead`, so the AI Agents panel and the agent quota match. Before 2026-09-25 a removed member's agent kept reading and writing. `npm run test:agent-access`.
- RFC 9728 metadata is served from **both** the bare `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` (where existing clients look) and the path-scoped `[...path]` route. Both must keep answering: the 401 challenge points at the path-scoped form, and a 404 there breaks auth discovery. `isProtectedMcpPath` is the single list of what gets a metadata document.
- The consent screen reads the RFC 8707 `resource` indicator and collapses the workspace picker to the pinned workspace, re-checking it server-side in `handleApprove` (the form field is still client input).

### 2. Install channel

`remnus init` never shows a token. The CLI invents a `deviceId`, opens `/install?device_id=…&project=…`, and polls `/api/install/poll` until the browser side lands ([src/lib/services/installSession.ts](src/lib/services/installSession.ts)).

- Storage reuses `client_auth_tokens` (**no migration**). The Tauri sign-in bridge stores a bare JWT there; install results are a JSON envelope tagged `kind: 'install'`, and a read that finds anything else reports "nothing pending" instead of consuming someone else's sign-in.
- One-time read, 5-minute TTL.
- `/api/install/poll` is the **only** public path (allowlisted in [src/auth.config.ts](src/auth.config.ts)); `no-store` is mandatory or a cached `{ready:false}` hangs the install forever.
- `/install` is **not** allowlisted, but it must not rely on `getCurrentUser()` for the redirect — that goes to a bare `/login` and drops the device id. The page builds its own `callbackUrl`, same as the OAuth consent screen.
- `mode=oauth` mints nothing and only carries the workspace choice back.
- **Who may connect what:** `init` (no `workspace` param) offers only workspaces the user **owns** — connecting a project is an owner's decision, and `mintAgentToken` is owner-only. `join` (`workspace=<id>`) reaches a workspace by the id the project already carries and needs only **membership**; it mints through `mintProjectAgentToken`, which is a separate function on purpose (see §5).

### 3. The `cli/` package

Standalone ESM Node package, **zero dependencies**, published to npm as `remnus`. Not part of the Next app — ignored in [eslint.config.mjs](eslint.config.mjs) alongside `mcpb/`.

- `init` writes `.mcp.json`, `.remnus/config.json`, `.remnus/credentials.json` (git-ignored, 0600), `.remnus/workspace-map.md` (git-ignored by default, §6) and a marked section in `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`. **Every writer edits in place**: other MCP servers, existing ignore rules and hand-written prose all survive a re-run. Markers make it idempotent.
- `.mcp.json` runs `npx remnus mcp` rather than a direct HTTP transport, so no secret sits in a committed file, both auth modes produce the same file, and there is one place that notices a broken connection. `--http` opts out.
- `mcp` is a stdio↔HTTPS bridge. **Nothing but protocol bytes may touch stdout** — every log in the package goes to stderr ([cli/src/lib/ui.js](cli/src/lib/ui.js)). On 401/403 it answers the request with a JSON-RPC error (never leave an agent hanging) *and* tells the human to run `remnus init`.
- OAuth mode delegates to `mcp-remote` against the pinned URL.
- Ignore rules come from one place, `ignorePatternsFor` in [cli/src/lib/map.js](cli/src/lib/map.js). `init`, `join` and `sync` all pass it to `ensureGitignore`, so a later writer can never drop a pattern an earlier one added.

### 4. Signed-in project windows (workspace-locked sessions)

`npx remnus open` (and the Claude Code `SessionStart` hook that runs it) opens the workspace **already signed in**, but only to that project's workspace.

**Flow:** CLI `POST /api/window/ticket` with the project PAT → server returns a path to `/api/window/activate?ticket=…` → CLI opens it with Chromium `--app` in a **per-workspace browser profile** (`--user-data-dir` under the OS app-data dir, [cli/src/lib/window.js](cli/src/lib/window.js)) → activate route calls `signIn('workspace-window')` → redirect to `/w/<id>`.

**The lock** ([src/lib/auth/workspaceLock.ts](src/lib/auth/workspaceLock.ts)): the session may do what the project's write token could already do over MCP — content inside that one workspace — and nothing at account or workspace-management level. It is **deny by default** at two chokepoints:
- `auth()` from `@/auth` is a wrapper that reads a locked session as **signed out**. Only [src/lib/auth/session.ts](src/lib/auth/session.ts) may call the raw `authWithWorkspaceLock`.
- `getCurrentUser()` **throws** for a locked session.

Code that must work in a window opts in via `getCurrentUserAllowingWorkspaceLock()` / `getSessionAllowingWorkspaceLock()` and then **must** call `assertWorkspaceLockAllows(user, resolvedWorkspaceId)` — on the workspace it resolved, **before** any admin shortcut. Opted in today: the private access helpers in `actions/{workspace,page,database,recurrence,comments,history,trash}.ts`, workspace listing/active-workspace (filtered to the lock), page knowledge (context policy stays strict), `getWorkspaceMembers`, download signing, the three content routes, the knowledge map (`actions/graph.ts` + `/graph/<workspaceId>` — another workspace's map is "not found" in a window, verified P12), the `(app)` layout + `/app`, `/api/upload` (must name the lock workspace), the download proxy, `/api/activity/ping` (change signal scoped to the lock). Workspace management (delete/rename/icon/hide) uses the strict `assertWorkspaceManagementAccess`.

**Invariants — do not break these:**
- A new server action or route uses `getCurrentUser()` / `auth()` unless it is content inside a workspace **and** confines itself to the lock. A forgotten opt-in is a feature that errors in the window; a careless opt-in is an escape.
- The `jwt` callback writes the lock claims on **every** sign-in (a normal login must clear an old lock) and **never** reads them from a client `session` update. A `workspace-window` sign-in without lock claims throws (fail closed).
- Locked sessions are re-validated per request in `session.ts`: 12h lock TTL, PAT not revoked/expired, write scope, `createdBy` = user, still a member. JWTs are stateless — without this, revoking the token would not close the window.
- Tickets ([src/lib/services/windowTicket.ts](src/lib/services/windowTicket.ts)): 32 random bytes, stored as `win_<sha256>` in `client_auth_tokens`, 60s, single-use via delete-`returning`. The ticket route requires a **write-scoped PAT** whose owner is still a member.
- The ticket route returns a **path**, not a URL — the CLI joins it onto its configured server and never follows a sign-in link to another origin.
- `activate` does not replace an existing **full** session of the same user with a locked one. The CLI only requests a ticket when it can open an isolated profile (`hasAppWindowBrowser`), and `openAppWindow` never falls back to a normal tab for a sign-in link.
- `/api/window/` is public in `auth.config.ts`; both routes authenticate themselves.
- **The window's UI is the workspace and nothing else.** The `(app)` layout derives one
  `isProjectWindow` boolean from the lock claim and passes it to `WorkspaceSidebar`,
  `MobileNavWrapper` and `ActivityTracker`. Never re-derive it on the client (user agent,
  window size): the lock claim is what actually decides which surfaces would work.
  Left out in a window: AI Agents, Plan/Billing, account Settings, PWA install, the admin
  link, sign-out, workspace create/switch/hide, the hidden-workspaces toggle, the workspace
  icon picker, workspace settings and the onboarding checklist (`OnboardingGuide` /
  `AgentDetectGuide` — `getOnboardingProgress` threw on every page load in a window until
  P9.5). Kept: the tree, the knowledge map (P12), Trash and What's New. Omitted in JSX,
  **not** hidden with CSS — `getUserAgentTokenCount` and `getMyTier` both call
  `getCurrentUser()`, which throws for a locked session, so rendering those buttons meant
  firing round-trips that could only fail.
- **The banner never offers "sign in".** The window lives in a throwaway Chromium profile
  (`--user-data-dir`), so signing in there rebuilds the same one-workspace window in a
  browser nobody uses, and the web cannot hand a link to the OS default browser
  (`target="_blank"` stays inside the isolated profile). `ProjectWindowBanner` therefore
  links to the public `/download` page and copies `origin + /app` to the clipboard; sign-out
  survives only as a demoted "End this session". `remnus open` prints the workspace URL for
  the same reason.

### 5. Joining a connected project (`npx remnus join`)

The second person's path. `.remnus/config.json` is **committed** (workspace id, name, MCP URL); `.remnus/credentials.json` is **not** (per-person token, git-ignored, 0600). So a teammate who clones the repo has the address and no key, and `init` was the wrong answer for them — it offered to *replace* the project's connection.

**Flow:** CLI `join` → same install channel as `init`, plus `workspace=<id>` on the URL → [`/install`](src/app/[locale]/install/page.tsx) renders `joinView` instead of the picker → member? mint via `mintProjectAgentToken`; not a member? `workspace_access_requests` row + owner email → result comes back over `/api/install/poll` carrying a `status` (`connected` | `requested` | `denied`).

**Invariants — do not break these:**
- **The workspace id is not a credential.** It is in a committed file, so everyone with a clone has it. Membership is re-read from the database on every render *and* again inside every action; the URL/form value is client input.
- **A non-member learns nothing.** Not the workspace name, not its members, not whether that id exists — `createAccessRequest` returns the same `pending` for an id matching no workspace (the row is simply not written). The name on the join screen is the *project* directory the CLI read off the joiner's own disk. Same rule in the poll payload: a non-`connected` result carries the project name, never the workspace's.
- **`mintAgentToken` stays owner-only.** The AI Agents panel mints there. `join` uses `mintProjectAgentToken` ([src/lib/actions/agentToken.ts](src/lib/actions/agentToken.ts)), which shares the private `issueAgentToken` (row + funnel capture + first-agent email) but applies its own gate. Widening the shared one would have silently given every member a token mint in the UI too.
- **Scope is clamped by role, not by the form.** `viewer` → read only, and a write request is **refused** (`Errors.viewerCannotWrite`) rather than quietly downgraded, so the CLI can explain it instead of writing a credential that fails on first use. The OAuth consent screen clamps a viewer to `read` the same way. A role or membership change *after* the join also reaches tokens already issued (see §1, "Agent access follows membership").
- **Each join revokes this person's previous token for this project** (matched on workspace + `createdBy` + the `Remnus CLI · <project>` name), *before* the limit check so a re-join is never blocked by the slot it is about to free. `countAgents` counts every un-revoked PAT and every join mints one, so without this a second machine would eat the owner's quota again. The CLI says so; it is never silent. **Quota is still per token, not per person** — see the open question below.
- **The quota belongs to the billing owner**, not the joiner (`checkCanAddAgent` → `resolveBillingOwner`). A blocked join reports `Errors.agentLimitReachedJoin` ("ask its owner"), not a raw limit error.
- **Approval is the seat-consuming act, not the request.** `approveWorkspaceAccessRequest` runs `checkCanAddSeat` at approval time and inserts the member as `member` — never the role the requester asked for, and never an owner. A seat refusal leaves the request **pending**, so the owner can free a seat and approve the same row.
- **Read-only is explained, never guessed.** A `connected` join result carries the joiner's own `role` (`InstallResult.role` → `/api/install/poll`); CLI ≥ 0.1.10 says "Read only was chosen on the connect screen" vs "your role is Viewer" instead of blaming the role for every read scope. `remnus doctor` (≥ 0.1.10) prints `Access: read and write | read only` from a `tools/list` probe (write tools carry `readOnlyHint: false`), so it reflects the holder's *current* role.
- **`join` writes exactly one file: `.remnus/credentials.json`.** Not `config.json` (its `calibrated` flag above all), not the `AGENTS.md` section, not `.claude/settings.json`. It *will* create a missing `remnus` entry in `.mcp.json` (a clone without one cannot reach Remnus) and a missing `.gitignore` block, but never overwrites either. `git status` after a join shows nothing.
- **`init` on an already-connected project hands over to `join`.** Replacing the connection needs `--reconnect` (or `--new`). Silently re-pointing a committed `config.json` forks a team's shared memory and nobody notices until both halves have content.
- One `workspace_access_requests` row per (workspace, user) **forever**, reused rather than re-inserted — that is what lets a `denied` row hold for the 7-day `DENY_COOLDOWN_MS` instead of being erased by an immediate re-send.
- The owner email (`access_request`, a new `email_log.kind` — plain TEXT, **no migration**) is best-effort and fires *after* the row is committed. It is treated as transactional in `canReceiveEmail` (an unsubscribe must not hide a teammate who is blocked); suppression still mutes it. Pending requests are visible in `MembersTab` regardless.

**Known gap, deliberately not solved here:** two people's agents writing the same page concurrently. `update_page` merges and there is no version/ETag check, so the later write wins field-by-field with no conflict signal.

### 6. The local workspace map (`.remnus/workspace-map.md`)

A cached copy of `getWorkspaceDigest` written next to the code, so an agent's **first** turn starts with the ids in hand. [cli/src/lib/map.js](cli/src/lib/map.js) fetches the digest resource over [cli/src/lib/rpc.js](cli/src/lib/rpc.js) (one stateless JSON-RPC POST — the endpoint needs no `initialize` first) and renders header + digest.

The honest case for it is **not** "fewer tokens than an MCP read" — the file enters context too. It is: no round-trip on the cold turn, it can be **grepped** (an MCP response is all-or-nothing), and the agent starts the session knowing which id to go to instead of search-then-read.

**Invariants — do not break these:**
- **It lists items in sidebar order**: `sortOrder`, then `createdAt`, then id — the same tie-break the sidebar uses (every new item gets `sortOrder: 0`). Ordering by id alone showed a shuffled tree, and calibration's "overview first" check read it as wrong (P9.5).
- **It is a cache and says so**, twice: the HTML comment header (`workspace`, `server`, `cursor`, `generated`, parsed by `readMapMeta`) and a human-readable blockquote. Nothing past its cursor is verified.
- **The digest carries the cursor, not the CLI.** `getWorkspaceDigest` takes it from `getChangeHeadCursor` **before** querying the items, so a write landing mid-assembly is reported by the next `get_changes_since` rather than falling through the gap. Generating a cursor on the CLI side would reintroduce that gap.
- **Cursor settling** (`settledCursor` in [src/lib/services/workspace.ts](src/lib/services/workspace.ts)): timestamps are second-granular. Within `HOT_SECOND_MS` of now the second is still open, so the cursor's id half stays `''` and that second is re-reported once; older than that, nothing can be stamped into it any more and the id becomes `'~'` (sorts above every uuid) so an idle poll returns empty instead of replaying forever. `get_changes_since` therefore returns `nextCursor` **always**, not only when `hasMore`.
- **The bridge refreshes it, off the protocol stream.** [cli/src/commands/mcp.js](cli/src/commands/mcp.js) refreshes once at start-up and after each successful write, debounced 1.5s so a bulk fill is one refresh. Write tools are learned from `tools/list` (`annotations.readOnlyHint === false`) with a hardcoded set as the pre-list fallback, so a new server-side write tool counts without a CLI release. A refresh failure is a single stderr warning — **never** a failed tool call, and never a byte on stdout.
- **Write-then-rename**, with a plain write as the Windows fallback (a locked file cannot be replaced), so a grepping agent never reads half a file.
- **Git-ignored by default**, since it changes on every agent write. `remnus sync --track` stores `trackMap: true` in the *committed* `config.json` so the whole team's CLI agrees; `--untrack` reverts. `doctor` warns (does not fail) when an older install's `.gitignore` block predates the map.
- **The `AGENTS.md` block is the contract**: map → delta → targeted read → search, last. It is rendered into every connected project and rides in every session's context, so a line that does not change behaviour does not belong in it.

### 7. Calibration guide + playbooks (`/wiki/calibrate`, `/wiki/playbooks`)

After `init`, an agent fills the workspace in from [docs/mcp/calibrate.md](docs/mcp/calibrate.md), fetched **live** from the instance (never copied into the project). Version 2 (2026-09-23) was redesigned from Serena's onboarding/self-check prompts, Cline's Memory Bank, Aider's repo map, Claude Code `/init` and Cursor rules; ~3,000 tokens (bytes/4), down from ~4,050 in v1. **Version 3** (2026-09-23, P9.5) came out of a field test — an independent headless Claude Code session calibrating a clone of a real Hytale mod, interrupted mid-build and resumed in a fresh session. It fixed what that agent actually got wrong: the first log was written with every step pre-ticked and invented ids, then never ticked while building (the guide now says: create unticked, tick each step as it lands, only ids from tool results; a resume checks unticked steps against the map before building); status options sent as `{name, group}`, which the server stored as `"[object Object]"` (server fixed, guide shows the shape); an unknown Lucide name; `npx remnus sync` missing from the published CLI (the guide now reads the map the bridge already refreshes); and 29 `[[Title]]` "links" in 13 bodies with zero `page_links` rows (the guide now gives the `data-page-link` form, and a v2→v3 re-run converts them — verified: 29 → 29 real links, `page_links` 0 → 23). ~3,290 tokens.

**Contract — do not break these:**
- **Agents read the raw markdown, `/wiki/calibrate.md`** (P9.5). The HTML page is 229 KB (~57k tok) against the 12 KB file (~3k); `curl` on the page pays ~19×, and a summarizing fetch tool (WebFetch) can drop rules. `init` prints the `.md` address (`calibrateUrlFor` probes it and falls back to `/wiki/calibrate` on an instance that predates the route) and renders it into the `AGENTS.md` block, both followed by "(read the whole file, e.g. with curl)" — in the field test one of three sessions reached for WebFetch first, which on an https instance would have handed it a summary (the hint is left out for the HTML fallback, which is too big to read whole); the raw route turns the guide's relative links into `/wiki/playbooks.md`, `/wiki/playbooks/game.md`, so the source keeps plain relative links that also work on GitHub.
- **`Guide version: N`** is stated in the guide. Bump it when the agent's behaviour changes, and add what the new version brings to *Running it again* step 2, so a re-run knows what an older calibration is missing.
- **Calibration Log** — a workspace page created *before* anything else: header (date, guide version, project commit), sources, the plan written before building, a ticked build checklist with ids, skips and open questions. It is the resume point (unticked steps = interrupted run; the `AGENTS.md` block tells a fresh session to resume) and the reviewable proposal a human watches in the live window. There is no approval gate — the guide explicitly says not to ask for plan approval.
- **`.remnus/config.json`**: the agent sets `calibrated: true`, `calibratedAt` (ISO) and `calibrationGuide` (the version). The CLI only ever writes `calibrated: false` (`init`); `sync --track` rewrites the file from what it read, so the extra fields survive; `init --reconnect` builds a fresh config on purpose (new workspace = not calibrated); `join` never touches it.
- **A second run extends, never rebuilds**: read the log (none = v1), scope to `git log <logged commit>..HEAD` plus what later guide versions added, never create a second database/page for a concept, never overwrite a body it didn't write in that run, never delete — `add_comment` instead.
- **Knowledge labels:** concept rows/pages/databases carry `knowledge` (`conceptType`, `tags` in the team's language *and* English, `sources` = repo-relative files). `bulk_create_pages` entries accept that subset (`BULK_KNOWLEDGE_INPUT` in [tools/write.ts](src/app/api/mcp/tools/write.ts), +366 B ≈ 92 tok model-visible on write sessions) and it is written by `recordGeneratedKnowledgeBulk`'s per-item `metadata` — without it, the guide's own "rows in one bulk call" rule left every calibrated row unlabelled. `prepare_context` ranks tags/sources at 2.5×; `search_workspace` does not read them (yet). Since P13 the `sources` are also what `get_related_pages` with `resource` (file → pages, asked before an agent edits a file) and the knowledge map's Code files layer read, so a calibration that skips them leaves both empty.
- **Links in calibrated content are `<a data-page-link href="/page/<id>">`** (`/db/<databaseId>[/<rowId>]`) — the only form `extractPageRefs` turns into `page_links` rows, which `get_related_pages`, context packs and the knowledge map read. The guide, `write-tools.md` and the bundled skill all say so; keep them in step if the serialization in `PageLinkNode.ts` ever changes.
- **Select/status options accept `value`, `name` or `label`** (`autoColorOptions` in [services/workspace.ts](src/lib/services/workspace.ts)); status groups accept `to do`/`in progress`/`done` aliases. An option with no text or an unknown group **throws** naming the column — it used to be stored as `"[object Object]"`, and since MCP has no "edit column options" call, the only repair was a destructive remove + re-add.
- **Every example icon in the guide/playbooks must be in `CURATED_ICON_NAMES`** ([src/lib/icons.ts](src/lib/icons.ts)) — an agent copies examples verbatim, and an unknown Lucide name is refused.

**Playbooks** ([docs/mcp/playbooks.md](docs/mcp/playbooks.md) + [docs/mcp/playbooks/](docs/mcp/playbooks/)): type-specific hints read at the end of calibration Phase 1 — web app/SaaS, API service, game (incl. mods/server plugins), data/ML, library/SDK. Mobile was deliberately left out of the first round (it shares most concepts with web app + API).
- **Selection:** at most two, by concrete *Pick it when* signals; none matching → none ("closest one" is the wrong answer); a monorepo combines parts, one section per part.
- **Fixed skeleton** (every playbook): descriptive first line (becomes the `/llms.txt` description) · the identical **Evidence first** sentence · *Pick it when* (manifest deps, file names, folders — never "looks like a game") · *Concepts to model* (columns as `Name` type, each with its `conceptType`; status options as `to do` → `in progress` → `done`) · *Example rows* with bodies · *Status screen* · *Don't*.
- **Extension rule:** ≤ ~1,200 tokens per playbook (today 707–896); column types only from `SchemaColumn` in [src/lib/templates.ts](src/lib/templates.ts) (no `id`); dashboard blocks and operators only from `DASHBOARD_BLOCK_CATALOG` / `dashboardFilterSchema` ([src/lib/dashboard/schema.ts](src/lib/dashboard/schema.ts)); add the row to the index table and the `WIKI_PAGES` entry.
- **Visibility:** the index is in the wiki nav and sitemap ("what would Remnus build for my kind of project?" is a pre-signup question); the playbooks are `hidden: true` like `calibrate` — agent-facing instructions, reached from the index, still listed in `/llms.txt`. Slugs are nested (`playbooks/game`); `rewriteLinks` resolves `playbooks/x.md` and `../x.md` so the same markdown links correctly on GitHub.

## Live refresh (the change signal)

How a human sees an agent's writes appear. Four pieces, and each one is load-bearing:

1. **`services/changeVersion.ts`** — one monotonic epoch-seconds number per caller, from
   `max()` over workspace items, standalone pages, databases, rows, comments **and
   `deleted_items`**. The tombstone branch is not optional: without it a delete *lowers*
   `max(updatedAt)` instead of raising it, so removing a page never triggered a refresh.
   Tombstones are insert-only, which also keeps the number monotonic. Emitted as a single
   `unionAll` statement — it is polled every few seconds, and against Turso the round-trips
   are the cost. Keep the `epochMax` TEXT/INTEGER guard (see the `createdAt` gotcha).
   `knowledge_metadata`/`knowledge_reviews` are deliberately **not** branches here (P10,
   measured: +~2 ms, about +50%, on every poll for no screen that needs it); the
   `prepare_context` corpus cache adds them in its own key instead — see **Performance
   Rules → Context & search read path**. `changeVersionQuery` exposes the same UNION
   unawaited so a caller can put it in a `db.batch`.
2. **`GET /api/activity/changes`** — the fast, read-only half. Body is `{"v":…}`, about
   twenty bytes. Do not grow it: this replaced a 10s `router.refresh()` poll that re-fetched
   ~100 KB of RSC payload per tick and was the dominant Fast Origin Transfer cost.
   `POST /api/activity/ping` still returns the same number, but every ping writes a
   `user_sessions` row, so it must never be called at the fast cadence.
3. **`ActivityTracker` adaptive cadence** — heartbeat stays at 30s. The change poll runs at
   2.5s only when something is happening: flat in a project window (that window exists to
   watch an agent work), and in a normal tab for ~90s after an observed change, backing off
   1.6× per quiet tick until it stands down entirely and the heartbeat carries the signal
   again. **An idle normal tab must keep costing exactly one request per 30s.**
4. **`lib/interactionGate.ts`** — the shared reason to defer a refresh, used by
   `useWorkspaceEvents` and `TabHost`. It blocks on a held pointer, a keystroke in the last
   1.5s, or an editable focused with a keystroke in the last 15s. It deliberately does
   **not** treat `mousemove` as activity: the 10s idle timer it replaced meant a human
   watching an agent deferred every refresh indefinitely just by holding the mouse, for a
   worst case of 40s+.

There is no server push. `lib/realtime/publish.ts` and its 45 no-op call sites were removed
rather than left looking live; an in-memory EventEmitter cannot fan out across Vercel's
multi-instance runtime, and adding external pub/sub is a separate decision.

## Color Theme

| Role                | Hex       | Tailwind token |
| ------------------- | --------- | -------------- |
| Main canvas bg      | `#1d1f23` | `neutral-950`  |
| Sidebar / card bg   | `#21252b` | `neutral-900`  |
| Content canvas bg   | `#282c34` | `neutral-850`  |
| Borders / dividers  | `#383b41` | `neutral-800`  |
| Silver text         | `#cccccc` | `neutral-100`  |
| Muted text          | `#d7dae0` | `neutral-50`   |
| Primary / accent    | `#445c95` | `blue-500`     |
| Destructive         | `#cd4d55` | `red-400`      |
| Success             | `#7fc36d` | `green-400`    |
| Warning             | `#cc7d45` | `amber-500`    |

Tokens defined via `@theme` overrides in `src/app/globals.css`.

## UI & Design Aesthetics

- **Flat and borderless:** Settings panels, drawers, sidebars — always `rounded-none`, no shadows.
- **Three-tier background:** `neutral-950` body frame → `neutral-900` sidebars/floating panels → `neutral-850` content/canvas. Separate with a single `border-neutral-800` line.
- **Flat-Line Separators:** Use `border-b border-neutral-850` + `hover:bg-neutral-800/10` rows instead of cards.
- **Auth Pages Exception:** `/login` and `/register` use `rounded-xl` cards and `rounded-lg` inputs. Do not apply this style inside the workspace.

## Technology Stack

- **Framework:** Next.js 16.2.6 (App Router)
- **Styling:** TailwindCSS, Lucide React icons
- **Database:** SQLite (`file:local.db`), Turso/Serverless compatible target.
- **ORM & Driver:** Drizzle ORM + `@libsql/client`.
- **Image Uploads:** Cloudinary (`cloudinary` npm). Upload API at `POST /api/upload`. Env vars: `CLOUDINARY_CLOUD_NAME`, `CLOUDINARY_API_KEY`, `CLOUDINARY_API_SECRET`. Images stored in `remnus/icons/` folder, auto-resized to 256×256.
- **Auth:** Auth.js v5 (`next-auth@beta`) + `@auth/drizzle-adapter` + `bcryptjs`.
- **Desktop:** Tauri v2 (Rust shell, loads `remnus.com` in system WebView).
- **Mobile:** Capacitor v8 (iOS + Android, loads `remnus.com` via `server.url`).
- **PWA:** `@ducanh2912/next-pwa` (Workbox service worker, `public/manifest.json`). Real icon set in `public/icons/` (192/512 + maskable variants) + `public/apple-touch-icon.png` (180×180), all generated from `logo-square-dark.png`. Install CTA lives on `/download` (see `DownloadView`); the one-shot `beforeinstallprompt` event is captured globally by `PwaInstallCapture` ([src/components/providers/PwaInstallCapture.tsx](src/components/providers/PwaInstallCapture.tsx)) into the [src/lib/pwa/installPrompt.ts](src/lib/pwa/installPrompt.ts) store (also exports `detectInstallPlatform()` — ios/android/desktop). **Install discovery surfaces** (all open the shared `PwaInstallModal` [src/components/features/PwaInstallModal.tsx](src/components/features/PwaInstallModal.tsx) — polished install explainer: benefits row, one-click native prompt when available, else platform-tabbed iOS/Android steps, /download pointer): sidebar "Install app" row (`PwaInstallButton`, web only — hidden in Tauri/standalone), landing nav button (`PwaNavButton`, visible on mobile/tablet only — `lg:hidden`; on desktop widths the "Download" nav link covers it instead), footer Company "Mobile app" link (→ `/download#mobile-install`), and `PwaInstallNudge` (one-time gentle toast for mobile-browser users ~30s into an app session, non-demo, coarse-pointer only, `localStorage['remnus_pwa_nudge_done']`). Manifest `screenshots` (Chrome's rich install UI): `public/screenshots/` — 2 narrow (390×844) + 1 wide (1280×800), captured from the demo workspace. **Middleware bypass gotcha:** `manifest.json`, `sw.js`, and `workbox-*` are excluded in BOTH `proxy.ts`'s matcher and `auth.config.ts`'s `isPublicAsset` — the browser fetches them cookie-less, so without the exclusions the auth check bounced them to `/login` and broke PWA install.

## Architecture & Conventions

### Database Tables

We use the **JSON Column Pattern** (not EAV) for dynamic user-defined properties:

| Table | Purpose |
| ----- | ------- |
| `workspaces` | Workspace list — `icon` (emoji/lucide/https URL), `icon_color`, `billing_owner_id` (nullable FK→user; the paying user whose plan governs this workspace's limits — set on create, follows `transferWorkspaceOwnership`; migration `0027`) |
| `workspace_items` | Sidebar items (pages + databases + dashboards), recursive `parent_id` nesting. `type` is plain TEXT with no DB-level CHECK, so widening the set costs an audit of every `type === 'page' \| 'database'` branch, not a migration — see **Dashboards** for that audit. |
| `recurrence_series` | One repeating calendar rule + the template its occurrences are stamped from (migration `0043`). `rule` is the JSON `RecurrenceRule` from [src/lib/recurrence/rule.ts](src/lib/recurrence/rule.ts) — an RFC 5545 subset, NOT an RRULE string, so MCP clients can read/write it without a parser. `template` = title/properties/icon/content/`dateShape` (time-of-day + duration) every generated card starts from. `parent_series_id` is set by a "this and following" split: that operation ENDS the current series the day before and inserts a NEW row here rather than mutating the old rule, which is what makes already-materialized past cards immutable by construction. `materialized_until` is how far the horizon has been extended. Occurrences link back via `pages.series_id` / `pages.occurrence_date` / `pages.series_detached`, with a UNIQUE index on (series_id, occurrence_date) making top-up idempotent (SQLite treats NULLs as distinct, so non-recurring rows never collide). See **Recurring Calendar Cards** below and `.ai/RECURRENCE_DESIGN.md` |
| `standalone_pages` | Markdown content for page-type items (1:1 with `workspace_items`) |
| `dashboards` | Block spec for dashboard-type items (1:1 with `workspace_items`, migration `0050`). `spec` is the versioned JSON validated by [src/lib/dashboard/schema.ts](src/lib/dashboard/schema.ts) — a list of blocks that each name a data SOURCE (`databaseId` + filters), never a copy of the rows, so a dashboard cannot go stale. Every block has a stable `id`; nothing addresses a block by array position. See **Dashboards**. |
| `databases` | `schema` JSON (columns) + `views` JSON (named view configs). `item_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` — deleting a workspace must go through `deleteWorkspaceData` or its databases and rows are left behind (see **Deleting a whole workspace**). |
| `pages` | Database rows — `properties` JSON + `title`, `content`, `icon`, `icon_color`, `agent_edited_at` + `agent_token_id` (nullable; stamped on MCP writes) |
| `user` | Auth.js accounts — `role` ('user'\|'admin'\|'demo'), `password_hash`, `analytics_consent` (nullable; 'granted'\|'denied'\|null — the user's **effective** server-side analytics-capture permission, persisted by the client `ConsentProvider` so cookie-less server contexts (MCP agent calls, OAuth token endpoint) can pick identified vs anonymous capture; migration `0029`). **First-touch acquisition attribution** copied off the `remnus_first_touch` cookie at signup: `signup_ref` (raw `?ref=` tag e.g. `scoutforge`), `signup_utm_source`/`_medium`/`_campaign`, `signup_referrer` (all nullable; migration `0031`) — pinned as `$set_once` person props so the PostHog signup funnel breaks down by channel. (The admin's own **landing-traffic** card no longer reads these — it pulls all-visitor referrers straight from PostHog via `getTrafficSources`; these columns remain the signup-level attribution record.) **Mailing suppression** (migration `0033`): `email_unsubscribed_at` (user clicked unsubscribe — mutes everything except the transactional welcome) + `email_suppressed` ('bounced'\|'complained', written by the SES→SNS webhook — mutes ALL sends). |
| `account` | OAuth provider links (Google) |
| `session` | Auth.js sessions |
| `verificationToken` | Email verification |
| `workspace_members` | User↔Workspace join — `role` ('owner'\|'member'\|'viewer'), `hidden` (boolean, default false — **per-user** flag that hides the workspace from that member's sidebar behind a "Show hidden" toggle; toggled via `setWorkspaceHidden`; migration `0026`) |
| `workspace_invites` | Email invitations for people without an account yet — `email`, `role`, `token` (bearer secret in the `/invite/[token]` link), `invited_by`, `expires_at` (nullable), `accepted_at` (nullable). Pending invites **reserve a seat**. Migration `0028`. |
| `agent_tokens` | MCP bearer tokens — `token_prefix`, `token_hash`, `scope` ('read'\|'write'), `expires_at` (nullable, null = no expiry), `revoked_at` |
| `uploaded_assets` | One row per Cloudinary upload — `public_id`, `resource_type`, `kind` ('icon'\|'image'\|'file'), `bytes`, `url`, `user_id`, `workspace_id` (nullable). Powers reliable Cloudinary cleanup on delete + storage-usage accounting per user/workspace (future plan limits). |
| `agent_activity` | Audit log for every MCP tool call — since migration `0034`: `token_id` nullable (PAT calls) + `oauth_token_id` (OAuth calls — previously OAuth inserts silently failed the NOT NULL FK and were invisible), `owner_user_id` (denormalized token owner for per-user usage sums), `response_bytes` (serialized response payload size; token estimate ≈ bytes/4, powers the usage meters). Migration `0049` adds `baseline_bytes` (what the same information would have cost the naive way — **NULL means no baseline was computable, never a guess**), `duration_ms` (server-side handling time) and `items_affected` (pages/rows one bulk call touched; NULL = single-target). See **Agent Savings Metrics**. The digest resource read is logged here too, under the tool name `resource:digest`. Indexed on (`workspace_id`, `created_at`) since migration `0053`, which replaced the single-column `workspace_id` index (a strict prefix of it) — time windows over one workspace are a range scan, not a read of up to 400 days of audit rows. |
| `agent_savings_rollup` | Lifetime savings of `agent_activity` rows deleted by the audit hard limit (400 days): `workspace_id`, `owner_user_id` (SET NULL with the user), `saved_bytes`, `saved_calls`, `updated_at`; UNIQUE (workspace_id, owner_user_id). Written only by `pruneAgentActivity`, summed into the savings card. Migration `0051`. See **Billing & Plan Limits** → audit retention. |
| `subscriptions` | Billing — keyed by `owner_user_id` (the **billing owner**, a user; NOT a workspace). `tier` ('free'\|'startup'\|'professional'\|'enterprise'), `status` ('active'\|'past_due'\|'canceled'), `stripe_customer_id`, `stripe_subscription_id`, `current_period_end`, nullable `seat_limit_override`/`agent_limit_override`/`storage_bytes_override` (enterprise/custom). **No row = implicit Free.** Limits derived from `PLAN_LIMITS` ([src/lib/billing/plans.ts](src/lib/billing/plans.ts)). Migration `0027`. |
| `demo_feedback` | In-app feedback from demo visitors — `sentiment` ('positive'\|'neutral'\|'negative'), `comment` (nullable), `user_id` (nullable, **ON DELETE SET NULL** so feedback survives the periodic demo-account cleanup), `created_at`. Written by the `DemoFeedbackPrompt` (fires ~3 min into a demo session); surfaced in the admin dashboard's "Demo Feedback" section. Migration `0032`. |
| `demo_sessions` | **Durable** demo-usage log — one row per "Try the demo" click: `user_id` (nullable, **ON DELETE SET NULL**), `started_at`, `last_seen_at`, `active_seconds`. Exists because demo accounts (and their `user_sessions` rows) are reaped 6h after signup, so neither table can answer "how many demos ran last Tuesday, and for how long" — before this the admin panel could only show demos that were still alive. Opened by `loginAsDemo()`; `/api/activity/ping` then **accumulates** `active_seconds` per heartbeat tick, adding a tick's delta only when it lands inside the 2-min presence window (a tab left open for an hour is not an hour-long demo). One row per demo ACCOUNT, not per activity gap — a visitor who wanders off and comes back is still one demo. Surfaced in the admin dashboard's "Demo Usage" section via `getDemoUsage()`. Migration `0044`. |
| `email_campaigns` | Admin-composed newsletters (see **Mailing System**) — `subject`, `preheader` (nullable), `body_md` (markdown), `status` ('draft'\|'sending'\|'sent'), `recipient_count`/`sent_count`/`failed_count`, `created_by` (SET NULL), `created_at`, `sent_at`. Migration `0033`. |
| `email_log` | One row per sent/failed email — `user_id` (**ON DELETE SET NULL**, null for `contact` — recipient is the team inbox, not a user), `email`, `kind` ('welcome'\|'inactivity'\|'agent_nudge'\|'agent_connected'\|'account_deletion'\|'contact'\|'newsletter'\|'test'), `campaign_id` (nullable FK), `subject`, `status` ('sent'\|'failed'), `error`. Doubles as the **idempotency guard** for the one-shot lifecycle emails + the `/admin/mailing` send history. `kind` is a plain TEXT column (no DB-level CHECK — SQLite/Drizzle enum is TS-only), so widening the set needs no migration. Migration `0033`. |
| `shared_pages` | Public page sharing — `slug` (unique; UUID for regular users, custom path for admins e.g. `docs/mcp`), `page_id`, `workspace_id`, `permission` ('read'\|'write'), `width` ('narrow'\|'wide'\|'full', default 'narrow'), `in_sitemap` (boolean, default false — admin-only, cascades to children), `created_by` (**NOT NULL FK→`user`**, so a writer without a real user id cannot have a row here — see below). Powers `/share/[...slug]` public route. **Share inheritance:** creating a page under a shared parent publishes it too, because the public tree in [share/[...slug]/page.tsx](src/app/[locale]/share/[...slug]/page.tsx) is built ONLY from items that have their own row here — an unshared child is invisible to visitors and its child-block button in the parent dead-ends. Web UI: `autoShareIfParentShared` in [actions/workspace.ts](src/lib/actions/workspace.ts) (stamps the acting user). MCP: `autoShareIfParentShared` / `createPagesInWorkspaceBulk` in [services/workspace.ts](src/lib/services/workspace.ts), which **inherit `created_by` from the parent share** — an MCP caller has only a token id, and writing that (`agentCtx?.tokenId ?? 'system'`, the shape until 2026-09-22) violated the FK, so the best-effort insert failed silently and agent-created sub-pages never appeared in a published tree at all. Inheriting also makes ON DELETE CASCADE coherent: retiring the publisher retires the whole inherited subtree with them. Migrations: `0020` (initial), `0021` (width), `0022` (in_sitemap). |
| `client_auth_tokens` | Short-lived desktop OAuth tokens — `device_id` (PK), `token` (JWT), `expires_at` (5 min TTL). DB-backed; safe for multi-instance deployments. |
| `user_sessions` | Engagement / time-in-app tracking — `user_id`, `started_at`, `last_seen_at`, `duration_seconds`, `platform` (nullable; `'web'`\|`'tauri'`, stamped once at creation from the `remnus_platform` cookie — migration `0035`). Extended by the `/api/activity/ping` heartbeat (`ActivityTracker`); a new row opens after a 2-min inactivity gap. Powers admin engagement stats + desktop-usage stats. |
| `oauth_clients` | RFC 7591 dynamic client registration — `client_id`, `client_name`, `redirect_uris` (JSON), `grant_types`, `response_types`, `token_endpoint_auth_method`. Populated by `POST /api/oauth/register`. |
| `oauth_auth_codes` | Short-lived PKCE auth codes (10 min TTL) — `code`, `client_id`, `user_id`, `workspace_id`, `redirect_uri`, `code_challenge`, `code_challenge_method` (S256), `scope`, `agent_name` + `display_name` (nullable; agent brand-icon id + friendly label picked on the consent screen, copied onto the access token at exchange — migration `0030`), `expires_at`, `used_at`. Single-use; marked used immediately on exchange. |
| `oauth_access_tokens` | OAuth 2.1 issued tokens — `token_prefix` + `token_hash` (bcrypt, `oa_` prefix), `refresh_token_prefix` + `refresh_token_hash` (`or_` prefix, rotated on use), `client_id`, `user_id`, `workspace_id`, `scope`, `agent_name` (nullable; user-set canonical agent id for brand-icon display — migration `0024`), `display_name` (nullable; friendly label from consent screen, falls back to `client_name` in the UI — migration `0030`), `expires_at` (1hr), `revoked_at`. Migration: `0023`. Apply: `npx tsx src/db/apply-0023-oauth.ts`. |
| `deleted_items` | Tombstones for hard-deleted pages/databases/database-rows — `workspace_id` (FK→workspaces CASCADE), `item_id`, `item_type` ('page'\|'database'\|'database_row'), `title` (last known title), `deleted_at`. Powers the MCP `get_changes_since` delta-sync tool (a deletion has no surviving row to read `updatedAt` from). Written best-effort via `recordDeletionTombstone()` ([src/lib/services/workspace.ts](src/lib/services/workspace.ts), exported) from every hard-delete path: `deleteItemFromWorkspace` (MCP), `deleteWorkspaceItemRecursive` ([actions/workspace.ts](src/lib/actions/workspace.ts), web UI), `deletePage` ([actions/page.ts](src/lib/actions/page.ts), web UI row delete). Migration `0035`. |
| `page_links` | Content-derived link graph — one row per `pageLink` (`<a data-page-link>`) or `childBlock` (`<div data-cb-id>`) reference in a page body: `workspace_id` (FK→workspaces CASCADE), `from_id` + `from_type` ('page'\|'database_row'), `to_id` + `to_type` ('page'\|'database'\|'database_row'), `link_kind` ('page_link'\|'child_block'), unique on (from,to,kind). **Target ids stored as-written, unresolved** (a database can appear as `databases.id` from a `/db/<id>` href OR as its workspace item id from a childBlock — `getRelatedPages` resolves both at read time). Re-synced best-effort (delete+insert per `from_id`) by `syncPageLinks()` ([src/lib/services/pageLinks.ts](src/lib/services/pageLinks.ts)) on every content write: web (`updateStandalonePageContent`/`createStandalonePage`/`duplicateWorkspaceItem` in [actions/workspace.ts](src/lib/actions/workspace.ts); `updatePageContent`/`duplicatePage` in [actions/page.ts](src/lib/actions/page.ts)) + MCP (`createPageInWorkspace`/`updatePageById` in [services/workspace.ts](src/lib/services/workspace.ts)) + seed (`createRichWorkspaceData` in [src/lib/seed.ts](src/lib/seed.ts), one explicit call after its batch insert — seed content is written directly to the DB, bypassing the action-layer write paths above, so without this every freshly seeded workspace's "Start Here" → "How This Was Built" child-block link never entered the graph); a delete to the Trash calls `removeOutgoingPageLinks()` (drops only the deleted items' OWN rows — rows other pages hold TO them stay, so a restore needs no repair and the editor dims those links meanwhile); only when the trash copy is gone for good does `releaseTrashedReferences()` (services/snapshots.ts) call `purgeReferencesTo()` (rewrites referencing pages to strip the now-dead childBlock buttons / inline links — **must run first**, it locates them via these rows) then `removePageLinksFor()` (drops rows where the item is source OR target). A delete with no trash copy (recurrence cleanup) still purges at once. Powers the MCP `get_related_pages` tool, the in-app **backlinks panel** **and** the knowledge map's link layer (`services/graph.ts`, same three-form id resolution) (`PageBacklinksPanel` — see below). Migration `0036` + backfill script `src/db/backfill-page-links.ts`. |
| `search_docs` + `search_fts` | Full-text index behind MCP `search_workspace` (migration `0052`). `search_docs` gives every sidebar item (page, database, dashboard) and every database row a stable INTEGER id (`item_id` UNIQUE, `workspace_id`, `kind` 'item'\|'row'); `search_fts` is a **contentless** FTS5 table (`ws`, `title`, `body`; `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`) whose rowid is that id. **Written only by the 11 `search_*` triggers** on `workspace_items`, `standalone_pages`, `pages`, `databases` and `workspaces` — never from app code, so every write path (web, MCP, bulk, seed, restore, import, cascade) is covered. A migration that rebuilds one of those tables drops its triggers: re-run `apply-0052` afterwards; `npm run db:drift` reports missing ones. |
| `knowledge_metadata` | Canonical OKF-aligned metadata for pages, databases, and rows: type/description/tags/sources/lifecycle/freshness, owner, agent provenance, and preserved external assertions/frontmatter. Imported `human:*` is external provenance, never a local review. Migration `0039`. |
| `knowledge_reviews` | Authenticated human reviews bound to the exact current title+body SHA-256. An edit makes the prior review historical. Agents cannot create reviews through MCP. Migration `0039`. |
| `workspace_context_policies` | Per-workspace `manual`/`smart`/`strict` behavior, default 2,000-token budget and trust policy. Strict gates Remnus MCP mutations only. Migration `0039`. |
| `context_runs` | Thirty-minute `prepare_context` handles bound to workspace + PAT/OAuth actor. Stores task/concept/revision hashes and token estimate, not raw task text. Migration `0039`. |
| `account_deletion_tokens` | Short-lived, single-use, DB-backed confirmation token for GDPR self-service account deletion (see **Account Deletion**) — `token` (PK, `crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex')`, 256-bit), `user_id` (FK→user CASCADE), `expires_at` (30 min TTL), `used_at` (nullable — set on redemption, makes the token single-use), `created_at`. Unlike the stateless-HMAC unsubscribe token, this needs a real expiry + a redemption mark since it gates an irreversible action. Migration `0038`. |
| `page_comments` | Time-ordered comment thread attached to a standalone page or database row, separate from its markdown body — `page_id` (no FK; points at either `workspace_items` or `pages`, same pattern as `agent_activity.workspace_id`), `workspace_id`, `body` (max 4,000 chars, enforced at write time — rejected, never truncated), `kind` ('note'\|'closure'), `author_kind` ('human'\|'agent'), `author_user_id` (nullable, SET NULL), `author_label` (denormalized at write time so the byline survives token revocation — same reasoning as `agent_activity`), `token_id`/`oauth_token_id` (nullable, SET NULL). Agent comments (via the MCP `add_comment` tool) are append-only — no update/delete tool exists for them; only a human (comment author or workspace owner) can delete one, via the UI. Index on (`page_id`, `created_at`). Migration `0045`. |
| `page_snapshots` | **Trash + version history**, split by `reason`: `'delete'` is a full-content copy captured immediately before every delete (single or bulk, web UI or MCP) so a human can restore it; `'update'` is a page's prior content captured before a content-changing edit, for its History panel — see **Trash & Version History** below for both. `workspace_id` (FK→workspaces CASCADE), `original_id` (no FK — for `'delete'` the row is gone by read time; for `'update'` it's the still-live page's own id), `item_type` ('page'\|'database'\|'database_row'), `title`, `content` (null for `database`), `properties` (JSON, `database_row` `'delete'` snapshots only — `'update'` snapshots don't version properties), `schema` (JSON, `database` only — lets a restored database shell recreate its columns), `icon`/`icon_color`/`parent_id`/`sort_order` (unused by `'update'` rows), `database_id` (dual-use: for a `database_row` `'delete'` snapshot it's the row's parent database id; for a `database` snapshot it's the database's OWN `databases.id`, distinct from `original_id` which is its workspace-item id — stored so a restore can recreate it under the same id and let its row snapshots reattach), `content_hash` (SHA-256, unused so far — written for parity with `content`), `deleted_by_kind`/`deleted_by_label`/`deleted_by_user_id`/`token_id`/`oauth_token_id` (nullable, SET NULL — reused as-is for `'update'` rows' "who made the edit"; the names read oddly there but a rename migration isn't worth it). Index on (`workspace_id`, `created_at`). `'delete'` rows written by `snapshotBeforeDelete()`, `'update'` rows by `maybeSnapshotContentUpdate()` (both [src/lib/services/snapshots.ts](src/lib/services/snapshots.ts), best-effort — a snapshot failure must never block the write it's recording). Migration `0047`. |

### Auth System

- **Auth System:** Supports Google and GitHub OAuth providers. Email/password login and register pages have been removed.
- **Config split:** `src/auth.config.ts` (edge-safe, no DB, middleware only) + `src/auth.ts` (full, server components/actions).
- **Session access:** Always use `getCurrentUser()` from `src/lib/auth/session.ts` in server actions — **never** `auth()` directly. It is `React.cache`-wrapped to run at most once per request.
- **createdAt gotcha:** DrizzleAdapter stores `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` as text (breaks Drizzle timestamp parsing). All `workspaces` and `workspace_members` inserts pass explicit `createdAt: new Date()`. The `createUser` event also force-updates OAuth users immediately after row creation. **This isn't DrizzleAdapter-specific — it's plain SQLite:** a column `DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` evaluates to a TEXT value (`'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS'`), not a Unix integer, regardless of which driver inserts the row. Any `integer(..., {mode:'timestamp'})` column left to that default reads back as `Invalid Date`. This was found (2026-07) to silently affect **every** `workspace_items`/`standalone_pages`/`databases`/`pages` insert that omitted `createdAt`/`updatedAt` — fixed by stamping both explicitly on every insert in `services/workspace.ts`, `actions/workspace.ts`, `actions/page.ts`, and `seed.ts`. Guard reads of these columns with `isValidDate()`/`safeDate`-style checks (see `getWorkspaceDigest` and `getChangesSince` in `services/workspace.ts`) since older rows predating the fix still carry the bad TEXT value.
- **First-user bootstrap:** The very first non-demo user (any provider) is auto-promoted to `admin` and added as owner to all memberless workspaces.
- **Demo mode:** **per-visitor ephemeral accounts** — each "Try the demo" click provisions a fresh throwaway user (`demo+<uuid>@remnus.com`, role `demo`) with its own freshly seeded workspace, so concurrent demo visitors never share or overwrite each other's data. `loginAsDemo()` ([demo.ts](src/lib/actions/demo.ts)): (1) if the caller already holds a live demo session with a workspace, redirects straight back into it (no new account / no reseed on refresh); (2) requires at least one real (non-demo) user to exist; (3) reaps stale demo accounts inline (`cleanupStaleDemoUsers` — demo users older than `DEMO_USER_TTL_MS` = 6h, bounded to `DEMO_CLEANUP_LIMIT` = 25/login, best-effort; no cron infra). All demo detection elsewhere keys on `role === 'demo'` (never the email), so ephemeral emails are transparent to the banner, analytics-skip, consent, and admin panel. **Cleanup deletes the demo user's workspaces explicitly** (via `workspace_members`) since `workspaces` has no FK to the user; the user delete then cascades sessions/accounts/members. **Usage is logged durably** in `demo_sessions` (migration `0044`, `user_id` SET NULL) precisely because that purge otherwise erases every trace of the visit — that table, not `user`/`user_sessions`, is what the admin panel's demo history reads.
- **Access control:** All actions call `assertWorkspaceAccess(workspaceId)` or `assertDatabaseAccess(databaseId)` before executing. Unauthorized → throws; unauthenticated → `redirect('/login')`.
- **Env vars:** `AUTH_SECRET`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET`, `AUTH_GITHUB_ID`, `AUTH_GITHUB_SECRET`.

### Billing & Plan Limits

**Model:** The subscription belongs to the **billing owner** (a user), not a workspace. A workspace's limits come from its `workspaces.billing_owner_id`'s plan (`subscriptions` row, default Free). The owner holds a **seat pool** and distributes seats by inviting members; limits cover **all** workspaces the owner owns.

- **Limits source of truth:** `src/lib/billing/plans.ts` — `PLAN_LIMITS` (free/startup/professional/enterprise → `seats`, `agents`, `storageBytes`, `auditDays`, `workspaces`). Mirrors the pricing page. `Infinity` = unlimited. **Seats count the owner too** (Free=2 → owner + 1 invitee).
- **Service:** `src/lib/services/billing.ts` (cookie-free, owner-id params) — `getOwnerPlan`, `getPlanForWorkspace`, `resolveBillingOwner`, `countSeats` (distinct users across owner's workspaces), `isUserInOwnerPool`, `countAgents` (PAT+OAuth), `getOwnerStorageBytes` (pooled), `getOwnerUsage`, and `check*` helpers returning a `BillingLimitCode | null` (`seatLimitReached`/`agentLimitReached`/`storageLimitReached`/`workspaceLimitReached`).
- **Enforcement (all "block new, keep existing"; platform admins bypass):** seat → `inviteToWorkspace` ([auth.ts](src/lib/actions/auth.ts)); workspace cap → `createWorkspace` ([workspace.ts](src/lib/actions/workspace.ts)); agent → `mintAgentToken` ([agentToken.ts](src/lib/actions/agentToken.ts)) **and** OAuth issuance ([api/oauth/token](src/app/api/oauth/token/route.ts), `authorization_code` grant only); storage → [api/upload](src/app/api/upload/route.ts) (413). Re-adding a user already in the pool is free.
- **Demo accounts (`role==='demo'`) can browse pricing but never reach Stripe.** `createCheckoutSession`/`createPortalSession`/`cancelSubscription` ([billing.ts](src/lib/actions/billing.ts)) hard-return `Errors.billingDemoBlocked` for demo. Client-side: `BillingModal`/`PlanPickerModal`/`PricingCtaButton` take an `isDemo` prop (threaded from `currentUser.role`/`session.user.role`) and, instead of checkout, render the shared `DemoBillingNotice` ([src/components/features/DemoBillingNotice.tsx](src/components/features/DemoBillingNotice.tsx)) — a friendly "sign in with your own account" panel whose CTA calls `logout()` → `/login`. `BillingModal` also hides the Stripe-portal button for demo. i18n: `Billing.demo{Title,Body,Cta}` + `Errors.billingDemoBlocked` (all 8 locales).
- **Billing-owner consistency:** set on `createWorkspace`, seed (`createSeedWorkspace`), first-user bootstrap (`auth.ts` `createUser`), and moved by `transferWorkspaceOwnership`.
- **Stripe:** `src/lib/stripe.ts` (null when `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` unset), actions in `src/lib/actions/billing.ts` (`createCheckoutSession`, `createPortalSession`, `getMySubscription`, `getMyTier`, `getWorkspaceSeatUsage`), webhook `src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts` (raw body + signature; whitelisted in `auth.config.ts` + bypasses intl in `proxy.ts`).
  - **Self-healing sync:** every `customer.subscription.*` event calls `syncSubscriptionForCustomer(customerId)` ([src/lib/billing/sync.ts](src/lib/billing/sync.ts)) which **recomputes the effective tier from Stripe's live state** (lists the customer's subscriptions, picks the highest active tier, or Free if none) — robust to event ordering AND duplicate subscriptions. Never trust a single event's tier.
  - **No duplicate subscriptions:** Checkout (subscription mode) always creates a NEW sub, so `createCheckoutSession` **switches the existing active subscription's price in place** (`stripe.subscriptions.update` + proration) when one exists, instead of opening a second Checkout — otherwise the user would be double-billed. Only the first purchase goes through Checkout.
- **Seat distribution & invites:** `inviteToWorkspace` ([auth.ts](src/lib/actions/auth.ts)) adds an existing user directly; for an **unregistered email** it creates a `workspace_invites` row and returns a shareable `/invite/[token]` link. `countSeats` counts members **+ pending invites** (de-duped by email) so invites reserve a seat. Accept flow: `/invite/[token]` page ([src/app/[locale]/invite/[token]/page.tsx](src/app/[locale]/invite/[token]/page.tsx), whitelisted) → logged-in users auto-accept (`InviteAcceptClient` → `acceptInvite`); logged-out users get a `pending_invite` cookie + sign-in, then `/app` redirects them back to finish. Invite actions live in `src/lib/actions/invites.ts` (`acceptInvite`/`getWorkspaceInvites`/`revokeWorkspaceInvite`/`getInviteByToken`). Invited people get access **only to the invited workspace(s)** (access scope stays per-workspace via `workspace_members`).
- **Central people management:** `getPoolMembers()` / `removeUserFromPool(userId)` ([billing.ts](src/lib/actions/billing.ts)) power `PoolPeopleSection` inside `BillingModal` — lists everyone across the owner's workspaces + pending invites; **removing someone deletes them from ALL the owner's workspaces** (frees the seat). `MembersTab` also lists/​revokes a workspace's pending invites and surfaces the copyable invite link.
- **UI:** global `BillingModal` (sidebar "Plan & Billing" button, with a current-tier badge fed by `getMyTier()`) shows plan + usage meters + upgrade (Checkout) / manage (Portal); `WorkspaceSettings` → Billing tab is a thin redirect to it; `MembersTab` shows a seat meter and disables invite at the cap; pricing CTAs (`PricingCtaButton`) start Checkout for logged-in users on paid tiers. `BillingSuccessModal` (mounted in `[locale]/layout.tsx`) fires on `?billing=success` (forwarded through the `/app` redirect) — celebratory modal listing the unlocked plan limits; polls `getMySubscription` a few times to outrun the webhook.
- **Env vars:** `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `STRIPE_PRICE_STARTUP`, `STRIPE_PRICE_PRO`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`.
- **Audit retention (`auditDays`, enforced since 2026-09-23)** — [src/lib/services/auditRetention.ts](src/lib/services/auditRetention.ts), two layers:
  - **Visibility window, at read time.** `auditVisibleSince(workspaceId)` = now − the workspace billing owner's `auditDays`. Applied on every surface that shows the audit log to a customer: `queryAuditLog` (`query_audit_log`), the `remnus://audit-log/recent` resource, `getUserAgentActivity` (AI Agents list; per-workspace cutoffs via `activityVisibleIn`, since one user's workspaces can sit on different plans) and the dashboard `activity` block. Upgrading shows older history immediately; a downgrade hides it. Always compare through `activityAtOrAfter` (legacy CURRENT_TIMESTAMP TEXT rows).
  - **Hard limit, nightly.** `pruneAgentActivity` (called from the daily `/api/cron/recurrence` maintenance run, next to the snapshot purge — no extra cron) deletes rows older than `AUDIT_HARD_RETENTION_DAYS` = 400, after upserting their savings into `agent_savings_rollup` in the **same** libsql batch (migration 0051).
  - **Deliberately NOT windowed:** the savings card's 7/30-day figures, `getMyAgentUsage`, onboarding's "an agent has connected" check and the admin funnels. Physically deleting at 7 days would have broken all of them for every Free workspace — which is why the window is a view, not a delete.

### Mailing System (AWS SES)

Lifecycle emails + admin-composed newsletters, sent through **Amazon SES** (nodemailer 7 → SESv2 `SendEmailCommand` from `@aws-sdk/client-sesv2`). English-only by design (recipient locale isn't stored); all copy lives in `src/lib/email/templates.ts`, NOT the i18n files. Modeled on ScoutForge's mailer, restyled to the dark Remnus palette.

- **Core (`src/lib/email/`):** `theme.ts` (brand hexes mirrored from globals.css — email clients need inline styles; `SITE_URL` = `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` or `https://www.remnus.com`), `layout.ts` (`renderEmailLayout` — 600px dark table layout + `button`/`card`/`step`/`para` helpers), `templates.ts` (4 lifecycle templates + `renderNewsletterHtml` — `marked` renders the campaign markdown, then per-tag inline styles are injected), `send.ts` (lazy SES transport; `sendEmail` logs every send into `email_log` and adds `List-Unsubscribe` + RFC 8058 one-click headers when `unsubUserId` is set; `canReceiveEmail` gating; HMAC unsub tokens via `UNSUB_SECRET`; **no-op with a console warning when `MAIL_FROM_EMAIL`/`SES_REGION` are unset** — safe for dev/forks), `lifecycle.ts` (no-throw `sendWelcomeEmailTo` / `maybeSendAgentConnectedEmail`), `snsValidator.ts` (SNS signature verification, ported from ScoutForge).
- **7 email kinds:** `welcome` (instant — `createUser` event in [src/auth.ts](src/auth.ts), transactional so it ignores unsubscribe) · `agent_connected` (instant, once ever — fired from `mintAgentToken` + the OAuth `authorization_code` grant, right after the `mcp_token_created` captures) · `agent_nudge` (cron — signed up 24h–7d ago, zero PAT/OAuth tokens, once ever) · `inactivity` (cron — last activity from `user_sessions` 4–30 days old, once per inactivity episode: resends only if the user came back after the previous one) · `account_deletion` (instant, on request — GDPR delete-account confirm link, see **Account Deletion**; transactional like `welcome`, ignores unsubscribe) · `contact` (instant, on submit — **outbound-to-team**, not to a user; see **Contact Form** below) · `newsletter` (manual, admin-composed markdown).
- **Contact Form:** `/contact` ([src/components/marketing/ContactForm.tsx](src/components/marketing/ContactForm.tsx), client) posts to `submitContactForm()` ([src/lib/actions/contact.ts](src/lib/actions/contact.ts)).
