Unlike every other email kind, the recipient is the fixed team inbox `info@remnus.com` (hardcoded `CONTACT_INBOX`, `userId: null` in the `email_log` row) — `sendEmail()`'s new optional `replyTo` sets the reply-to to the *visitor's* address so a human can just hit reply; template is `contactFormEmail()` in `templates.ts`. **Spam protection** (no external captcha dependency): a honeypot field (`company`, visually hidden + `aria-hidden`, non-empty ⇒ bot), a minimum fill-time check (`startedAt` timestamp captured on mount, rejects submits faster than 2.5s), and a per-IP rate limit (5/hour, in-memory `Map` — same token-bucket pattern as the MCP route's rate limiter, see the note there about resets on cold start). Both spam signals **fail soft** (report success without sending) so a bot never learns what tripped it; the rate limit fails loud (visible error) since a real over-eager human should see it. Errors: `Errors.contactForm{MissingFields,InvalidEmail,RateLimited,SendFailed}`.
- **Cron:** `vercel.json` schedules `GET /api/cron/emails` daily at 09:00 UTC. The route requires `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}` (Vercel attaches it automatically when the env var exists), caps 50 sends per job per run, throttles to `MAIL_SEND_RATE`/sec (default 10).
- **Unsubscribe:** footer link → `/unsubscribe?u=<userId>&t=<hmac>` ([src/app/[locale]/unsubscribe/page.tsx](src/app/[locale]/unsubscribe/page.tsx)) — **confirm-button page, mutation NOT on GET** (mail scanners prefetch links); the `List-Unsubscribe` header points at `POST /api/unsubscribe` (RFC 8058 one-click; GET redirects to the page). Sets `user.email_unsubscribed_at`.
- **Bounce/complaint suppression:** `POST /api/webhooks/ses` — SES configuration set → SNS topic → HTTPS subscription to this route. Signature-verified; auto-confirms `SubscriptionConfirmation`; hard `Bounce`/`Complaint` set `user.email_suppressed` (protects the shared SES account reputation). Open/click analytics = future work.
- **Newsletter flow:** drafts CRUD via [src/lib/actions/mailing.ts](src/lib/actions/mailing.ts) (all admin-gated): `getMailingOverview` / `saveCampaign` / `deleteCampaign` (draft-only) / `previewCampaignHtml` (iframe srcdoc) / `sendTestCampaign` (`[TEST]` to admin). Send-to-all = `POST /api/admin/mailing/send-campaign` (API route for `maxDuration = 300`; only `status='draft'` campaigns send — double-send protection; recipients = non-demo users with email, not unsubscribed/suppressed).
- **Admin UI:** `/admin/mailing` ([(app)/admin/mailing/page.tsx](src/app/[locale]/(app)/admin/mailing/page.tsx)) — stat cards (sent/failed 30d, unsubscribed, suppressed, newsletter audience + per-kind chips), newsletter composer (subject/preheader/markdown, live preview, test send, two-step confirmed send-to-all) and the recent-50 send log. Components in `src/components/features/admin/mailing/` (`MailingDashboard`, `CampaignManager`, `EmailLogTable`). Linked from the `/admin` header (Mail button). i18n: `Mailing` namespace.
- **Middleware whitelist:** `/unsubscribe`, `/api/unsubscribe`, `/api/cron`, `/api/webhooks/ses` in `auth.config.ts` (`isMailingPublic`).
- **Template test:** `npx tsx scripts/test-emails.ts` — sends all 5 templates to `MAIL_TEST_EMAIL`, or (mail env unset) dumps HTML previews to `email-previews/` (git-ignored).
- **Env vars (names only):** `SES_REGION`, `SES_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `SES_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `MAIL_FROM_EMAIL`, `MAIL_FROM_NAME`, `MAIL_REPLY_TO`, `MAIL_TEST_EMAIL`, `SES_CONFIGURATION_SET`, `UNSUB_SECRET`, `CRON_SECRET`, `MAIL_SEND_RATE` (optional).

### Account Deletion (GDPR)

Self-service "right to erasure" — email-confirmed two-step flow, not immediate-on-click, since the action is destructive/irreversible and a UI click alone (e.g. from a hijacked/left-open session) isn't sufficient proof of intent.

- **Step 1 — request:** `UserSettingsModal` → Account tab → Danger Zone (`DeleteAccountSection`) → type `DELETE` to confirm → `requestAccountDeletion()` ([src/lib/actions/account.ts](src/lib/actions/account.ts)). Checks preconditions (see below), mints a 256-bit single-use `account_deletion_tokens` row (30 min TTL, any previous unused token for the user is deleted first), emails a confirm link via `accountDeletionConfirmEmail()` ([src/lib/email/templates.ts](src/lib/email/templates.ts)) as kind `account_deletion`. **Nothing is deleted at this point.** The modal then shows a "check your email" success state instead of navigating away.
- **Step 2 — confirm:** the emailed link points at `/account-delete/confirm?token=…` ([src/app/[locale]/account-delete/confirm/page.tsx](src/app/[locale]/account-delete/confirm/page.tsx)) — a **confirm-button page, not a GET-triggers-delete link** (same reasoning as `/unsubscribe`: mail scanners prefetch links). This route is **not** whitelisted in `auth.config.ts`, so it's protected by the default middleware behavior — an unauthenticated visit bounces to `/login?callbackUrl=…` and NextAuth's own redirect callback sends the browser back here after sign-in. Confirming requires the **same live session** that made the request (the emailed link's token alone is not sufficient — this is the two-factor property: something you clicked + something you're still logged into). The page validates the token server-side (`isTokenLive()` — belongs to the current session's user, unused, unexpired) before rendering the real "Permanently delete my account" button; an invalid/expired/foreign token instead shows a "link invalid or expired" state. Submitting calls `confirmAccountDeletion(token)`, which re-validates everything, marks the token used, deletes, and `signOut({redirectTo:'/'})`s — on error it redirects back to the same page with `?error=1`.
- **Preconditions (checked on both steps):** blocked for `role==='demo'` (already ephemeral/auto-reaped) and for anyone who's the `billing_owner_id` of a workspace with other members or pending invites (must transfer ownership or remove them first).
- **What actually gets deleted** (`performAccountDeletion()`, private to `account.ts`): best-effort Stripe subscription cancel, best-effort Cloudinary purge of every asset in `uploaded_assets`, nulls `agent_tokens.created_by` (that FK has no `onDelete` action, unlike almost every other user FK — would otherwise block the user delete for tokens left in a shared workspace), deletes every workspace where the user is the **sole** member through `deleteWorkspaceData` (see **Deleting a whole workspace** below — until 2026-09-24 a bare delete here left the workspace's databases, rows and comments in the database, so erasure was incomplete), then deletes the `user` row (cascades sessions/accounts/oauth/subscriptions/user_sessions/account_deletion_tokens; SET-NULLs `agent_activity`/`demo_feedback`/`email_log`). Workspaces shared with teammates are left intact — the user is just removed from them.
- **`account_deletion_tokens` table** (migration `0038`) — DB-backed rather than the stateless-HMAC scheme `/unsubscribe` uses, because a destructive action needs a real expiry and a way to mark itself consumed; an unsub token is fine to be forever-valid, a delete-my-account token is not.
- i18n: `Errors.accountDelete{DemoBlocked,HasTeammates,NoEmail,EmailUnavailable,EmailFailed,InvalidToken,TokenExpired}` + `UserSettings.dangerZoneTitle`/`deleteAccount*` (all 8 locales).

### Deleting a whole workspace

**Always `deleteWorkspaceData(workspaceId)`** ([src/lib/services/workspaceDeletion.ts](src/lib/services/workspaceDeletion.ts)) — never a bare `db.delete(workspaces)`. The five callers: `deleteWorkspace` + `adminDeleteWorkspace` ([actions/workspace.ts](src/lib/actions/workspace.ts)), `performAccountDeletion` ([actions/account.ts](src/lib/actions/account.ts)), the demo reaper `purgeDemoUser` ([actions/demo.ts](src/lib/actions/demo.ts)), and the OKF import rollback ([api/import/okf](src/app/api/import/okf/route.ts)).

Why a service: most of a workspace goes by `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `workspace_id`, but **`databases.item_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`**, so a bare workspace delete left every database behind with a NULL item — with all its rows (`pages`: titles, bodies, properties) and recurrence series — and **`page_comments.workspace_id` has no FK**, so comments stayed too. Unreachable from the app, but real user content kept after a delete and after a GDPR account deletion (found 2026-09-24: a local DB held 14 such databases, 120 rows, ~84k characters of bodies). The service deletes series → rows → databases → comments → workspace in ONE `db.batch` (all-or-nothing), children explicitly so it does not depend on the connection's `foreign_keys` pragma. Deliberately untouched: `agent_activity` / `agent_savings_rollup` (audit, no FK by design) and `uploaded_assets` (`workspace_id` SET NULL — the Cloudinary files of a deleted workspace stay; only account deletion destroys a user's uploads; separate decision). Item-level deletes (a page or database from the sidebar/MCP) were never affected — they delete the database before the item.
- Regression: `DATABASE_URL="file:local.db" npm run test:workspace-deletion` — seeds two identical workspaces, shows a bare delete still leaves database/rows/series/comments, and that the service leaves nothing in 11 tables.
- Existing leftovers: [src/db/cleanup-orphaned-workspace-data.ts](src/db/cleanup-orphaned-workspace-data.ts) — dry run by default (counts only, no content printed), `--apply` deletes in one batch, 10-minute grace, prints the target host. Applied to local on 2026-09-24; **Turso production is a deploy-day step** (roadmap deploy list). `npm run db:drift` reports any leftovers as a note.

### Performance Rules

- **No query waterfalls:** Fetch independent sources with `Promise.all`.
- **Revalidation:** Call `revalidatePath('/')` only for sidebar-structural mutations (create/delete items, workspace rename/delete). Content saves (`updatePageContent`, `updatePageProperties`) must NOT call revalidatePath.
- **Optimistic UI:** `WorkspaceSidebar` applies mutations to local state immediately; server revalidation is background.
- **SQLite PRAGMAs:** WAL mode, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`, `cache_size=-20000`, `temp_store=MEMORY` applied at startup in `src/db/index.ts`.
- **Bulk write path (`createPagesInWorkspaceBulk`, `services/workspace.ts`):** Turso is remote, so the cost of a write tool is its **statement count**, not its CPU. `bulk_create_pages` used to loop `createPageInWorkspace`, costing ~7 statements per record (~750 sequential round-trips for 100 records — the "calibration takes minutes" complaint). It is now **5 round-trips regardless of batch size**: one batched read (parents ∥ databases-with-schema ∥ parent share rows), one grouped `max(sort_order)` per target database, one `db.batch` of multi-row inserts, then the best-effort side effects. Invariants a future change must keep:
  - **Ids are generated up front** (`crypto.randomUUID`). That is the whole reason inserts can be batched: `ref`/`parentRef` resolves in memory instead of needing the previous insert's id. Input order is already topological — `parentRef` only resolves **backwards**, to a **page** created earlier in the same call.
  - **Validate in memory, then write once.** Everything that can fail per entry (icon, duplicate `ref`, unknown `parentRef`/`parentId`/`databaseId`, and the cascade onto children of a failed entry) is decided before the batch runs, so the "valid entries land, invalid ones report why" contract survives without partial-write repair.
  - **Never re-read a whole database to find the next `sortOrder`.** One `select database_id, max(sort_order) … group by database_id`, then walk the increments in memory for rows sharing a database.
  - **`createdAt`/`updatedAt` written explicitly as `Date`s** — see the `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` gotcha under **Migration Notes**.
  - **Side effects stay OUT of the content batch.** `syncPageLinksBulk` (`services/pageLinks.ts`, one delete + chunked multi-row insert in a single batch), `recordGeneratedKnowledgeBulk` (`services/knowledge.ts`, one upsert; carries each entry's optional `knowledge` subset — `conceptType`/`tags`/`sources` — and on conflict only moves provenance, never curated metadata) and the inherited `shared_pages` rows all run afterwards under `Promise.allSettled` — a failing link sync or share must never roll back the pages it describes. Share inheritance itself is resolved in memory and propagates to grandchildren, which the unawaited per-page version could not do reliably.
  - **Chunk multi-row inserts** with `chunkRows(rows, columnsPerRow)` (`services/sqlChunk.ts`, 900 bind params). Several chunks still ship inside one batch, so round-trips stay flat as the cap grows.
  - **`bulk_delete_pages` was deliberately left on its per-item path.** Its ordering is load-bearing: per item snapshot → delete → tombstone, then ONE `removeOutgoingPageLinks` over every dead id of the whole batch, after every trash copy exists (no other page is rewritten at delete time — links to trashed items are purged only when the trash copy is gone for good). It recurses over descendants and already runs concurrently via `Promise.allSettled` — so the wall-clock win is small and the blast radius of a mistake is deleted user content. See **Trash & Version History** → links survive a delete + restore.
- **Context & search read path (P10, 2026-09-23)** — how agents *find* things. Rules a change here must keep:
  - **One folding rule on both sides of every match.** `foldText()` ([services/textFold.ts](src/lib/services/textFold.ts)): NFKD, strip `\p{M}`, locale-free lowercase, `ı`→`i`. Never `toLocaleLowerCase('tr-TR')` for matching — it turned English "Invites"/"API" into "ınvites"/"apı", so a lowercase task missed every capital-I title.
  - **`prepare_context` is lexical; the agent expands.** `keywords` (≤ 24 × 60 chars) are synonyms and the workspace's own language; ranked with the task terms at `KEYWORD_WEIGHT = 0.5` (measured: distractor-keyword MRR 0.92 at 0.3–0.6, 0.75 at 1.0). The same one-line rule lives in `renderInstructions` (`handler.ts`), `cli/templates/agents-section.md`, `skills/remnus/SKILL.md` and `docs/mcp/context-first.md` — keep them saying the same thing, and re-run `npm run bench:mcp-budget` (P10 added ~224 B ≈ 56 tok per session).
  - **Corpus = one round-trip, index cached per process.** `listKnowledgeCorpus` ships its 4 reads in one `db.batch` (the OKF snapshot uses it too; its output is unchanged). `contextPack.ts` builds an inverted index (per-field term counts + a sorted vocabulary, so a query scores only documents containing one of its terms) and keeps it in a ~24 MB LRU keyed by `getKnowledgeCorpusVersion` (knowledge.ts): change version + item/row counts (recurrence pruning writes no tombstone) + knowledge metadata/review/revocation maxima, one batched round-trip. It returns **null inside the hot second** (same 2 s margin as `settledCursor`) and a null key is never cached. Freshness (`stale`) is recomputed on every call, never taken from the cached copy. Serverless: speed is best-effort, correctness comes only from the key. Measured on 3,000 items / 3.4M chars locally: warm call ~600 ms → ~20 ms; cold ~400 ms (index build).
  - **`search_workspace` = ranked FTS5, substring scan as the fallback.** `searchWorkspaceIndexed` asks the migration-`0052` index (`searchMatchExpression` in [services/searchIndex.ts](src/lib/services/searchIndex.ts): every word a quoted prefix phrase, `ws : "w<id>"` filter, bm25 weights ws 0 / title 10 / body 1, rank in a subquery **then** join the bodies — joining first read 2× the rows on a common term). The scan (`searchWorkspaceScan`: `col LIKE :skeleton ESCAPE '\' AND col GLOB :classes` from `foldingSearchPatterns`, fold groups = tr/es/fr/de accents) answers when the query is < 3 letters, is in a script without spaces (CJK/Thai — one unicode61 token per run), the index is missing (warns once), or the index found nothing (a mid-word fragment). Query side never strips combining marks: Hindi vowel signs are unicode61 separators and must reach the tokenizer as typed. Measured on a Turso dev DB (3,000 docs): rows read per search 5.4–7k → 1.0–1.6k for specific terms (≈1k of that is the breadcrumb tree read), equal for a term in half the docs; +2 rows written per edit; +28% storage (a stored-content FTS table was +120%, and snippets come from the source rows anyway). Latency is network-bound either way.
  - **No embeddings (P11, 2026-09-24, Hakan's decision).** Retrieval stays lexical on purpose: semantic search would mean sending every page to a third-party embedding model (a new sub-processor, a backfill of all content) for a gap `keywords` already closes — and Remnus's own pitch is "ordinary pages, not a vector store". Workspace content leaves Remnus only through the agent the user connected. Do not add an embedding/vector path without a new decision; the design and provider comparison that was prepared is in the roadmap's P11 completion note.
  - **Prefix rule, both directions, with a stem floor (2026-09-25).** A document token matches a query term when either is a prefix of the other ("limitlerini" → "limit"); a document token SHORTER than the term counts only from `MIN_STEM_LENGTH` = 4 letters, or 3 when it is a one-letter stem ("row" → "rows"). Before, any 3-letter word stood in for a long term — "per" matched "performance"/"performans", "ara" matched "araştır" — ranking unrelated pages and filling their `selectionReason.matchedTerms` with words they never contain (live report). `bench:context` → `checkShortPrefixNoise` asserts both sides: the noise stays out, "hatasını" still reaches "hata". Shipped docs/ eval unchanged (7/12, 0.583 / 0.917).
  - **Vocabulary-miss warning (P11).** When a strict majority of the task's terms match no document (same prefix rule as ranking) and no keyword matched, `prepareContextPack` adds a warning naming up to 8 of them and telling the agent to call again with `keywords`; it replaces the generic "No workspace concepts matched" line. Strict majority on purpose — one unknown word in a matching task must not cost a second call. Measured on the repo's own `docs/` (46 pages) as a corpus: 0/24 false alarms on English tasks of 3–13 terms, 5/7 Turkish/Spanish tasks caught when the corpus is English-only, 1/7 when it contains one Turkish page (that page genuinely matches — mixed-language workspaces need keywords up front). A second trigger, "top concept covers < half the task terms", was measured and rejected: English tasks bottomed at 57% coverage, too close to fire safely. `prepareContextPackWithStats` returns numbers only (`keywordCount`, `taskTerms`, `missingTaskTerms`, `vocabularyMiss`); the MCP tool adds `conceptCount` and sends them on its `agent_call` event — see **Activation Funnel Analytics**.
  - **FTS as `prepare_context`'s first stage: decided against for now (P10-D.5, closed in P11).** Warm calls are ~20 ms from the cached index; the cold cost (~400 ms build + ~3.4 MB read at 3,000 items) hits only a cold instance of a large workspace. `search_fts` indexes title/body but not knowledge description/tags (ranked at weight 2.5 here), and unicode61 prefix queries do not match the in-process rule in both directions ("limitlerini" → "limit"), so a first stage would change `mustHit` ranking and move the real path out of reach of the in-memory bench. Revisit when post-deploy `agent_activity.duration_ms` for `prepare_context` on large workspaces says so (roadmap deploy list). The final order is still built in one place (`rankCorpus` → trust filter → sort in `prepareContextPackWithStats`) — the hook for any future reranker.
  - **Related refs = the top 3 concepts' neighbourhoods, room reserved first (P13, 2026-09-24).** Before P13 `related` came from the top concept only and was appended after the bodies had filled the budget: at the default 2,000 tokens a pack carried 0.9 refs on average. Now `relatedCandidates` (in `contextPack.ts`) reads `getRelatedPages` for the top `RELATED_SEEDS = 3` ranked concepts in parallel, votes each neighbour by the scores of the concepts pointing at it (links before parent before children on a tie), keeps `MAX_RELATED = 6`, and reserves their exact JSON size — at most `RELATED_BUDGET_SHARE = 8 %` of the budget — **before** sizing the bodies. Measured on the repo's own `docs/` (46 pages, 148 links, 12 tasks whose relevance sets were written before any run; `context-graph-expansion.ts`, part of `bench:context`): relevant pages reachable from the pack (body or ref) 0.69 → **0.92** (re-run after this change's own docs edits, 150 links: 0.65 → 0.92), ~140 ref tokens, pack ≈ 1,984 of 2,000 tokens, no concept dropped, top-1 unchanged (7/12); the control without a graph (the next six lexical hits as refs) reached 0.78. Rejected: a personalized PageRank **in the body ranking** (+0.11 body recall, but an index page — `blog/README` — took a body slot in 6 of 12 tasks; a calibration's overview page would do the same), and PageRank for the refs (0.89, no better than the vote, heavier). A failed neighbourhood read still only adds the "could not be loaded" warning.
  - Regression: `npm run bench:context` asserts the `mustHit` group (English, capital I, Turkish letters, TR task + keywords, synonym + keywords) with **no** vocabulary-miss warning on any of them, and the cache key behaviour. The `tracked` group (TR task / synonym **without** keywords) reports its first-call rank (0/4, lexical by design) and asserts the recovery: the warning fires and a retry with the listed `retryKeywords` ranks the concept first (4/4). Since P13 it also runs the docs/ graph report and asserts that the shipped refs beat the same number of lexical refs and stay inside the budget.
- **Idle Polling Convention:** Automatic background content refreshing via `useWorkspaceEvents()` is configured to only run when the user is inactive (idle) for at least 10 seconds. Active user interactions (keyboard, mouse, touches, scrolling) pause automatic polling to avoid jarring layout/focus resets during data entry.
- **Manual Refresh:** Pages (Databases and Standalone Pages) have a dedicated, localized refresh/sync button in the upper header bar next to the width settings to allow on-demand synchronization when the user is actively working.

### Activation Funnel Analytics

**Goal:** measure the activation funnel **Landing → signup → first MCP token → first agent call**, broken down by channel. Targets: signup→token > 40%, token→first-call > 50%.

- **Events** (all server-side via [src/lib/analytics/server.ts](src/lib/analytics/server.ts) — the middle steps have no browser): `$pageview` on `/` (client, already existed) → `signup` ([auth.ts](src/auth.ts) `createUser` event) → `mcp_token_created` (PAT: `mintAgentToken` [agentToken.ts](src/lib/actions/agentToken.ts); OAuth: `/api/oauth/token` **authorization_code grant only** — refresh is rotation, not a new agent) → `agent_call` ([api/mcp/context.ts](src/app/api/mcp/context.ts) `logActivity`, **successful calls only**, fire-and-forget; resolves the token owner via `TokenContext.ownerUserId` set in [api/mcp/route.ts](src/app/api/mcp/route.ts)).
- **Consent (GDPR):** capture respects consent but **never drops a funnel count**. `allowed` ⇒ identified on `userId`; `!allowed` (EU/EEA/UK not-accepted) ⇒ **anonymous** event (random distinctId, PII stripped, `$process_person_profile:false`) — aggregate counts survive, nothing personal stored. **admin + demo are never captured** (mirrors `PostHogIdentify`). Request scopes resolve consent from cookie+geo; cookie-less scopes (MCP/OAuth) read `user.analytics_consent`, which the client `ConsentProvider` persists via `setAnalyticsConsent`.
- **Agent-add funnel instrumentation (the middle, between `signup` and `mcp_token_created`):** the coarse funnel hides *where/why* a user fails to connect an agent, so the OAuth/PAT add flow is instrumented step-by-step:
  - **In-app (client, posthog-js, consent auto-respected):** `connect_flow_opened` → `connect_editor_selected` → `connect_completed` — all carry a `source` prop (`onboarding`|`agents_modal`|…) threaded `OnboardingGuide`/`AgentsModal` → `ConnectModal` → `ConnectFlow`. The gap `connect_editor_selected` → `oauth_authorize_viewed` = config friction (editor never kicked off OAuth); `connect_completed` vs `agent_call` = "thinks connected but no real call".
  - **OAuth (server, [api/oauth](src/app/api/oauth/)):** `oauth_authorize_viewed` (consent screen reached — [authorize/page.tsx](src/app/[locale]/oauth/authorize/page.tsx)) → `oauth_consent_result` (`{result: approved|denied}`, same file's approve/deny actions) → on exchange: success keeps `mcp_token_created`, **failure emits `oauth_token_exchange_failed` with `reason`** ([api/oauth/token](src/app/api/oauth/token/route.ts): `missing_params`/`code_not_found`/`code_already_used`/`code_expired`/`*_mismatch`/`pkce_invalid`/**`agent_limit_reached`**). Pre-user failures are anonymous (`captureAnonymous`); user-known ones attribute via `captureForUser`.
  - **PAT (server):** `mcp_token_mint_blocked` (`{reason: <BillingLimitCode>, type:'pat'}`) when `mintAgentToken`'s `checkCanAddAgent` blocks — the PAT mirror of `agent_limit_reached`.
  - **Helpers** added to [analytics/server.ts](src/lib/analytics/server.ts): `captureForUser(event, userId, props)` (resolves consent+role from the user record) and `captureAnonymous(event, props)` (no user context); `captureServer` now accepts `userId: string | null` (null ⇒ forced anonymous). `FunnelEvent` union extended with the 4 new server events. **No new env, no migration, no new i18n.**
- **Tool counters on `agent_call` (P11):** `logActivity`'s `metrics.analytics` adds content-free numbers to the event (`captureAgentCall(…, extra)`). Only `prepare_context` uses it: `keywordCount`, `taskTerms`, `missingTaskTerms`, `vocabularyMiss`, `conceptCount` — never task text, terms, titles or ids. The question it answers: how often agents skip `keywords` and hit the vocabulary-miss warning, i.e. whether the "no embeddings" decision costs real users anything (PostHog: `agent_call` where `tool = prepare_context`, breakdown by `vocabularyMiss` / `keywordCount > 0`).
- **Channel attribution:** `AttributionCapture` writes a first-touch `remnus_first_touch` cookie (UTM + referrer); `signup` reads it and pins `initial_utm_*`/`initial_referrer` as `$set_once` person props.
- **Dashboard:** build the PostHog funnel insight (`$pageview /` → `signup` → `mcp_token_created` → `agent_call`) with a breakdown on `initial_utm_source`/`initial_referrer` (PostHog-side config, not code). **Agent-add drill-down funnel:** `connect_flow_opened` → `connect_editor_selected` → `oauth_authorize_viewed` → `oauth_consent_result(approved)` → `mcp_token_created` → `agent_call`, with `oauth_token_exchange_failed`/`mcp_token_mint_blocked`/`oauth_consent_result(denied)` broken down by `reason`/`result` to pinpoint the drop cause (PostHog-side config). Both funnels can be queried or built through the `posthog` MCP server — see **Agent MCP Servers**.
- **Env:** reuses `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST` server-side (no new env).

### Crash Reporting & Source Maps

Render crashes are caught by the error boundaries (`global-error.tsx` + `[locale]/error.tsx`) and forwarded to PostHog **Error Tracking** as `$exception` events via `src/lib/reportClientError.ts` (`posthog.captureException` + `digest`/`path`/`boundary` props). See the **Root app files** entry for the boundaries. Live issues are readable through the `posthog` MCP server (`query-error-tracking-issues-list` / `-issue-events`) — see **Agent MCP Servers**.

- **Source-map upload (build-time):** [next.config.ts](next.config.ts) wraps the prod config with `withPostHogConfig` (`@posthog/nextjs-config`, devDependency). It generates hidden browser source maps during `next build`, uploads them to PostHog so minified production stacks (e.g. React #310 → `aP`/`L`) resolve to real component/file/line, then **deletes them (`deleteAfterUpload: true`) so they never ship to users**. EU cloud → upload `host: 'https://eu.posthog.com'` (the API host, NOT the `eu.i.posthog.com` INGEST host in `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST`).
- **Gating:** applied only when `NODE_ENV === 'production'`, both build creds are set, **and** either Vercel sets `VERCEL=1` or a non-Vercel release build explicitly sets `POSTHOG_SOURCEMAPS_UPLOAD=true`. Credentials alone never trigger a local upload: Next loads `.env` during `next build`, so the old credential-only gate unexpectedly wrote local source maps into the live PostHog project.
- **Build env (NOT `NEXT_PUBLIC`):** `POSTHOG_API_KEY` (a PostHog **Personal API key** with error-tracking/source-map write scope) + `POSTHOG_PROJECT_ID` (numeric project id). Vercel needs only those two because `VERCEL=1` is automatic. Non-Vercel release pipelines must also opt in with `POSTHOG_SOURCEMAPS_UPLOAD=true`. Without the gate/credentials the build still succeeds, but stacks remain minified.

### Migration Notes

- New migration `when` values must be **greater than** all existing values. `_journal.json` ends at `0016` (`1780700000000`); the documented manual sequence continues through `0039`. **Current next migration threshold: `when` > `1782100000000`** — recheck the end of this list before creating one.
- `0018_user_sessions`, `0019_uploaded_assets` (and `0017`) are NOT in `_journal.json` — applied manually via direct DDL due to the libsql `batch()` caveat below. Apply `user_sessions` with `npx tsx src/db/apply-0018-user-sessions.ts`, `uploaded_assets` with `npx tsx src/db/apply-0019-uploaded-assets.ts` (both idempotent).
- `0020_shared_pages`, `0021_shared_pages_width`, `0022_shared_pages_sitemap` — also manually applied. Scripts: `src/db/apply-002{0,1,2}-*.ts`. Apply to both local and Turso.
- `0023_oauth` — OAuth 2.1 tables (`oauth_clients`, `oauth_auth_codes`, `oauth_access_tokens`). Script: `src/db/apply-0023-oauth.ts`. Applied manually (same idempotent pattern).
- `0024_oauth_agent_name` — adds nullable `agent_name` to `oauth_access_tokens` (user-set canonical agent id for brand-icon display). Idempotent (PRAGMA column check). Script: `src/db/apply-0024-oauth-agent-name.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0025_workspace_hidden` — **superseded by 0026.** Added `hidden` to `workspaces` (workspace-global). Wrong scope: one member hiding affected all members. Column dropped by 0026.
- `0026_member_hidden` — moves `hidden` to `workspace_members` (**per-user**: a member hides a workspace from their own sidebar only) and drops the orphaned `workspaces.hidden`. `getWorkspaces` joins the caller's membership row to surface `hidden`. Idempotent (PRAGMA column checks). Script: `src/db/apply-0026-member-hidden.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0027_billing` — adds the `subscriptions` table + `workspaces.billing_owner_id` (backfilled to each workspace's earliest `role='owner'` member). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS + PRAGMA column check). Script: `src/db/apply-0027-billing.ts`. Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0028_invites` — adds `workspace_invites` (email invitations + bearer token for `/invite/[token]`; pending invites reserve a seat). Idempotent. Script: `src/db/apply-0028-invites.ts`. Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0029_user_consent` — adds nullable `user.analytics_consent` ('granted'\|'denied'\|null) for cookie-less server-side analytics capture (see **Activation Funnel Analytics**). Idempotent (PRAGMA column check). Script: `src/db/apply-0029-user-consent.ts`. Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0030_oauth_agent_consent` — adds nullable `agent_name` + `display_name` to `oauth_auth_codes` and `display_name` to `oauth_access_tokens` (`agent_name` there already exists from `0024`). Lets the user pick the agent brand icon + give the connection a friendly name on the OAuth consent screen; both are stamped on the auth code and copied to the access token at exchange (and preserved across refresh-token rotation). Idempotent (PRAGMA column checks). Script: `src/db/apply-0030-oauth-agent-consent.ts`. Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0031_signup_attribution` — adds nullable first-touch acquisition columns to `user` (`signup_ref`, `signup_utm_source`, `signup_utm_medium`, `signup_utm_campaign`, `signup_referrer`), copied off the `remnus_first_touch` cookie in the `createUser` event. Feeds the PostHog signup-funnel channel breakdown (`$set_once` person props); the admin landing-traffic card reads PostHog directly (`getTrafficSources`), not these columns. Idempotent (PRAGMA column check per column). Script: `src/db/apply-0031-signup-attribution.ts`. Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0032_demo_feedback` — adds the `demo_feedback` table (sentiment + comment; `user_id` ON DELETE SET NULL). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0032-demo-feedback.ts`. Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0033_mailing` — adds `email_campaigns` + `email_log` and the `user.email_unsubscribed_at`/`user.email_suppressed` suppression columns (see **Mailing System**). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS + PRAGMA column checks). Script: `src/db/apply-0033-mailing.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0034_agent_usage` — **rebuilds `agent_activity`** (SQLite can't relax NOT NULL in place): `token_id` nullable (PAT calls; FK CASCADE kept) + new `oauth_token_id` (FK→`oauth_access_tokens` SET NULL — fixes the silent OAuth audit-log drop), `owner_user_id` (FK→user SET NULL; denormalized token owner, backfilled for legacy PAT rows EXCLUDING the `rmns-demo` seed token so owner-based queries keep ignoring seed activity), `response_bytes` (nullable; response payload size for the usage meters). New index `agent_activity_owner_created_idx (owner_user_id, created_at)`. Idempotent (PRAGMA column check gates the rebuild). Script: `src/db/apply-0034-agent-usage.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0035_deleted_items` — adds the `deleted_items` tombstone table (powers the MCP `get_changes_since` delta-sync tool — see **Database Tables**). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0035-deleted-items.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0036_page_links` — adds the `page_links` content-derived link-graph table (powers the MCP `get_related_pages` tool — see **Database Tables**). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0036-page-links.ts`; existing content backfilled via `src/db/backfill-page-links.ts` (idempotent, run AFTER the apply script). Both applied/run on local + Turso.
- `0037_user_sessions_platform` — adds nullable `user_sessions.platform` (`'web'` | `'tauri'`; null = legacy row, treated as web). Stamped once at session creation by `/api/activity/ping` from the `remnus_platform` cookie (`isTauriRequest()`). Powers the admin panel's desktop-usage stats (`getEngagementOverview`'s `desktopUsersTotal`/`desktopUsersActive30d` + per-user `usesDesktop`, and `getUserDetail`'s `activity.usesDesktop`). Idempotent (PRAGMA column check), simple `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` — no rebuild needed. Script: `src/db/apply-0037-user-sessions-platform.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0038_account_deletion_tokens` — adds the `account_deletion_tokens` table (short-lived, single-use, DB-backed confirmation token for GDPR self-service account deletion — see **Database Tables** and **Account Deletion**). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0038-account-deletion-tokens.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0039_knowledge_context` — adds `knowledge_metadata`, exact-revision `knowledge_reviews`, `workspace_context_policies`, and short-lived `context_runs`, plus cleanup triggers. Idempotent. Script: `src/db/apply-0039-knowledge-context.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0040_prospect_invites` / `0041_prospect_invite_opens` / `0042_page_card_collapsed` — prospect-invite funnel tables + per-row `pages.card_collapsed`. Scripts: `src/db/apply-0040-prospect-invites.ts`, `apply-0041-prospect-invite-opens.ts`, `apply-0042-page-card-collapsed.ts`.
- `0043_recurrence` — adds the `recurrence_series` table plus `pages.series_id` / `pages.occurrence_date` / `pages.series_detached` and the UNIQUE (series_id, occurrence_date) index that makes materialization top-up idempotent. Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS + `PRAGMA table_info` guards before each ADD COLUMN). Script: `src/db/apply-0043-recurrence.ts`. See **Recurring Calendar Cards**. **Next migration: `when` > `1782100000000`.**
- `0044_demo_sessions` — adds the `demo_sessions` table (durable per-demo usage log; `user_id` ON DELETE SET NULL so it outlives the 6-hourly demo purge). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0044-demo-sessions.ts`. Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0045_page_comments` — adds the `page_comments` table (time-ordered comment thread on a page/row, separate from its body — see **Database Tables** and the MCP `add_comment` tool). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0045-page-comments.ts`. Apply to Turso **before** deploying the code that reads/writes this table (0042's deploy-before-migrate mistake left every database view blank workspace-wide — don't repeat it). Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0046_refresh_rotation_grace` — adds `oauth_access_tokens.replaced_by_token_id` / `.replaced_at`, set together when a refresh grant rotates a row away. Lets `handleRefreshToken` (`src/app/api/oauth/token/route.ts`) tell a same-refresh-token replay within a short grace window (a legitimate concurrent client instance losing the rotation race — served from an in-memory, best-effort, per-instance cache of the just-issued pair) apart from stale reuse well after (treated as token theft per OAuth 2.1 §4.13.2: the whole rotation chain is revoked). See `.ai/FIX_OAUTH_FOLLOWUP.md` §3. Idempotent (`PRAGMA table_info` guards). Script: `src/db/apply-0046-refresh-rotation-grace.ts`. Apply to both local + Turso.
- `0047_page_snapshots` — adds the `page_snapshots` table (trash + content version history — see **Database Tables** and **Trash & Version History**). Idempotent (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0047-page-snapshots.ts`. Applied to both local + Turso.
- `0048_access_requests` — adds the `workspace_access_requests` table (the `npx remnus join` request a non-member sends to a workspace owner — see **Project Install** §5). UNIQUE (workspace_id, user_id) is load-bearing: one open request per person, and a reused `denied` row is what makes the cooling-off window enforceable. Idempotent (CREATE TABLE / CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0048-access-requests.ts`. **Applied to local and Turso (2026-09-23).**
- `0049_agent_metrics` — adds nullable `agent_activity.baseline_bytes` / `.duration_ms` / `.items_affected` (the savings card — see **Agent Savings Metrics**). Legacy rows keep NULL in all three and drop out of every metric; no backfill is possible and inventing one would defeat the point. Idempotent (PRAGMA column check), plain `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`. Script: `src/db/apply-0049-agent-metrics.ts`. **Applied to local and Turso (2026-09-23).**
- `0050_dashboards` — adds the `dashboards` table (the 1:1 detail table for the new `dashboard` item type — see **Dashboards**). `workspace_items.type`, `page_snapshots.item_type` and `deleted_items.item_type` need NO DDL: all three are plain TEXT with no CHECK. Idempotent (CREATE TABLE / CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS). Script: `src/db/apply-0050-dashboards.ts`. **Applied to local and Turso (2026-09-23).**
- `0051_agent_savings_rollup` — adds `agent_savings_rollup` (lifetime savings of `agent_activity` rows the audit hard limit deletes; UNIQUE (workspace_id, owner_user_id) is the upsert target, owner SET NULL with the user). Idempotent. Script: `src/db/apply-0051-agent-savings-rollup.ts`. **Applied to local and Turso (2026-09-23).**
- `0052_search_index` — the `search_workspace` full-text index: `search_docs` (+ UNIQUE `search_docs_item_id_idx`, `search_docs_workspace_idx`), contentless FTS5 `search_fts`, 11 `search_*` triggers, and a full rebuild in one transaction (see **Database Tables**). Idempotent — tables IF NOT EXISTS, triggers dropped and recreated, index rebuilt — so it is also the repair command. The code falls back to the substring scan while the index is absent, so apply order against a deploy is not critical, but apply it before relying on ranked search. Script: `src/db/apply-0052-search-index.ts` (prints the remote host, not just "REMOTE"). Proven on a Turso dev database (2026-09-24: #1811 did not reproduce, FK cascades fire the triggers, contentless_delete supported, SQLite 3.47.0). **Applied to local; NOT yet to Turso production** (deploy day). `db:drift` now also checks `search_fts` and the triggers.
- `0053_agent_activity_workspace_created` — replaces `agent_activity_workspace_id_idx` with `agent_activity_workspace_created_idx (workspace_id, created_at)`. Measured (bench:graph, 30,000 audit rows in one workspace): the knowledge map's 7-day activity window read all 30,000 through the old index and 1,770 through the new one (`SEARCH … (workspace_id=? AND created_at>?)`). Index-only — no code depends on it for correctness, so apply order against a deploy does not matter; the dropped index was a strict prefix of the new one. Idempotent (CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS / DROP INDEX IF EXISTS), prints the query plan. Script: `src/db/apply-0053-agent-activity-workspace-created.ts`. **Applied to local (2026-09-24); NOT yet to Turso production** (deploy day, with 0052).
- **Before every deploy: `npm run db:drift`** ([src/scripts/check-schema-drift.ts](src/scripts/check-schema-drift.ts), read-only) compares every table/column/named index in `schema.ts` with the target database and exits 1 on anything missing. Most migrations here are hand-applied scripts outside `_journal.json`, so this is the only reliable answer to "what is applied where" — on 2026-09-23 it found 0048–0051 missing on Turso and 0030/0037/0040/0041 missing on a local DB that notes called up to date.
- **Two databases / env precedence gotcha:** `.env` has the **Turso** `DATABASE_URL`; `.env.local` overrides it with `file:local.db`. Next.js (dev) uses `.env.local` → **local.db**, but the apply scripts call `dotenv.config()` which reads only `.env` → **Turso**. So a plain `npx tsx src/db/apply-00xx-*.ts` migrates Turso only; for local dev also run it with an explicit override: `DATABASE_URL="file:local.db" npx tsx src/db/apply-00xx-*.ts`. Apply every manual migration to **both**.
- **tsx/ESM import-hoisting gotcha (scripts importing `@/db`):** in a tsx script, `import * as dotenv from 'dotenv'; dotenv.config(); import { db } from '@/db';` does NOT work — imports are hoisted, so `@/db` initializes its libsql client *before* `dotenv.config()` runs, sees no `DATABASE_URL`, and **silently falls back to `file:local.db`** (the script then reads/writes local.db while claiming to be on Turso; this misdirected a "Turso" backfill on 2026-07-07 and likely earlier "applied to Turso" claims for `0034`, which turned out never to have reached prod). Fix: make `import 'dotenv/config';` the **first import** (module evaluation order guarantees it runs before `@/db`) — see `src/db/backfill-page-links.ts`. Raw `createClient` apply scripts are immune (they read env in the module body after `config()`); a command-line `DATABASE_URL=...` prefix is always safe (set before the process starts). Also established: **the Turso target loaded from `.env` is the production database** — treat plain (no-override) script runs as prod operations.
- **libsql DDL caveat:** Drizzle's `migrate()` runs SQL in a `batch()` call. libsql's `batch()` silently fails DDL statements (ALTER TABLE, CREATE TABLE, etc.) — the call returns "complete" but changes are not applied. Use `client.execute()` directly for DDL, or manually apply + insert into `__drizzle_migrations` via a helper script. Migration 0016 was applied this way.
- Apply with: `npx tsx src/db/migrate.ts`

### Agent Savings Metrics

The sidebar's savings card ([AgentSavingsCard.tsx](src/components/features/AgentSavingsCard.tsx)) and the breakdown inside `AgentsModal`. Service: [src/lib/services/agentMetrics.ts](src/lib/services/agentMetrics.ts) (cookie-free); action: [src/lib/actions/agentMetrics.ts](src/lib/actions/agentMetrics.ts). Migration `0049` adds the three columns it reads.

**The governing rule: a baseline is measured or it is NULL.** Every "saved" figure is the difference between what a tool actually sent and what the *same information* would have cost by the only other route available — computed at call time from objects already in memory, never extrapolated from an average. A call whose naive alternative cannot be derived exactly writes NULL and contributes nothing. This is the whole reason the number is worth showing: one invented figure and none of them are believable again.

**Baselines that exist (and their formula):**

| Call | Baseline = | Where |
| ---- | ---------- | ----- |
| `resource:digest` | Σ page body + title lengths, plus Σ (title + content + properties) of every database row — i.e. reading the workspace instead of its map | `getWorkspaceDigest` returns `{ text, naiveBytes }`; both sums come off the two queries it already runs |
| `get_page` / `get_pages` `mode:"outline"` | `JSON.stringify` of the same payload with full bodies (plus comments, when asked for) | [tools/read.ts](src/app/api/mcp/tools/read.ts) |
| `query_database` with `fields` | the identical query without the projection: untrimmed `properties` on every row + the full schema | `queryDatabaseRows` returns an extra `baselineBytes`; **read.ts destructures it off before serializing** — it is measurement, not payload |

**Baselines that deliberately do NOT exist:** `get_changes_since` (the naive alternative is re-reading the whole map, whose size is unknown without building it — a per-call cost on a hot path), `search_workspace`, `prepare_context`, and every write tool. They log NULL. Do not "fix" this with an estimate.

**The four displayed metrics:**

1. **Tokens saved** (primary, all time) — `SUM(MAX(baseline_bytes - response_bytes, 0)) / 4`. The ÷4 bytes→tokens conversion is the same one the usage meters have always used. All-time stays honest under retention: the nightly prune deletes rows only past `AUDIT_HARD_RETENTION_DAYS` (400) and, in the same transaction, adds their savings to `agent_savings_rollup`, which `getAgentMetrics` sums in (see **Billing & Plan Limits** → audit retention). The rollup condition must stay identical to this query's.
2. **Recalled, not rediscovered** (30 days) — distinct `target_id`s of successful `get_page` calls whose page/row was created more than 24h before the read. Joins both `pages` and `workspace_items`, since `target_id` can name either. Timestamps pass through `asEpochSeconds()`: legacy rows hold `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` TEXT (see the createdAt gotcha), and SQLite's type ordering sorts every TEXT value above every integer, so comparing raw made this metric read **zero forever** on the oldest workspaces. `unixepoch()` converts the stored UTC string; it does not guess.
3. **Pages written by agents** (7 days) — `SUM(COALESCE(items_affected, 1))` over `create_page`/`update_page`/`bulk_create_pages`/`bulk_update_pages`. Only the bulk tools set `items_affected`; without it a 40-page `bulk_create_pages` would have counted as 1 and understated agent output by 40×. This counts **writes, not distinct pages** — a page edited twice counts twice, which the tooltip says out loud.
4. **Typical answer time** (30 days) — median `duration_ms`, taken by `ORDER BY duration_ms LIMIT 1 OFFSET n/2`. The timer is a request-scoped `AsyncLocalStorage` opened at the top of `handleMcpRequest` (so auth/bcrypt is included, because the agent waited for it) and read in `logActivity`, which is why ~30 handlers needed no stopwatch threading. Network time is excluded and the tooltip says so.

**Never displayed: money saved or "hours saved."** Both need assumptions the product cannot verify, and one wrong dollar figure discredits the honest numbers beside it. If asked for, it ships default-off with its price assumption written on screen.

**Scope and the project window:** `getMyAgentMetrics(workspaceId?)` sums by `owner_user_id` when called bare, and filters by workspace when given an id. A project window must pass its workspace id — the card is workspace-scoped content data a locked session may see, while the account-wide sum is not (`getCurrentUser()` throws there). The card also renders nothing at all while `savedBytes` is 0: a dashboard full of zeroes makes the product look smaller than it is.

**`resource:digest` is the one resource read in the audit log.** It is logged under a `resource:` name precisely so nobody mistakes it for a callable tool when filtering `query_audit_log`.

### Recurring Calendar Cards

Design doc: `.ai/RECURRENCE_DESIGN.md`. Migration `0043`. Engine tests: `npm run test:recurrence`.

**Occurrences are real rows, not a virtual expansion.** Google Calendar can compute occurrences on the fly because an occurrence there is nearly empty. In Remnus every database row is also a *page*: it carries a body, sub-items, links, backlinks, agent-edit provenance and MCP addressability. A computed ghost has no `page.id`, so it could not be written into, linked to, dragged, or read by an agent — which is why materialization won, at the cost of a horizon (`max(today + 90d, visible window + 7d)`, hard cap 500/series) and a top-up.

**Top-up is read-triggered.** `CalendarView` calls `loadRecurrenceState(databaseId, windowEnd)` whenever the visible window changes; paging forward past the horizon simply extends it. This is deliberate — it means the feature needs no cron, so it works identically on Vercel and on a self-hosted Docker install. The UNIQUE (series_id, occurrence_date) index makes a concurrent second top-up (a future cron, another tab) a no-op instead of a duplicate.

**Two invariants everything is built to preserve:**

1. **The past is immutable.** "This and following" does not mutate the rule — `splitSeriesAt` ENDS the current series the day before the split point (`endRuleBefore`) and inserts a NEW `recurrence_series` row starting there, linked via `parent_series_id`. So past cards keep their own frozen rule and you never have to ask "was this card generated before or after the change?". The user-visible case this exists for: *"I ran this daily for a week, now make it weekly from today — don't touch last week."*
2. **User work is never silently destroyed.** `findDirtyOccurrenceIds` treats an occurrence as dirty when its body, title or properties diverged from the template, it has a nested sub-page or any link in/out, `agent_edited_at` is set, it was dragged to a day other than its `occurrence_date`, or it is already detached. Dirty future occurrences are **detached and left in place**, never regenerated away — and their dates are added to the new series' `exDates`, so the new rhythm does not stack a second empty card on top of work someone already did.

**Delete scopes** (`deleteOccurrences`): `this` (row + EXDATE so top-up can't resurrect it) · `thisAndFollowing` (UNTIL = occurrence − 1, forward rows removed) · `all` (whole series). Preserving dirty cards is the default in all three; the dialog exposes an explicit "delete cards with content too" checkbox after telling the user how many there are. `deleteOccurrences` returns ids — `actions/recurrence.ts` owns the tombstone + `purgeReferencesTo` + `removePageLinksFor` cleanup, exactly as `deletePage` does for a single row.

**Rule-change scopes:** `thisAndFollowing` (split, the default) and `all` (`replaceSeriesRule`, re-rhythms from the series start and does rewrite the past). There is no "just this occurrence" for a rhythm change. Dragging a series card to another day needs no dialog — it just changes the date and the `occurrence_date` mismatch marks it dirty.

**Deleting a recurring row must EXDATE it.** `exdateOccurrenceForPage` is called from BOTH plain delete paths — `actions/page.ts` `deletePage` (Table/Kanban/editor) and `services/workspace.ts` `deleteItemFromWorkspace` (MCP). Without it the next materialization sees a date the rule still calls for with no card on it, which is exactly the "create it" signal — so the delete silently undid itself. If you add a third row-delete path, it needs this too.

**Where recurrence is reachable:** the calendar card's ⋯ menu and its right-click menu (Repeat / Edit repeat / Remove from series), and the `SeriesPanel` inside an opened card. `KanbanBoard`, `TableLayout` and the peek modal show the ⟳ badge and route their delete through the scope dialog via `DatabaseView`'s `useRecurrenceControls` instance (`onRecurringDelete`). That instance passes an EMPTY rule map on purpose — those views have no "repeat" menu, so only the scope path is used and the map would cost a second query per mount. Its `onPlainDelete` sets `confirmDeletePageId` rather than deleting: the opened card's Delete goes through the same call, and a non-series row must still get its confirm.

**Agents — writes:** `create_page` and `update_page` both take an optional `recurrence` input (flattened — `until`/`count` rather than the nested `end` union — with an optional `dateColumn` resolved by id, then case-insensitive name, then the first date column). It is applied AFTER the row write and reports `recurrenceError` in the result instead of throwing: the row landed, so failing the call would make the agent retry into a duplicate. On `update_page`, a row that ALREADY repeats requires `recurrenceScope` (`thisAndFollowing` | `all`); without it the call is refused and returns the per-scope impact counts. That refusal is the feature — an agent choosing silently would be deciding, on the user's behalf, whether their filled-in past cards get rewritten, and it has no way to ask.

**Agents — reads:** `get_page`/`get_pages` carry a `recurrence` block (seriesId, occurrenceDate, detached, rule, occurrences) on series rows, so an agent can tell it is editing one of many. `query_database` rows carry only `recurring: true` (plus `recurringDetached`) — a full rule per row would cost ~25 tokens × page size, and the marker is enough to say "call get_page before changing the rhythm". Ordinary rows carry neither field, so a database with no recurring rows pays nothing.

**Cron:** `/api/cron/recurrence` (03:00 daily, `vercel.json`) extends every stale series to the default horizon, bounded at 300 series per run. It is a backstop, not the primary path — read-triggered top-up is what makes the feature work on installs with no scheduler.

**OKF:** the rule rides along under `x-remnus.recurrence` (seriesId, occurrenceDate, detached, rule) on each row concept — nested rather than top-level because OKF v0.2 has no core concept of a repeating item, and inventing a core-namespace key would misstate the spec. The series table is fetched once per export, not joined per row (one rule is shared by up to 500 cards). **Import does not reconstruct a live series, deliberately:** the OKF importer flattens every concept into a single generic "Concepts" database with a fixed 8-column schema and no date column — it does not rebuild original database structure at all, so hanging a rule off a column that doesn't exist would be fabricating structure. The rule text still survives a Remnus→OKF→Remnus round trip inside the preserved raw frontmatter column. Reconstructing series on import only becomes meaningful if the importer ever restores real schemas.

**Not yet done:** per-plan caps on generated rows (a global 500/series + 90-day horizon is in place; the per-tier numbers are an open product decision), and reconstructing series on OKF import (see above).

### Dashboards

A third `workspace_items.type`, alongside `page` and `database`. A dashboard is a status screen assembled from a fixed catalog of blocks that read **live** from the workspace's own databases. Its intended author is an **agent**: the whole screen is one strict JSON document, small enough to write in a single call and patchable one block at a time. Migration `0050`. Agents build and patch them over MCP (`create_dashboard` / `update_dashboard`, below); public reference: [docs/mcp/dashboards.md](docs/mcp/dashboards.md) (`/wiki/dashboards`, in `WIKI_PAGES`).

**Storage.** `dashboards` (`item_id` FK→`workspace_items` CASCADE, `spec` JSON, explicit `created_at`/`updated_at`) — the same 1:1 detail-table shape `standalone_pages` has for pages. Widening `workspace_items.type` needed **no DDL**: the column is plain TEXT with no CHECK (SQLite/Drizzle enums are TS-only). The same is true of `page_snapshots.item_type` and `deleted_items.item_type`, which now also accept `'dashboard'`.

**Spec.** [src/lib/dashboard/schema.ts](src/lib/dashboard/schema.ts) is the ONE source of truth — zod, versioned (`version: 1`), shared by the server renderer, the MCP write tools and the web block editor. Two invariants everything else rests on:

1. **Every block carries a stable `id`** (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,32}$`, unique per dashboard). Blocks are patched, moved and deleted **by id, never by array position** — an update tool that trusted the order would corrupt a dashboard the moment two writers reordered blocks.
2. **Blocks name a data SOURCE, never the data** — `databaseId` + filters, resolved at render time. Embedding rows would make a dashboard stale the day it was written.

Objects are `z.strictObject`: an unknown field is a **rejected write**, not a silently dropped one. Reads are lenient **per block** (`parseDashboardSpec`) — an unreadable block costs its own tile ("this block could not be read"), never the page. `validateDashboardSpec` is the write gate. Filters reuse the database-view operator set and are evaluated by the **same** code ([src/lib/tableFilters.ts](src/lib/tableFilters.ts), extracted out of `DatabaseView.tsx` for exactly this reason): a filter that selects one set of rows in a view must select the same set in a dashboard block.

**Catalog (v1, fixed — 7 types).** `metric` · `chart` (bar/line/donut) · `database_embed` · `list` · `text` · `links` · `activity`. Each carries an agent-facing description in `DASHBOARD_BLOCK_CATALOG`, beside the schema it describes; [src/lib/dashboard/catalog.ts](src/lib/dashboard/catalog.ts) serves it as the MCP resource `remnus://dashboard/catalog` with field signatures **derived from the zod schemas via `z.toJSONSchema`** (so the catalog cannot drift from the write gate) plus three templates (`project-status`, `backlog-health`, `weekly-pulse`) that are run through `validateDashboardSpec` before being served. `width` is `quarter`/`half`/`full` on a 4-column desktop grid, full width on mobile; omitted = the type's own default.

**Render.** Server component throughout ([src/components/features/dashboard/](src/components/features/dashboard/)); `resolveDashboard` ([src/lib/dashboard/data.ts](src/lib/dashboard/data.ts)) resolves the WHOLE dashboard in a fixed number of queries — every block's source database is collected up front and each database's rows are read **once**, then filtered in memory per block, so ten metrics over one database cost one row query. `content` is not selected (no block renders row bodies, and it is the largest column). Access control is **structural, not a per-block check**: the database lookup is already constrained to the dashboard's workspace, so a block naming another workspace's database simply finds nothing and renders as unavailable. Charts are inline SVG using the same validated 8-hue palette as `TrafficTrendChart` — **no charting dependency was added**. `database_embed` reuses the real `TableLayout`/`KanbanBoard`: row edits go through the same server actions the database route uses, while view-config edits (column order, hidden columns, ad-hoc filters/sorts) stay local to the visit — a dashboard embeds someone else's saved view, it does not rewrite it.

**Three failure states, all local to one tile:** source database deleted → "source removed"; a column the block names is gone → "column removed"; the block itself does not validate → "could not be read" plus the reason. A spec that is not a v1 dashboard at all shows one banner and an otherwise-working page.

**Human editing.** Rename + icon (inline, writing to `workspace_items` like a page's), reorder, remove, **add a block and edit any block's settings** (P7) in `DashboardBlockEditor` — a flat right-side drawer whose every field is a picker over the workspace's real databases/columns/select options/views (`getDashboardEditorOptions`, loaded when the drawer opens, never with the page), so a human cannot name a column that does not exist and required fields gate Save instead of producing a server error. It edits the same JSON an agent writes, through the same service (`saveDashboardBlock` → `patchDashboard` with `add`/`replace`), and strips values equal to read-time defaults so a human-edited block stays as small as an agent's. The service's English error text is logged, never shown; the drawer shows its own localized message. Blocks that cannot be read have no edit button — only remove.

**MCP (P7).** `create_dashboard` / `update_dashboard` in [tools/write.ts](src/app/api/mcp/tools/write.ts), both thin over the cookie-free [src/lib/services/dashboards.ts](src/lib/services/dashboards.ts), which the web actions use too — one write path. Rules that are load-bearing:

1. **The catalog is a resource, not schema.** Block arrays are `z.array(z.any())` in `inputSchema` and validated per block in the service; seven block shapes in `tools/list` would be paid by every session. Measured (`bench:mcp-budget`, bytes/4): `create_dashboard` ≈ 253 + `update_dashboard` ≈ 302 model-visible tokens on write-scoped sessions, zero on read-scoped ones; the catalog resource is ~1,500 tokens, read only when building one. A typical six-block create call is ~1.1 KB ≈ 270 tokens of arguments.
2. **Patches by id, all-or-nothing, only touched blocks validated.** `update_dashboard` = `{add, update ({id,…fields} merged, `null` removes, type immutable), remove, order (listed first, rest keep relative order)}` + title/icon, applied remove → update → add → order in memory before any write. Untouched blocks — including unreadable ones — pass through verbatim, exactly like P6's `loadSpecForWrite`.
3. **Compare-and-swap.** The stored spec is read as RAW TEXT (a raw `sql` select of `dashboards.spec`, bypassing the json-mode decoder) and the UPDATE is guarded by `spec = <that text>`; a lost race re-reads and re-applies the id-based ops (3 retries). Two agents patching different blocks both land.
4. **References are normalized, then the caller's input is stored — not the zod output.** `databaseId` accepts `databases.id` or the item id; every column field accepts a name (case-insensitive) and stores the id; select/status filter values are case-corrected to the option; `viewId` accepts a view name; `links` accept a database id. Defaults are applied at READ time, so storing them would only lengthen every later `get_page`.
5. **Refusal vs warning.** A block that fails zod, or a source database not in THIS workspace (a foreign id and a made-up one produce the same message, so nothing leaks), refuses the whole call — except an `update` that leaves `source` alone, where it is a warning (retitling a tile whose database a human deleted must not fail). Unknown column / non-option filter value / non-number sum column / non-date trend / dead link → `warnings`. After writing, `resolveDashboard` runs once more and reports touched blocks that match no rows, plus any block anywhere that renders unavailable or unreadable — one warning per block. Errors name the call's argument path (`add[0] ("id")`, generated ids omitted), warnings name the block id.
6. **Output:** `{id, url, blocks: [ids in order], warnings?}`. `url` comes from `appUrl(ctx, …)` — `TokenContext.appOrigin` is set in `runMcpRequest` from the request URL (the app is served from the same origin), falling back to `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`.
7. **Live refresh:** a spec write also bumps `workspace_items.updated_at` — that is what `changeVersion` sees; `dashboards.updated_at` alone would never refresh an open page. No `publish()`, no `revalidatePath` from MCP (the web actions still revalidate their own route).

`get_page` returns a dashboard as `{…, url, spec}` with the spec as an OBJECT (the service still stores it as a compact JSON string in `content`; `shapeDashboardRead` in tools/read.ts parses it — an escaped string costs a backslash per quote), and in `mode:"outline"` as `blocks: [{id,type,title}]`. `get_pages` shapes dashboards the same way. `delete_page` needed nothing new (P6's `dashboard` branch; preview/confirm unchanged, verified over HTTP).

**Route.** `/dashboard/<workspace item id>`. Unlike `/page/*` and `/db/*` it does **not** bow out under Tauri — the content is server-rendered, so there is no client pane for `TabPane` to mount. It behaves like `/admin`: `isTabbable` yes, `isKeepAlivePane` no. Project windows work: both data calls go through `getCurrentUserAllowingWorkspaceLock()` + `assertWorkspaceLockAllows()`.

**What the enum widening touched** — every one of these was audited, and none is left silently broken:

| Area | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Sidebar | Own icon (`LayoutDashboard` via `PageIcon` `fallbackType`), `/dashboard/<id>` href, active state, context menu, rename, duplicate, delete. Like a database, a dashboard **cannot hold children** — `isInvalidDropTarget` now tests `type !== 'page'` instead of listing types. |
| Delete (web + MCP) | Both recursions have a `dashboard` branch; the spec is snapshotted as JSON in `page_snapshots.content` (a dashboard has no markdown body competing for the slot) and a `'dashboard'` tombstone is written for delta-sync. |
| Trash / restore | Listed and restorable with its own icon; an unparseable stored spec restores as an **empty** dashboard rather than failing the restore — the item, its title and its place in the tree are what the user asked back for. |
| Duplicate | Copies the spec as-is: same workspace, same databases, same live numbers. |
| `checkItemHasContent` | A dashboard counts as non-empty once it has at least one block. (The old `else` branch treated every non-page as a database.) |
| `getPageById` (MCP `get_page`) | Returns `type: 'dashboard'` with the spec as its body (compact JSON since P7; the MCP handler hands it out as a `spec` object). Without this it fell through to the database branch and came back claiming to be a database with a null `databaseId`. |
| `list_workspace` / `search_workspace` / `get_changes_since` | Dashboards flow through as `type: 'dashboard'`; `ChangeEntry` and `deleted_items` accept it. |
| Workspace digest | Counted separately ("N pages, M databases, K dashboards") instead of being lumped into the page count, and emits no char count (a dashboard has no body, so `0 chars` would only invite a pointless `get_page`). |
| `page_links` | **Out of the graph in both directions, deliberately.** `to_type` only knows page/database/database_row, so a link to a dashboard would have to be recorded as something it is not — and `getRelatedPages` plus the backlinks panel would repeat that lie. The editor's link picker (`editor/pageLinkData.ts`) therefore filters dashboards out. A dashboard's own references are data sources, resolved live (and shown as "source removed" when they die), which is strictly better than a `purgeReferencesTo` rewrite of a JSON spec. Revisit both halves together if dashboards ever need backlinks — half-connected is the state to avoid. |
| Knowledge / OKF | Not knowledge items, and not in an OKF bundle: the format carries knowledge documents, and a dashboard is a live view whose data is already exported through its source databases. Excluded in **one** place — `getOkfWorkspaceSnapshot` — so every consumer of the snapshot inherits it. `listKnowledgeCorpus` and `resolveKnowledgeItem` skip them. |
| Sharing (`shared_pages`) | **Out of scope for v1.** Not offered in the UI and never in a public tree; the share route's item→href resolver names the in-app route rather than silently falling back to `/app`. |
| Admin analytics | Counted per type explicitly; dashboards get no tile of their own yet and are **not** counted as pages. |
| Tabs (Tauri) | Tabbable, not keep-alive; title/icon resolve from the workspace item, and a deleted dashboard's tab is pruned with it. |

**Explicitly not done** (each a decision, not an oversight): public sharing, cross-workspace sources, user-defined block types, drag-and-drop layout, and a `template` parameter on `create_dashboard` (templates live in the catalog resource; a server-side template filler would need column auto-detection and more tool schema).

### Knowledge Map (Remnus Graph, P12)

The workspace as a graph — built as a **maintenance and way-finding view**, not an Obsidian star map. Remnus has few explicit links (a local workspace set on 2026-09-23: 331 items, 8 `page_links` rows, 0 knowledge metadata), so a link-only graph would be scattered dots; this one draws every relation the workspace already records, plus what a link graph cannot show: trust, agent activity, and what needs attention.

**Where.** `/graph/<workspaceId>` ([route](src/app/[locale]/(app)/graph/[workspaceId]/page.tsx); `/graph` redirects to the active workspace). Sidebar row "Knowledge map" above Trash (active workspace), kept in project windows. Tauri: tabbable, not keep-alive (like `/dashboard/*`), tab icon `lucide:Map`. Resumable via `LastPathTracker`. Per page: `LocalGraphPanel` under the backlinks (see Project Structure).

**Service** ([services/graph.ts](src/lib/services/graph.ts), cookie-free; [actions/graph.ts](src/lib/actions/graph.ts) adds `getCurrentUserAllowingWorkspaceLock()` → `assertWorkspaceLockAllows()` → membership, in that order). Edge kinds, in priority order — when two nodes are related more than one way only the strongest (lowest code) is kept per pair:

| Code | Layer | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | hierarchy | `workspace_items.parentId` (a row can parent items) |
| 1 | membership | database → its rows; rows arrive **folded** into the database node (count badge); a view expands chosen databases by asking again (`expanded`) |
| 2 | link | `page_links` (page_link + child_block), ids resolved like `getRelatedPages`: item id → `databases.id` → row id; dangling/foreign ids drop out |
| 3 | mention | a body names another node's title without linking it (drawn dashed) |
| 4 | tag | `knowledge_metadata.tags`, as **tag nodes** (k edges per tag, not k(k-1)/2 pairs — calibration writes two-language tags on every concept); shown only when a tag joins ≥ 2 visible nodes |
| 5 | source | item → a repo **file/folder node** its `knowledge_metadata.sources` names (code layer, P13 — see below) |
| 6 | folder | folder node → file/folder inside it (code layer only; never the page tree) |

Rules a change must keep:
1. **One model, cached; activity per call.** Everything content-derived is built from ONE `db.batch` (items + bodies, rows + bodies, `page_links`, metadata, reviews) and cached per process under `getKnowledgeCorpusVersion` (same contract as the `prepare_context` index: null in the hot second = not cached; 16 MB LRU). Bodies are scanned and dropped — the cached model holds ids, titles, edges. Agent activity is NOT in the key (a read changes no content timestamp), so it is one grouped query per call, in parallel with the key.
2. **Trust uses `trustFor` from knowledge.ts** (exported for this) — never a second copy of the rule. Hashing only happens for nodes that have metadata. Stale is recomputed per call.
3. **Mentions** (`findMentions`): `foldText` on both sides; titles as word phrases (≤ 6 words) in a hash set + a prefix set, bodies scanned with a word window — O(words × matched prefix), never titles × bodies. Not indexed: generic titles (Notes/Todo/Backlog and Turkish equivalents), one-word titles < 4 letters, numbers, phrases < 5 letters, titles shared by two nodes (recurring rows). Anchor text of `<a data-page-link>` is stripped first (it IS the linked title; counting it made every often-linked page "too frequent"). Cut after the scan: df > max(8, 10% of scanned bodies). Measured on the repo's own `docs/` (46 pages): cuts 7 vocabulary titles (Authentication 20, Prompts 18, Agent Memory 17, Write Tools 16, Resources 15, Remnus MCP 11, Read Tools 11), keeps 41 pairs incl. real cross-references at df 8 (Getting Started, Playbooks, Calibrate). ≤ 60 mentions per source. `npm run bench:graph` re-measures.
4. **Agent activity = audit-log information, so it stays inside the plan's audit window** (`auditVisibleSince`, same `now` for both windows — two clock reads once flagged a 7-day request as "cut by the plan"). Targets map item id / row id / `databases.id`; `READ_TOOLS` (get_page, get_database_schema, query_database, get_related_pages) are reads, every other targeted tool a write; rows' own `agentEditedAt` counts as a write (content, not audit — follows the requested window). Legacy TEXT timestamps: index range on `created_at >= since` plus the exact `asEpochSeconds` test. Payload says `auditLimited` when the plan cut it.
5. **"Needs attention" is computed over the whole model**, not the view, so a folded concept row can be listed (clicking expands its database): *orphans* = pages with a body + labelled rows/databases with no link and no shared tag (being mentioned does not count — it is the linking opportunity the entry shows); *stale or deprecated*; *well connected, never reviewed* = links + mentions ≥ 3 and not human-reviewed (top 15), **excluding orphans** — a node only ever mentioned would otherwise read as both "nothing links here" and "well connected" at once (2026-09-25; the orphan entry already shows its mention count).
6. **Compact payload** ([lib/graph/types.ts](src/lib/graph/types.ts)): nodes are tuples `[id, kind, title, state, agentAt, ref?, count?]` (state = trust/status/stale/concept/agent bits), edges `[srcIdx, dstIdx, kind, weight?]`. Measured on a synthetic 5,000-node workspace (814 items + 4,186 rows, 1,460 links, 3,518 mentions, 30k audit rows; local SQLite): model build cold ~290–430 ms, warm call ~40–60 ms; folded 874 nodes / 3,233 edges = 97 KB raw / 37 KB gzip; every database expanded 5,060 / 11,094 = 508 KB / 190 KB; local graph (cap 120 nodes) ~10 KB.
7. **Local graph** (`getLocalGraph`, depth 1|2): rows unfolded; a database's rows are followed only when the database is the subject (≤ 40); a row brings its database, not its siblings; ≤ 12 members per tag at depth 2; 120-node cap (`truncated`).

**Renderer** ([components/features/graph/](src/components/features/graph/)). sigma 3 + graphology, ForceAtlas2 in its web worker, Louvain, d3-hierarchy — all in ONE client chunk that is in **no route's first load** (verified on `next build` via `.next/diagnostics/route-bundle-stats.json`: `/graph/*` adds a 1.7 KB loader; `GraphScreen` 19 KB and the 210 KB library chunk load on demand; the page editors carry only the collapsed `LocalGraphPanel`). No `@react-sigma/core`: a thin wrapper in `GraphCanvas`.
- **Two layouts, one renderer:** *network* (FA2, seeded from the radial tree so it converges; budget min(12 s, 1.5 s + 2 ms/node)) and *tree* (radial d3-hierarchy under a virtual workspace root; cross-links dimmed until something is focused). Colour: type · trust · agent activity · clusters (Louvain; the option is hidden unless link+mention+tag edges ≥ max(20, 0.3 × nodes)). Size: degree over visible layers, scaled down for large maps.
- **Live refresh must not reshuffle:** listens to `CHANGE_EVENT` and refetches; existing nodes keep their positions, a newcomer is placed at its existing neighbours' centroid and a 1.2 s FA2 pass runs with every old node `fixed`. Verified: a page inserted in the DB appeared without a reload, old nodes moved 0. **The baseline is the payload's `generatedAt`, not the first broadcast** — this screen is a dynamic chunk that fetches after mount, so the tracker's first ping is usually missed and a write between fetch and the next heartbeat was silently taken as the baseline (found in verification).
- **WebGL colours come from theme tokens** (`graphTheme.ts`, re-read on `data-theme` change) and must be **opaque**: sigma's blending treats colours as premultiplied, so a translucent colour lighter than its alpha — `rgba(135,146,162,.55)`, any light grey on a light theme — drew nothing (hierarchy edges vanished on catppuccin). `blend(color, canvas, alpha)` mixes on the CPU. Only tokens every theme defines as hex (Tailwind's own defaults are `oklch()`, which sigma cannot parse).
- **Mentions are dashed** via `dashedEdgeProgram.ts` (sigma's rectangle edge program + a screen-pixel dash in the fragment shader; picking stays solid).
- Small maps: label threshold 0 and stage padding relative to the canvas (a fixed 120 px left a 288 px local panel 48 px to draw in). Interaction: click = select (card with trust, link/mention counts, agent activity, Open, show/hide rows), double-click = open; the local panel opens on click. View prefs (layout/colour/layers/window) in `localStorage['remnus_graph_view_v1']`, try/catch.
- Measured in the browser (dev build, 1440×900): 874-node folded map ~100 fps during camera animation; after expanding the 1,500-row database (2,374 nodes / 6,647 edges, 264 KB) ~95 fps; phone width 390 px has no horizontal scroll and "Needs attention" becomes a bottom sheet above the mobile nav.

**Not done** (decisions): semantic/embedding edges, turning a mention into a link, editing from the graph (drag to move), graph on public share pages, per-row search of folded databases (the search box covers nodes on the map).

#### Code layer and the agent side (P13, 2026-09-24)

"The whole project" = the knowledge + the code it rests on. Remnus never reads the repository: the layer is built **only from the paths already in `knowledge_metadata.sources`** (calibration writes repo-relative files; `bulk_create_pages` / `create_page` / `create_database` / `update_page` accept them). No CLI change, no migration, no new MCP tool.

- **Paths** ([lib/graph/codePaths.ts](src/lib/graph/codePaths.ts), pure, `npm run test:code-paths`, 44 checks): `normalizeSourcePath` reads a source as a repo path only when it has no scheme (URLs and `C:` drive paths are **not** drawn — they are not part of the tree; a URL still matches itself in the lookup) and contains `/`, ends in an extension, is a dotfile or a known extension-less name (Dockerfile, Makefile…); `#L10-L20` / `:42` anchors, `./`, backslashes and a leading `/` are dropped, a glob names the folder it starts in, `..` is refused, a trailing `/` = folder. `matchSource` = `exact` (also an absolute query by its repo-relative tail — a one-segment source such as `README.md` must match exactly) / `folder` / `inside`, case-insensitive.
- **Graph** ([services/graph.ts](src/lib/services/graph.ts) `buildCodeLayer`): node ids `code:<path>` (folder paths end in `/`), kinds `file`/`folder`, `count` = items citing it directly. A folder is drawn when a source names it or it holds ≥ 2 things; single-child chains collapse (`src/lib/services/auth.ts` alone does not bring three empty folders). A code node's parent is a **`folder` edge, never `hierarchy`** — the first version let the page-tree loop claim it (priority 0 beat 6), so every folder edge was silently a "Page tree" edge; the loop now skips code kinds. Code nodes stay out of the mention scan, carry no trust/agent state (their colour is fixed in every mode, `opt-pink`), and are never orphans/stale/hubs. Rows' sources fold onto their database like every other edge.
- **Sent only on request:** `getWorkspaceGraph({ code: true })`; the payload always carries `codePaths` (distinct cited paths) so the "Code files (N)" layer switch appears only when there is something to show.
