The switch is **off by default** and turning it on refetches (newcomers placed by the live-refresh path, old nodes pinned). The local panel always includes code nodes (a page's files, at two steps the other pages resting on them). Tree layout: code nodes sit outside the radial tree like tags (files at their items' centroid, folders at their files'), with a deterministic golden-angle nudge — without it two files of one page landed on one point and the force layout could not pull them apart (found in the P13 visual check).
- **Measured** (`bench:graph`, synthetic 5k workspace whose labelled items cite a 300-file repo): 286 cited paths → 274 files + 17 folders, +1,044 edges; folded payload 104 KB → 140 KB raw (41 → 47 KB gzip) with the layer on; model +0 queries (sources come with the metadata already read). Browser: ~100 fps idle with or without the layer.
- **File → pages for agents:** `get_related_pages` takes `resource` instead of `pageId` (**no new tool** — a second tool's schema would be paid by every session). [services/codeSources.ts](src/lib/services/codeSources.ts) `getPagesForResource`: ONE `db.batch` (labelled items + rows that have sources, with titles), matched in memory — `LIKE` cannot do anchors/absolute paths/folders. `note` only when **no** item records sources (empty = unknown, not "nothing"). Audit row: `targetType 'resource'`, no target id (a path is not a workspace id). The `pageId` answer now carries the subject's `sources`. Schema cost (`bench:mcp-budget`, model-visible): get_related_pages 387 → 523 B, **+136 B ≈ +34 tok per session** on both scopes (read 5,692 → 5,828 B). Verified with the MCP SDK client over Streamable HTTP against `npm run dev` (`/api/mcp/w/<id>`).
- **`prepare_context`:** related refs from the top 3 concepts, room reserved first — see **Performance Rules → Context & search read path**.
- **Decided against (measured, 2026-09-24):**
  - *"Most connected N" line in the digest / `.remnus/workspace-map.md`:* +32–82 tok per read on the local workspaces (+18–34 % of their digest), and those hubs are already lines of a 10–21-line map; at 5k items the line adds information (hubs at map lines 11–817) but computing it puts a cold graph-model build (~500 ms, vs the digest's ~170 ms) on a path the bridge refreshes after **every** agent write — and every write changes the model's cache key. No evidence it changes agent behaviour, so per the AGENTS-block rule it stays out; agents get neighbours from `prepare_context` refs instead.
  - *PageRank in the context ranking* (hub noise, see above). *CLI file tree + import edges (B):* not started — A has its fuel (the P9.5 field calibration left `sources` on 46 of 47 labelled items), but B needs a CLI release, a new authenticated upload path and storage; revisit once the code layer is used on live data (roadmap deploy list has a read-only prod fill-rate query). *"Knowledge health" dashboard block (C4, optional):* not done — an 8th catalog type (schema, renderer, block editor, 8 locales) plus a graph-model build on a server-rendered page, for numbers the map's "Needs attention" panel already shows.

### Trash & Version History

Design doc: `.ai/FEATURE_BULK_AND_TRASH.md` (Parts A/B = trash, Part C = content versions). Migration `0047`. Service: `src/lib/services/snapshots.ts`. Both features share one table, `page_snapshots`, split by `reason: 'delete' | 'update'`.

#### Trash (delete snapshots, `reason: 'delete'`)

**Why a new table instead of soft-delete.** `deleted_items` already exists but is a tombstone (id/type/title/date only, no content — built for `get_changes_since` delta-sync, not recovery). A `deleted_at` column + a filter on every query was rejected: it means auditing every existing query, and one missed filter leaks deleted content back into view. `page_snapshots` writes a full content copy at the moment of deletion and leaves every existing delete/query path completely unchanged — zero leak surface.

**Restore is per-item, not per-subtree.** Deleting a folder with nested children, or a whole database with its rows, writes one `page_snapshots` row per node — exactly mirroring how `recordDeletionTombstone` already fires once per node in the same recursion. There is no "delete operation" grouping concept. Restoring a database brings back the empty shell (schema included, same `databases.id` reused so its row snapshots can find it again); its rows restore separately and reattach correctly because the database exists again by the time they're restored.

**Two restore rules, never silent:**
1. **Page/database whose original parent is gone:** restored to the workspace root, but the caller is told explicitly (`rerootedToRoot: true` in `RestoreResult`) — never silently rooted with no signal.
2. **Database row whose original database is gone:** restore is refused outright (`{restored: false, reason}`) — a row has no meaningful "root" to fall back to, unlike a page.

**Snapshot writes happen at every hard-delete path, not just the bulk MCP tools** — `deleteItemFromWorkspace`/`deleteWorkspaceItemAndDescendants` (MCP, `services/workspace.ts`), `deleteWorkspaceItemRecursive` (`actions/workspace.ts`, web sidebar delete), `deletePage` (`actions/page.ts`, web row delete). A new hard-delete path needs this too, same obligation as the existing `recordDeletionTombstone` one. **Deliberately excluded:** the recurrence auto-prune path (`removable` in `splitSeriesAt`/`deleteOccurrences`, `services/recurrence.ts` + `actions/recurrence.ts`) — those rows are guaranteed content-empty by the recurrence dirty-occurrence invariant, so snapshotting them would just fill the trash with noise on every rule edit.

**Links survive a delete + restore (2026-09-25, Hakan's decision).** A delete to the Trash never rewrites another page's body. Links other pages hold TO a trashed item stay in their markdown and in `page_links`; only the deleted items' own outgoing rows go (`removeOutgoingPageLinks`, once for the whole subtree or bulk batch). Restore reuses the same id, so every link works again with no repair, and `restoreSnapshot` re-runs `syncPageLinks` for the restored page/row's own body. The links are stripped only when the trash copy is gone for good: `purgeExpiredSnapshots` (30 days) and `enforceWorkspaceSnapshotByteCaps` delete with `.returning(...)` and hand the evicted `delete` rows to `releaseTrashedReferences`, which runs `purgeReferencesTo` → `removePageLinksFor` in chunks of 500 and skips any id that exists again (a failed restore). Meanwhile the editor marks them: `TrashedTargetsExtension` decorates page links (`.page-link-trashed`, `data-trashed`, dimmed + struck through; `BlockEditor.handleClick` skips `data-trashed`, and its `handleDOMEvents.click` stops the native follow) and link-only child blocks (`ChildBlockView` reads the `trashed` decoration spec) — `BlockEditor` asks `getTrashedLinkTargets` (`actions/trash.ts` → `listTrashedIds`) once per editor, never in share views. Owned child blocks are not dimmed: the existing reconciliation drops them while the child is gone and re-adds them when it returns. Readers already tolerate dangling targets (`getRelatedPages`, the graph, the OKF exporter). Why: the old per-node purge flattened a parent's link to its deleted child *before* the parent was snapshotted, and any outside page's link was unwrapped for good (found live in a customer calibration test). Regression: `DATABASE_URL="file:local.db" npm run test:trash-links`.

**`bulk_delete_pages`/`bulk_move_items` never all-or-nothing.** `bulkDeleteItemsFromWorkspace`/`bulkMoveItemsInWorkspace` use `Promise.allSettled` (same pattern as `getPagesByIds`) so one bad id in a batch can't sink the rest — this exists directly because `bulk_update_pages`'s `Promise.all` silently lost 11 rows once when a connection dropped mid-call. `bulk_move_items`' cross-database mode (`bulkMoveRowsToDatabase`) is the one genuinely new capability: `move_item` only reparents within the sidebar tree, but moving a database row to a DIFFERENT database needs a schema-compatibility check first (target must cover every source column by name AND type) — refused as a whole batch, naming the missing columns, never a silent partial property drop.

**Restore is human-only — no MCP tool.** `actions/trash.ts` is the only way in; an agent reviving something a human deliberately deleted would make the delete meaningless (same reasoning as `page_comments` agent writes being append-only). Tool count stays at 24 for this reason — trash has no MCP surface at all, not even read-only.

**UI: a sidebar-level modal, not a per-workspace Settings tab.** `TrashModal.tsx` sits next to `AgentsModal.tsx` behind its own persistent sidebar button (`WorkspaceSidebar.tsx`, right below "AI Agents") — deliberately NOT nested in `WorkspaceSettingsModal`, because a deletion can happen in any workspace from any client and the entry point shouldn't be scoped to whichever workspace happens to be active. Same "common ground" shape as Agents: `actions/trash.ts`'s `getMyTrash()` mirrors `agentToken.ts`'s `getUserWorkspacesWithTokens()` exactly — every workspace the caller is a member of, each with its own section (workspace icon+name header, entries or an empty-state line), grouped client-side by `TrashModal`'s `WorkspaceSection`. `getUserTrashCount()` feeds the sidebar badge, mirroring `getUserAgentTokenCount()`.

**Each trash entry carries a breadcrumb** (`TrashEntry.breadcrumb: string[]`, root-to-immediate-parent order, workspace name and the item's own title excluded — those are the section header and the row's own label respectively) so a flat list across many workspaces still reads as "where did this come from." Built by `listTrashForWorkspaces()` in `services/snapshots.ts`: 3 prefetched queries (all snapshots for the caller's workspaces, all `workspace_items`, all `databases`) regardless of trash size, then an in-memory ancestor walk per entry — an ancestor may itself have been deleted, so the walk checks the live `workspace_items` first, then falls back to another snapshot in the same batch (capped at 8 levels, stops rather than guesses on an unresolvable ancestor). A `database_row`'s breadcrumb resolves its database first (live `databases` row, or — if the database was ALSO deleted — the matching `database`-type snapshot via `page_snapshots.database_id`), then continues the walk from there.

**Retention: 30 days**, cleaned up by `purgeExpiredSnapshots()` riding along on `/api/cron/recurrence` (03:00 daily) — reusing the one purely-backend-maintenance cron among the three in `vercel.json` (emails/prospect-gifts are both product-facing lifecycle jobs) rather than adding a fourth scheduler.

**Trash out of scope (see the spec's explicit list):** search within trash, a permanent-delete button, restoring an entire deleted subtree in one action.

#### Version history (content snapshots, `reason: 'update'`)

Lets a human recover an earlier revision of a page's *content* — the page still exists, an edit overwrote something worth getting back. Reachable via a **"History" item in the existing "..." menu** on both `StandalonePageEditor.tsx` and `PageEditor.tsx` (same menu as "View as Markdown"), opening `PageHistoryModal.tsx`. Not a persistent panel like `PageCommentsPanel` — used occasionally, shouldn't cost every page permanent vertical space.

**The hard part is definitional, not technical.** The block editor autosaves continuously (1s debounce client-side in both editors), so naively snapshotting on every save produces hundreds of near-identical versions per page per day. `maybeSnapshotContentUpdate()` collapses this with a **session + size threshold**, measured against the page's LAST snapshot (not the previous save):

1. If the new content is byte-identical to what's about to be overwritten, skip entirely (an autosave tick with no real change) — regardless of actor.
2. **Human writes are debounced**: skip unless either 10 minutes have passed since the page's last snapshot, or the new content's length differs from the last snapshot's content length by more than 30%. A page with no snapshot yet always gets one (establishing a baseline).
3. **Agent (MCP) writes are never debounced** — every real content change gets its own version, because a tool call is a discrete deliberate action, not a keystroke stream. `updatePageById`'s new `actor?: SnapshotActor` param (separate from the pre-existing `agentCtx?: {tokenId}`, which only feeds the unrelated `agentEditedAt`/`agentTokenId` columns) drives this: `debounced: actor.kind === 'human'` is derived automatically, so the write-share path (`sharing.ts`, human, still autosave-debounced) and MCP `update_page`/`bulk_update_pages` (agent, never debounced) fall out of the same one-line rule.

This runs at write time in all 4 content-write paths — `updateStandalonePageContent`/`updatePageContent` (web autosave), `updatePageById` (MCP + the write-share editor) — each fetches the row's content BEFORE overwriting it and passes that as `priorContent` (the value actually stored in the snapshot; never the new content). The OKF import route's post-import link-rewrite call to `updatePageById` passes no `actor` at all — no resolvable identity and not a meaningful edit, so `updatePageById` skips versioning entirely rather than inventing a "system" actor.

**Restore is itself a recorded, undo-able version** (spec: "geri dönmek de bir sürüm üretir") — `restoreContentVersion()` snapshots the CURRENT live content first (non-debounced — a deliberate one-off action) before overwriting it with the target version's content. Human-only, same reasoning as trash restore: no MCP tool exists for this.

**Retention, two independent caps:**
- **20 versions per page**, enforced at write time (not the cron) — after inserting a snapshot, `maybeSnapshotContentUpdate` deletes any beyond the newest 20 for that page. Unlike a delete snapshot (naturally one-shot per item), a page can accumulate versions indefinitely over months, so this has to be active.
- **25 MB total per workspace**, across ALL snapshots regardless of `reason` — `enforceWorkspaceSnapshotByteCaps()`, riding the same daily `/api/cron/recurrence` job as `purgeExpiredSnapshots()`. Evicts oldest-first per over-cap workspace, bounded to 200 workspaces/run as a safety net (not expected to bite at current scale). The number is a judgment call (the spec explicitly leaves it undecided) — revisit if it turns out wrong.
- The existing 30-day age cap (`purgeExpiredSnapshots`, no `reason` filter) already covers version snapshots too — no change needed there.

**UI's character-delta indicator is NOT a diff view.** `PageHistoryModal.tsx` shows a compact `+N / −M` next to each version, computed by a real per-line LCS diff (`lineDelta()`, O(lines²), capped at 2,000 lines/side with a common-prefix/suffix-trim fallback above that) — a naive prefix/suffix trim was tried first and badly overcounted whenever a document had more than one separate edited region (any single difference near the end stops the suffix match, so nearly the whole middle reads as changed). Still not a comparison VIEW — no line-by-line rendering, just the summary label (out of scope, same as trash). No MCP tool for reading or restoring history — total tool count stays 24.

**History and Share are reachable from BOTH the full page editor and the peek modal.** `StandalonePageEditor.tsx`/`PageEditor.tsx` own their own "..." menu (full page only — hidden via `!isPeek` in `PageEditor.tsx`); the peek modal (`DatabaseView.tsx`'s Center Peek and Side Peek variants, both `isPeek={true}`) has a SEPARATE, independent "..." menu built directly in `DatabaseView.tsx` (duplicated per variant — Center/Side aren't a shared component). Share was missing from peek since before this feature (pre-existing gap, not something this feature introduced) and got added alongside History when a user caught both in the same pass. `ShareModal` in the peek context is given `isAdmin={false}` unconditionally — `DatabaseView.tsx` only has `currentUserId`, not the caller's role, so the admin-only custom-slug option isn't available from a peek-triggered share (full threading of role state wasn't worth it for that one convenience field; revisit if it matters in practice).

### Project Structure

**Auth & middleware**
- `src/auth.config.ts` — Edge-compatible config (middleware only, no DB import). `/client-login` is handled specially: logged-in users with a `device_id` param are redirected straight to `/api/auth/client-bridge?device_id=…`; without a `device_id` they go to `/app`.
- `src/auth.ts` — Full config: DrizzleAdapter, `client-token` credentials provider (desktop OAuth), JWT callbacks, first-user bootstrap event.
- `src/proxy.ts` — Protects all routes (Next.js 16 proxy, replaces `middleware.ts`); whitelists `/login`, `/client-login`, `/tauri-app`, `/api/auth/*`, `/api/auth/client-activate`, `/api/mcp`, `/api/oauth/*`, `/api/health`, `/.well-known/*`, static assets, `/`, `/pricing`, `/contact`, `/download`, `/privacy`, `/security`, `/brand`, `/wiki`, `/docs`, `/share/`, `/unsubscribe`, `/api/unsubscribe`, `/api/cron`, `/api/webhooks/ses`. Matcher explicitly excludes `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `llms.txt`, `manifest.json`, `sw.js`, and `workbox-*` so Next.js metadata routes + PWA files + the AI-crawler map (all fetched cookie-less) bypass the intl/auth middleware entirely; `auth.config.ts`'s `isPublicAsset` whitelists the same PWA + `llms.txt` paths as a second layer. **Ordering note:** `next.config.ts`'s `redirects()`/`rewrites()` run *before* this proxy per the App Router request lifecycle (headers → redirects → proxy/middleware → rewrites) — so anything rewritten to a different internal path (e.g. `/llms.txt` → `/api/llms`) must be whitelisted by its **original** public path, not its rewrite destination.
- `src/lib/auth/session.ts` — `getCurrentUser()` — `React.cache`-wrapped `auth()`. Use this everywhere in server actions.

**Root app files (`src/app/`)**
- `layout.tsx` — True root layout. Renders `<html lang={locale}>` + `<body>` with Onest (sans/UI) / JetBrains Mono (code) / Fraunces (serif/display, normal+italic) font CSS variables. Reads the locale resolved by next-intl. Defines `export const viewport` without disabling browser zoom. Loads the vendored public accessibility preference widget after hydration for marketing/public routes only (resolved locale, bottom-left, default `1.25rem`/`1.25rem` offset hugging the corner — `CookieConsentBanner.tsx` bumps `offsetY` to `5rem` via the widget's `window.AccessibilityPreferenceWidget.configure()` API while its own full-width bottom bar is showing, dropping back to the corner once a consent choice is stored); `data-exclude-paths="/app,/admin,/db,/page"` on the `<Script>` tag hides it inside the authenticated app shell, where its floating button obstructs in-app controls — the widget's own router-change detection (MutationObserver + popstate/hashchange) re-evaluates this on every client-side navigation, so it mounts/unmounts correctly without a full reload. Refresh its reviewed bundle and notices with `npm run sync:accessibility-widget` from the sibling `accessibility-preference-widget-public` checkout (or set `ACCESSIBILITY_WIDGET_SOURCE`). The matching `/vendor/accessibility-preference-widget/*` public path is explicitly excluded from `src/proxy.ts` so locale/auth rewrites do not intercept its script or notices. The trigger button's teal glass look is reskinned to match the app palette by `AccessibilityWidgetTheme.tsx` (below) — re-verify it after every bundle refresh, since it depends on the vendor bundle's internal `#a11y-widget-trigger` id/rules. **All other layouts must NOT render `<html>`/`<body>`.**
- `global-error.tsx` — Last-resort React error boundary (catches crashes in the root/locale **layouts** themselves, above `[locale]/error.tsx`). REPLACES the root layout when it fires, so it renders its own `<html>`/`<body>` and uses NO providers/i18n (plain dark-themed English fallback + Reload). Reports to PostHog via `reportClientError`.
- `[locale]/error.tsx` — Branded, i18n'd error boundary for everything under `[locale]` (marketing/share/auth/in-app — route groups don't add segments, so it also catches `(app)` crashes). Renders inside `[locale]/layout` so `NextIntlClientProvider`+PostHog are available → localized (`Errors.crash{Title,Body,Reload,Back}`) UI (Reload=`reset()`, Back=`router.back()`) and crash reporting. **Why this matters:** before these existed there were NO error boundaries, so any render error (e.g. a Rules-of-Hooks violation → React #310) nuked the whole app to the bare browser "couldn't load" screen with ZERO telemetry. `src/lib/reportClientError.ts` is the shared best-effort reporter (forwards error + `digest` + path to `posthog.captureException`, never re-throws) — the `window error`/`unhandledrejection` listeners in `DebugConsole` do NOT catch React render errors, only boundaries do.
- `sitemap.ts` — **Async.** Generates `/sitemap.xml`. Lists static public URLs (incl. every `/wiki/*` + `/docs/*` page, from the manifest) + all `shared_pages` where `in_sitemap = true` (admin-opted, priority 0.7; the legacy `docs/mcp`/`blog` shared-page rows are filtered out — see **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog**). Child pages of sitemap-included parents are automatically included via `updateShare` cascade — no manual per-child toggle needed.
- `robots.ts` — Generates `/robots.txt`. Allows public marketing paths (incl. `/wiki`, `/docs`) + `/share/`; disallows `/app`, `/db/`, `/page/`, `/admin/`, `/api/`, `/login`. Rules are duplicated under named AI-crawler user-agent groups (`GPTBot`, `ClaudeBot`, etc.) alongside `*` — see **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog**.

**Routes (`src/app/[locale]/`)**
- `layout.tsx` — **Providers only** (shared by ALL `[locale]` routes — marketing, public share, auth, and in-app). Locale validation + `NextIntlClientProvider`, `PostHogProvider`/`PostHogPageView`/(`PostHogIdentify` when logged in, else `AttributionCapture`), `ConsentProvider` + `CookieConsentBanner`, `PwaInstallCapture` (global `beforeinstallprompt` capture for the /download install CTA), `Analytics`. Does NOT render `<html>`/`<body>` (those live in root layout) and **does NOT render the app shell** — the sidebar/tabs live in `(app)/layout.tsx`. Exports `metadata` (global title template + OG/Twitter tags). **Why no shell here:** the app shell must mount/unmount when crossing between a public share page and the app; a conditional in this shared layout would instead be *preserved* by App Router across client navigation (e.g. "Go to app" from a share page wouldn't bring the sidebar back).
- `(app)/layout.tsx` — **Route group** holding the authenticated app shell for the in-app routes (`(app)/app`, `(app)/db`, `(app)/page`, `(app)/admin` — the route group does not affect URLs). Guards `auth()` (→ `/login`), fetches `getWorkspaces()`/`getAllWorkspaceItems()`, and renders `ActivityTracker`/`LastPathTracker`/`BillingSuccessModal`/`UpdateBanner`/(`DemoFeedbackPrompt` for demo, `PwaInstallNudge` for non-demo) + `QueryProvider` → `AppShell` (sidebar `WorkspaceSidebar` + `MobileNavWrapper` + demo banner). Public routes (share, marketing, auth) sit OUTSIDE this group, so they never get the shell — a logged-in non-member opening a `/share/*` link sees `SharedPageView` as a foreign visitor (members are still redirected to the real `/page|/db` route by the share route).
- `page.tsx` — Landing page; redirects authed users to `/app`, otherwise renders `LandingBridgeSwitcher`. Exports `metadata` with `title.absolute`, landing-specific description, `alternates.canonical: 'https://remnus.com'`, and hreflang for all 8 supported locales (all point to same URL — `localePrefix: 'never'` constraint).
- `(app)/app/page.tsx` — Authenticated redirect gateway. Resolution order: (1) pending-invite cookie → `/invite/[token]`; (2) **resume** — the `remnus_last_path` cookie (last visited `/page`/`/db`/`/graph` route, written by `LastPathTracker`; a `/graph/<id>` path resumes while the workspace still has an item of the user's) when it was written by **this same user** (`readOwnedLastPath`, see [src/lib/server/lastPath.ts](src/lib/server/lastPath.ts)) *and* its item still exists/is accessible (validated against `getAllWorkspaceItems`) — the ownership check is load-bearing on its own, since accessibility alone lets an admin (or a co-member) resume the previously signed-in user's page; (3) the **top of the active workspace's hierarchy** — first **root** item (`parentId === null`), NOT merely the oldest item in the flat list; (4) empty-state. `/login` when unauthenticated.
- `login/page.tsx` — Google + GitHub OAuth login. In Tauri mode, renders a minimal UI (logo + "Sign in" button); on click generates a UUID `device_id`, opens `client-login?device_id=<uuid>` in the system browser, then polls `/api/auth/client-poll` every 2 s until a token arrives.
- `client-login/page.tsx` — Public. Full login page (Google + GitHub) opened in the system browser by Tauri. Reads `device_id` from URL search params and threads it through both auth paths so `/api/auth/client-bridge` can store the resulting token keyed by that id.
- `tauri-app/page.tsx` — Public entry for Tauri (`tauri.conf.json` devUrl/url). Sets `localStorage.platform=tauri`, auto-detects OS locale, redirects to `/app`.
- `(app)/db/[id]/page.tsx` — Database view (Table / Kanban / Calendar).
- `(app)/db/[id]/[pageId]/page.tsx` — Database row page editor.
- `(app)/page/[itemId]/page.tsx` — Standalone page editor.
- `pricing/page.tsx` — Public pricing (MarketingShell-wrapped). Exports page-specific `metadata` with canonical URL.
- `contact/page.tsx` — Public contact (MarketingShell-wrapped). Exports page-specific `metadata` with canonical URL.
- `download/page.tsx` — Public desktop download page (MarketingShell-wrapped). Renders `DownloadView` with static `releases/latest/download/<stable-name>` links. Exports page-specific `metadata` with canonical URL.
- `privacy/page.tsx` — Public privacy page (MarketingShell-wrapped). Exports page-specific `metadata` with canonical URL.
- `brand/page.tsx` — Public brand-kit page (MarketingShell-wrapped, whitelisted in `auth.config.ts`, in sitemap). Shows the color palette (literal hex mirroring the `@theme` tokens in globals.css) + the three typefaces (Onest/Fraunces/JetBrains Mono). Linked from the footer's Company column (`Landing.bridgeFooterCompanyBrand`). i18n: `Brand` namespace. Exports page-specific `metadata`.
- `share/[...slug]/page.tsx` — **Public** (no auth). Resolves `slug` (joined `/`-separated path parts) via `getShareBySlug`. **Workspace members are redirected** to the real `/page/[id]` or `/db/[id]` route instead of seeing the shared view. Fetches `shareMap` (pageId→slug for all workspace shares) and builds `navTree` (shared pages tree for sidebar). Passes `parentSlug` (parent's share slug if parent is also shared). Renders `SharedPageView`. **NOTE:** the MCP reference docs + blog used to live here (`/share/docs/mcp/*`, `/share/blog/*`); they moved to the file-driven `/wiki` + `/docs` routes below and those old URLs now 301-redirect (see **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog**).
- `wiki/page.tsx` + `wiki/[...slug]/page.tsx` + `wiki/layout.tsx` — **Public** MCP reference **Wiki** (left-sidebar tree). `MarketingShell`-wrapped; layout renders `WikiShell` (sidebar `WikiSidebar` from `getWikiNav()` + content slot). Each page renders `WikiArticle` (breadcrumb + title + `DocsProse` HTML + right-rail `WikiToc` scrollspy). Per-page `generateMetadata` (canonical `/wiki/<slug>`) + `TechArticle` + `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD; unknown slug → `notFound()`. **Rendered dynamically (server-rendered per request), not build-time static** — see the `generateStaticParams` removal note below. See **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog**.
- `docs/page.tsx` + `docs/[slug]/page.tsx` + `docs/layout.tsx` — **Public** **Docs** blog (article layout). `docs/layout.tsx` wraps both in `MarketingShell` + the same subtle blue glow treatment as `WikiShell`. Index = breadcrumb + hero + `BlogCard` grid (newest-first) + `Blog` JSON-LD listing every post. Article = breadcrumb + header (icon/date/reading time) + `DocsProse` + `DocsArticleFooter` (prev/next + back) + `BlogPosting` + `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD. **Rendered dynamically, not build-time static** — see below. See **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog**.
- `src/lib/server/sharing-internals.ts` — **Server-only** (no `'use server'` — NOT a server action endpoint). `import 'server-only'`. Contains `getShareMapForWorkspace(workspaceId)` and `checkUserHasWorkspaceAccess(userId, workspaceId)`. Imported directly by server components only. NEVER import from a `'use server'` file (IDOR risk).
- `(app)/admin/page.tsx` — Admin dashboard, laid out as an "instrument cluster" hierarchy rather than a flat grid of equal-weight stat cards (the original 9-card `xl:grid-cols-9` row wrapped/crowded once labels didn't fit — redesigned 2026-07). A **hero KPI row** (4 tiles, `HeroTile`/`SignalRow` helpers in this file) gives the 3 highest-signal numbers their own big tabular-nums tile — total users (+ new this week/month as one sentence), weekly active users (with a capacity bar + % of total), avg. session (+ total time) — and bundles the still-low/near-zero metrics (active demo sessions, desktop users, active desktop 30d) into one compact multi-row tile instead of 3 separate cards. Below that, a **2-column, height-equalized** (`items-stretch` + `flex-1` cards) row pairs **User Acquisition** (30-day signup trend chart) stacked with the **Agent Activation Funnel** — now a compact 3-row list (`ActivationFunnelList` in this file: label · count · conversion-% badge · thin progress bar, NOT a chart) — against **Landing Traffic** (PostHog `$referring_domain` breakdown + a `?ref=`/`utm_source` campaign-tag breakdown) on the right; stacking the funnel under the trend chart on the left roughly balances that column's height against the traffic card. Directly beneath that sits the full-width **Demo Usage** section (`DemoUsageChart` in `AdminCharts.tsx`) — the acquisition trend's counterpart for visitors who never sign up: a 30-day bar chart of demo STARTS with avg/median/longest time-spent chips, a per-day average in each tooltip, and a time-spent distribution strip (`< 1m` / `1–5m` / `5–15m` / `15m+`, bucket labels untranslated like `formatDuration`'s output). Backed by `getDemoUsage()` over `demo_sessions` — **not** `user`/`user_sessions`, which hold only the demos still alive right now. A second equalized 2-column row pairs **Desktop / Tauri** (download-click breakdown by OS) with **Demo Feedback** (sentiment counts + recent comments). Then the users table. Fetches `getAllUsers()` + `getEngagementOverview()` + `getActivationFunnel()` + `getDemoFeedback()` + `getDemoUsage()`. Header carries a **Mailing** button → `/admin/mailing`. **Demo users (`role='demo'`) are excluded from `getAllUsers()` and from every engagement metric** — they only appear as the dedicated active-demo-sessions signal row + the demo-feedback section (the table's demo role-filter option was removed). The standalone workspaces table was removed — workspaces now live in `AdminUserDetailModal`. The signup-trend chart (`SignupTrendChart`) lives in `src/components/features/admin/AdminCharts.tsx` (**client component** — needed for real hover tooltips; the page is a server component and passes plain data props); the traffic daily/weekly/monthly trend chart (`TrafficTrendChart`) has its own file, `src/components/features/admin/TrafficTrendChart.tsx` (see the `getTrafficTrend()` entry below for its line-first/bar/source-selector design); the activation funnel is plain server-rendered markup in `page.tsx` (no hover state needed, so it isn't in a client chart file). The **landing-traffic card** is `src/components/features/admin/AdminTrafficSources.tsx` — a **self-fetching client component** (`getTrafficSources()` + `getTrafficTrend()` in parallel on mount) so a slow/failed PostHog Query API call never blocks the admin page's server render; falls back to a localized "unavailable" state when PostHog read creds are absent. **Tabbed** (`Tab = 'sources'|'daily'|'weekly'|'monthly'`, local `useState`, no URL/persisted state): the "Sources" tab keeps the original channel/domain breakdown plus a **campaign-tags section** (`data.campaigns`, hidden when empty) so `?ref=`/`?utm_source=` partner-link traffic (e.g. `remnus.com?ref=scoutforge`) is visible without digging through raw PostHog events; the "Daily" tab renders the last 30 zero-filled days, while "Weekly"/"Monthly" render 12 buckets. Trend views default to the smoothed line chart; the bar toggle remains available. `AdminTrafficSources` owns (and lifts, so it survives tab switches) the chart's `viewMode`/`source` selection state, passed down as controlled props. The **Desktop / Tauri card** is `src/components/features/admin/AdminDesktopStats.tsx` — same self-fetching pattern, backed by `getDesktopDownloadStats()`; `AdminUsersTable` also shows a small `Laptop` badge per row when that user has ever used the desktop app (`activity[id].usesDesktop`). **Personal canvas width:** the whole layout (header + content) is wrapped by `src/components/features/admin/AdminShell.tsx` (client component) instead of being inlined in `page.tsx` — same per-user, local-only width toggle pattern as `StandalonePageEditor`/`PageEditor` (Narrow/Wide/Full via an `ArrowLeftRight` button + dropdown in the header), persisted to `localStorage['remnus_admin_width']` (no DB/cookie — purely a client preference, defaults to `wide` to match the previous fixed `max-w-7xl` look). `page.tsx` passes `icon`/`title`/`subtitle`/`headerActions` (the Mailing link) as props and the rest of the dashboard markup as `children`. i18n: `Admin.width{Narrow,Wide,Full}` (all 8 locales).
- `(app)/admin/workspaces/page.tsx` — Legacy stub; redirects to `/admin`.
- **Admin traffic range selectors (2026-07):** `AdminTrafficSources` keeps an independent range per trend granularity: Daily = 30/90/365 days, Weekly = 12/26/52 weeks, Monthly = 12/24/36 months. `getTrafficTrend()` fetches and zero-fills the maximum window once (`daily: 365`, `weekly: 52`, `monthly: 36`); the client slices that data for instant range switching without additional PostHog requests. `TrafficTrendChart` localizes the compact range labels and total-period copy, and derives the X-axis label interval from measured plot width so long daily series remain readable.
- `(app)/admin/mailing/page.tsx` — Admin mailing dashboard (linked from the `/admin` header Mail button): delivery stat cards, newsletter composer + campaign history, recent-send log. Renders `MailingDashboard` with `getMailingOverview()`. See **Mailing System**.
- `[locale]/unsubscribe/page.tsx` — **Public** confirm-button unsubscribe page (`?u=<userId>&t=<hmac>`; mutation NOT on GET — mail scanners prefetch links). i18n: `Mailing.unsub*`. See **Mailing System**.
- `[locale]/account-delete/confirm/page.tsx` — **Auth-required** (default-protected, not whitelisted) confirm-button page for the GDPR account-deletion email link (`?token=…`; mutation NOT on GET). i18n: `UserSettings.deleteAccount*`. See **Account Deletion**.
- `api/unsubscribe/route.ts` — **Public.** RFC 8058 one-click unsubscribe: POST verifies the HMAC token and sets `email_unsubscribed_at`; GET redirects to `/unsubscribe`.
- `api/cron/emails/route.ts` — `GET`, guarded by `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}` (attached automatically by Vercel Cron; schedule in `vercel.json`, daily 09:00 UTC). Runs the `agent_nudge` + `inactivity` jobs. See **Mailing System**.
- `api/webhooks/ses/route.ts` — `POST`. **Public** (SNS-signature-verified). SES bounce/complaint events → `user.email_suppressed`. Auto-confirms the SNS subscription. See **Mailing System**.
- `api/admin/mailing/send-campaign/route.ts` — `POST { campaignId }`. Admin-gated newsletter send-to-all loop (`maxDuration = 300`; draft-only = double-send protection). See **Mailing System**.
- `api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.ts` — Auth.js handler.
- `api/auth/client-bridge/route.ts` — GET. Called after browser-side login (as callbackUrl). Requires `device_id` query param. Creates a 5-min JWT signed with AUTH_SECRET, stores it in the in-memory `client-auth-store` keyed by `device_id`, and returns a "Close this tab" HTML page.
- `api/auth/client-poll/route.ts` — GET. Polled by the Tauri WebView every 2 s. Accepts `device_id`; returns `{ ready: false }` while waiting, `{ ready: true, token }` once the browser completes login (one-time consume).
- `api/auth/client-activate/route.ts` — GET. Tauri WebView navigates here with the token from the poll response. Signs in via `client-token` provider, redirects to `/app`.
- `api/activity/ping/route.ts` — `POST`. Auth-gated heartbeat for engagement tracking. Extends the user's most recent `user_sessions` row (or opens a new one after a 2-min gap) and recomputes `duration_seconds`. **Admins are skipped** (no rows created) so their reviewing doesn't pollute the stats. Best-effort; returns `{ ok: true, changeVersion }`. Called by `ActivityTracker`. **Demo callers additionally extend their `demo_sessions` row** (`touchDemoSession`, migration `0044`): `active_seconds` accumulates each tick's delta, and only when that delta is inside `SESSION_GAP_MS` — so it measures real presence, not wall-clock with the tab buried. Opens a row if none exists (demo account predating 0044, or a failed insert at login) rather than losing the visit. **`changeVersion`** = max `updatedAt` across the caller's `workspace_items` + `standalone_pages` + `databases` + `pages` (`databases` included since 2026-07-10 so a view/schema edit also triggers a refresh). **TEXT-timestamp gotcha (fixed 2026-07-10 — do not regress):** rows predating the explicit-timestamp fix (see the createdAt gotcha under **Auth System**) store `updated_at` as TEXT, and SQLite ranks TEXT above INTEGER — so a bare `max()` returned that TEXT value for the whole table and pinned it there permanently → `Number()` → `NaN` → JSON-serialized to `null` → the client guard dropped every tick, so **live refresh silently never fired** in any workspace holding even one legacy row (this was the "edits don't show up until I hit refresh" bug; it looked intermittent because it's per-table `max()`). Each aggregate is now `max(case when typeof(col) = 'integer' then col else 0 end)`, and the client guards in `ActivityTracker` / `useWorkspaceEvents` / `TabHost` test `Number.isFinite(v)` instead of `typeof v === 'number'` (NaN *is* a number and compares false against every later value, wedging the refresh loop shut instead of failing loudly).
- `api/health/route.ts` — `GET`. **Public** (no auth — whitelisted in `auth.config.ts` via `isHealthCheck`). Liveness/health probe for MCP registry submissions + uptime monitoring. Checks DB connectivity (`SELECT 1` with latency) and reports MCP transport readiness (endpoint, `streamable-http`, negotiated `LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION`). Returns `{ status: 'ok'|'degraded', timestamp, uptimeSeconds, checks: { database, mcp } }` — **200** when healthy, **503** when the DB is unreachable. `Cache-Control: no-store`, `runtime: 'nodejs'`, `dynamic: 'force-dynamic'`.
- `api/llms/route.ts` — `GET`, `force-static`. **Public**, exposed at the clean `/llms.txt` path via a `next.config.ts` rewrite (see **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog**). Returns `text/markdown` — a curated map of `/wiki` + `/docs` for LLMs/AI agents (llmstxt.org convention), generated from `WIKI_PAGES`/`BLOG_POSTS` so it can't drift from the real pages.
- `api/wiki-md/[[...slug]]/route.ts` — `GET`. **Public**, exposed at `/wiki/<slug>.md` (and `/wiki.md`) via a `next.config.ts` rewrite. Returns the wiki page's markdown file as `text/markdown` (`getWikiMarkdown`), hidden pages included, with relative `.md` links made root-relative (`/wiki/playbooks/game.md`) and a `Link: rel="canonical"` header to the HTML page. Unknown slug → 404. Same two-layer allowlist as `/llms.txt`. See **Project Install** §7.
- `api/upload/route.ts` — `POST`. Cloudinary upload. Auth-gated (session required). `multipart/form-data` with `file` + optional `kind` (`icon` default — 256×256 crop, `remnus/icons/`, 5 MB; `image` — content image, no crop, width≤1600px, `remnus/images/`, 10 MB; `file` — any attachment, `resource_type: auto`, `remnus/files/`, 25 MB) + optional `workspaceId`. Image kinds validate magic bytes; file kind skips that. **Every successful upload is recorded in `uploaded_assets`** (via `recordAsset`) for cleanup + storage accounting. Returns `{ url }` (image kinds) or `{ url, name, size }` (file kind).
- `api/upload/delete/route.ts` — `POST { url }`. Auth-gated. Removes a previously-uploaded asset from Cloudinary **and** the `uploaded_assets` ledger (via `deleteAssetByUrl`; authorized when the caller uploaded it or is a member of its workspace). Best-effort `{ ok }`. Called by editor image/file blocks when their delete button is pressed.
- `api/og/route.ts` — `GET ?url=` (Node runtime). Auth-gated. Fetches a page's Open Graph / Twitter / `<title>` metadata for the BookmarkBlock card. **SSRF-hardened:** http(s) only, credentials stripped, host resolved via `dns.lookup({all:true})` and **every** address classified — rejects loopback/private/link-local/CGNAT/ULA/IPv4-mapped/multicast (IPv4 + IPv6); redirects followed manually (max 3 hops) with per-hop revalidation. 6 s timeout, reads only up to `</head>`. (Residual: a small DNS-rebinding TOCTOU window remains — full closure needs IP pinning via a custom undici dispatcher.) Returns `{ url, title, description, image, favicon }` (favicon via Google s2). Best-effort — falls back to `{ url, title: hostname }` on any failure.
- `api/import/notion/route.ts` — `POST`. Auth-gated. **The ZIP is parsed entirely in the browser** (`ImportTab` via `parseNotionExport`); this route only receives the final, fully-materialized JSON tree for **one space per request**: `{ space: { name, items } }` where `items` is the serializable `ImportItem[]` (content already contains real image URLs). Creates a new workspace and bulk-inserts pages/databases/rows; normalizes date-column values via `normalizeNotionDate`. Notion's CSV export carries no view metadata, so `inferViews()` auto-adds views per database from the column types — always a Table, plus a Kanban grouped by the first `select` column and a Calendar on the first `date`/`datetime` column when present (databases with neither keep the default Table). Returns `{ ok: true, name, workspaceId, imported: { pages, databases, rows } }`. **Why client-side parse:** Vercel hard-caps serverless function request bodies at ~4.5 MB and Cloudinary caps single files at ~10 MB (free) — uploading the whole ZIP hit both (worked locally, 413'd on deploy). Parsing in the browser + per-space requests means the ZIP never leaves the client, so import size is effectively unbounded. There is **no** sign route and **no** server-side ZIP download anymore. Analysis/preview is also fully client-side (no network). Images (opt-in `importImages`) are read from the ZIP in the browser and uploaded one-by-one via `POST /api/upload` (`kind=image`), then their Cloudinary URLs are substituted into content before the JSON is sent.
- `[locale]/oauth/authorize/page.tsx` — **Public** (auth middleware redirects to login if unauthenticated, then back). Validates `client_id`, `redirect_uri`, `code_challenge` (S256), `scope`. Shows consent form (`OAuthAuthorizeForm` client component) with an **icon-based workspace picker** (workspace `icon`/`icon_color` via `PageIcon`), a **read/write scope selector** (defaults to the client-requested scope; the user can upgrade to write — most editors request `read` by default, so without this they'd be read-only), **an agent-type (brand icon) picker** (defaults to the mark inferred from `client_name` via `resolveAgentMark`, validated server-side against `AGENT_MARKS` ids), and a **connection-name input** (defaults to `client_name`). Server action reads `scope` + `agent_name` + `display_name` from the form and creates the `oauth_auth_codes` row, then redirects to `redirect_uri?code=…&state=…`. Deny redirects with `error=access_denied`.
- `[locale]/security/page.tsx` — **Public** marketing page (MarketingShell-wrapped). Documents auth methods (Google/GitHub OAuth), MCP token types (PAT + OAuth 2.1 + PKCE), token scopes/expiry/revocation, and responsible disclosure. Exports page-specific `metadata`.
- `api/oauth/register/route.ts` — `POST`. **Public.** RFC 7591 dynamic client registration. Validates `redirect_uris` (https or localhost only). Returns `client_id` + metadata.
- `api/oauth/token/route.ts` — `POST`. **Public.** Handles `authorization_code` (PKCE S256 verify, single-use code) and `refresh_token` (rotates: old row revoked, new pair issued) grant types. Accepts both `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` and JSON bodies.
- **MCP tool surface — the per-session token rule.** `tools/list` enters the model's context on **every** session, but only a tool's `description` + `inputSchema` reach the model: the tools API has no output-schema field, and `annotations` are client-side hints. So descriptions and input schemas carry only sentences that change an agent's behaviour (`update_page`'s merge semantics, the `confirm: true` rule, `bulk_move_items`' all-or-nothing refusal); anything restating a field's own name is waste. `outputSchema` and `annotations` are wire-only and may stay descriptive. A tool result is deliberately sent **twice** — `content[0].text` and `structuredContent` — because Claude Code forwards only the structured half to the model while Claude Desktop and Cursor forward only the text; dropping either blinds one family of clients, and each client forwards one copy, so the model-visible size is one copy (which is what `agent_activity.response_bytes` records). `handler.ts` registers `registerWriteTools` **only for `ctx.scope === 'write'`** — a read-only token used to be handed 14 write schemas it could never call. Measure with `npm run bench:mcp-budget` ([src/scripts/mcp-token-budget.ts](src/scripts/mcp-token-budget.ts)).
- `api/mcp/route.ts` — MCP transport shell: accepts both `rmns_` PAT tokens (from `agent_tokens`) and `oa_` OAuth tokens (from `oauth_access_tokens`). 401 response includes `WWW-Authenticate` header with `resource_metadata` pointer for OAuth discovery. Rate limit (60 req/min), stateless Streamable HTTP only (the legacy stateful SSE transport was removed 2026-08-03 — see `mem:conventions` for why; do not re-add).
- `api/mcp/context.ts` — `TokenContext` type + `logActivity()` helper (audit log insert, best-effort) + `appUrl(ctx, path)` (absolute in-app link from `ctx.appOrigin`, which `runMcpRequest` sets from the request URL). Imported by all tool/resource/prompt files.
- `api/mcp/resources.ts` — Registers 7 resources: `remnus://dashboard/catalog` (static; block types + fields + templates from `renderDashboardCatalog()`, logged as `resource:dashboard-catalog`), `remnus://workspace/{id}/knowledge-health` (heuristic link/freshness/lifecycle/human-review coverage; not a correctness certificate), `remnus://workspace/{id}/schema`, `remnus://workspace/{id}/digest` (compact one-line-per-item workspace map — titles/ids/row counts/**body sizes**/last-updated, headed by a `cursor:` line taken *before* the item queries so no write can fall between map and delta; the token-cheap orientation read, and the source the CLI caches as `.remnus/workspace-map.md`), `remnus://page/{id}` (lists recent 20 on `resources/list`), `remnus://database/{id}/schema`, `remnus://audit-log/recent` (filters by `tokenKind` — PAT vs OAuth FK columns).
- `api/mcp/prompts.ts` — Registers 7 prompt templates: `summarize-page` (page_id, style?), `weekly-status-report` (database_id, period?), `kanban-triage` (database_id), `extract-tasks` (page_id), `search-and-create` (title, query), **`save-memory` (content, memory_type?, tags?, database_id?)** and **`recall-context` (topic, limit?)** — the last two power the **Agent Memory** feature (the built-in `Agent Memory` template — see the `templates.ts` entry — plus the `docs/mcp/agent-memory.md` wiki page): `save-memory` resolves the target memory database via `listWorkspaceItems` (or returns a `create_database` instruction when none exists) and hands the agent a filled `create_page` instruction with structured Type/Tags/Date; `recall-context` bundles `searchWorkspace` + each hit's `buildContentOutline` (Faz 1) + the top hit's `getRelatedPages` neighborhood (Faz 3) into one compact message. Fetches DB content and returns filled prompt string; LLM call is done by the client.
- `api/mcp/tools/read.ts` — 11 read tools: `prepare_context` (task-specific, token-budgeted, OKF trust/freshness-aware context pack; optional `keywords` for agent-side query expansion — see **Performance Rules → Context & search read path**), `search_workspace` (ranked full-text, case- and accent-insensitive), `list_workspace` (cursor-based pagination), `get_page` (auto-detects type; `mode: 'full'|'outline'` — outline collapses the body to headings + first line per section and reports `fullContentChars`; `includeComments` (default false) attaches the page's `page_comments` thread — `{id, body, kind, authorKind, authorLabel, createdAt}` per entry, omitting `authorUserId`), `get_pages` (batch counterpart to `get_page` — `pageIds: string[]` (max 50) + same `mode`; for a specific, already-known, possibly cross-database/mixed-type id list, e.g. from `search_workspace`/`get_related_pages`/`get_changes_since`; uses `Promise.allSettled` — unlike `bulk_update_pages`'s `Promise.all` — so one bad id doesn't drop the rest, each result carries its own `ok`/`error`; see `getPagesByIds` in `services/workspace.ts`; added 2026-08-05), `get_database_schema` (schema **and views** — `{name, schema, views}`; `views` always has ≥1 entry, seeded with an implicit default Table view when `databases.views` is null, matching what the client would show), `query_database` (supports `filters: Record<string,any>`, `fields: string[]` projection — matches column ids OR names case-insensitively, trims properties+schema — and cursor-based pagination; **row markdown bodies are omitted by default**, included only when `'content'` is in `fields` — opt-in flip 2026-07-08 so unprojected queries stay cheap; **scalar-column filters (`select`/`status`/`text`/`number`) were broken until 2026-08-05** — the `json_each` branch fed a dequoted scalar into a JSON-array parser (`SQLITE_ERROR: malformed JSON`); fixed by sourcing that branch from the `->` operator instead of `json_extract`, which preserves JSON encoding; `multi_select` filters were never affected; rows carry `commentCount: n` when the row has ≥1 `page_comments` entry, absent otherwise — one grouped query per page of results, same pattern as the `recurring: true` marker), `list_members` (workspace_members JOIN user; role/email/joinedAt), `query_audit_log` (agentActivity; tool/status/date filters), `get_changes_since` (`since: ISO timestamp` and/or `cursor`, keyset-paginated — compact delta feed of items/rows `created`/`updated` plus `deleted` tombstones since a point in time; **`nextCursor` is always returned**, including when nothing changed, so a one-page bootstrap has something to keep; the cursor settles per `settledCursor` — see **The local workspace map** — so a second that is still open is re-reported once rather than missed; see `getChangesSince` in `services/workspace.ts`), `get_related_pages` (`pageId` — one-call knowledge-graph neighborhood: parent, children, outgoing links + backlinks from the `page_links` graph, same-database sibling rows, plus `sources` (the subject's knowledge sources as written, absent when none — one more statement in the same batch); titles + ids only, no bodies; see `getRelatedPages` in `services/workspace.ts`. **Or `resource`** (P13) — a repo file/folder path, relative or absolute: the items whose knowledge `sources` cite it, `match` = `exact`/`folder`/`inside`, ≤ 25 + `total`; `getPagesForResource` in `services/codeSources.ts`, see **Knowledge Map → Code layer and the agent side (P13)**). **Every tool declares an `outputSchema` and returns `structuredContent` alongside the `content` text block** (both required — see **MCP tool surface** above) plus rich `annotations` (`title` + read-only/destructive/idempotent/openWorld hints). The `content` text block is **compact JSON** (`JSON.stringify(x)`, no pretty-print). `list_workspace` omits `parentId`/`icon` when null (~15% of that payload; both are optional in the schema and absence reads the same) — the only response-shaping of this kind, since the other read payloads measured at 0–2% null share.
- `api/mcp/tools/write.ts` — 16 write tools: `create_dashboard` / `update_dashboard` (see **Dashboards → MCP**), `create_page`, `bulk_create_pages` (`{pages: [...] (1–100), contextRunId?}` — entries created **in order**, never atomic, per-entry `{index, ok, id?, type?, ref?, error?}` under `{requested, succeeded, failed, results}`; `ref`/`parentRef` nests a page under one created earlier in the same call, and `parentRef` can't be combined with `parentId`/`databaseId`; served by `createPagesInWorkspaceBulk`, **not** a loop over `createPageInWorkspace` — see **Bulk write path** under Performance Rules), `update_page` (merges properties, never overwrites; a real `content` change also records a version — see **Trash & Version History** — never debounced, unlike the web editors' autosave), `bulk_update_pages` (same version recording per entry; still one `updatePageById` per entry, concurrently — only its agent-provenance stamp is batched), `delete_page` (requires `confirm: true`; `destructiveHint`), `bulk_delete_pages` (`{pageIds: string[] (max 100), confirm?, contextRunId?}` — same preview/confirm pattern as `delete_page`, batched; response is `{deleted, requested, succeeded, failed, results: [{id, ok, error?}]}` on `confirm: true` — **never all-or-nothing**, `Promise.allSettled` under `bulkDeleteItemsFromWorkspace` means one bad id can't sink the rest, unlike `bulk_update_pages`; every deleted item gets a `page_snapshots` row first, see **Trash & Version History**), `move_item` (`newParentId: null` → root), `bulk_move_items` (`{itemIds: string[] (max 100), newParentId?, targetDatabaseId?, contextRunId?}` — exactly one of `newParentId` (batched sidebar reparent, same semantics as `move_item`, but resolved in three statements for the whole call: one existence check, one recursive-CTE ancestor read of the shared destination, one `UPDATE … IN`) or `targetDatabaseId` (cross-database row move) must be given; the cross-database path refuses the WHOLE call up front, naming the missing/mismatched columns, unless the target database's schema covers every source column by name AND type — never silently drops a property; same `{requested, succeeded, failed, results}` contract as `bulk_delete_pages`), `create_database` (title column auto-prepended; optional `icon`/`iconColor` and up to 5 `views` created right after it and reported per view — a view that fails never fails the call), `update_database_schema` (removing requires `confirm: true`; title column protected; `destructiveHint`), `create_database_view` (`{databaseId, name, type: 'table'|'kanban'|'calendar', groupByCol?, dateCol?, icon?, iconColor?}` — kanban auto-picks the first status/select column and calendar the first date/datetime column when the ref is omitted; `groupByCol`/`dateCol` accept a column id OR name; errors if the required column type doesn't exist and wasn't specified), `update_database_view` (`{databaseId, viewId, name?, icon?, iconColor?, config?}` — `config` is a partial patch merged into the existing config object; the view's `type` can't be changed after creation), `delete_database_view` (requires `confirm: true`; refuses to delete a database's last remaining view), `add_comment` (`{pageId, body (max 4,000 chars, rejected not truncated), kind?: 'note'|'closure', contextRunId?}` → `{id, createdAt}`; writes into `page_comments` via `addPageComment` in `services/comments.ts`, `authorKind: 'agent'`, `authorLabel: ctx.agentName ?? 'Agent'`; **append-only by design — there is no `update_comment`/`delete_comment` tool**, only a human can remove a comment, via the UI). **`search`→`search_workspace` and `bulk_update`→`bulk_update_pages` were renamed pre-launch for naming consistency (verb_object); audit-log `tool` values use the new names.** **Icons:** `create_page`/`bulk_create_pages`/`update_page`/`bulk_update_pages`/`create_database`/`create_dashboard`/`update_dashboard` take `icon` + `iconColor`, validated by `iconInputError` in [src/lib/icons.ts](src/lib/icons.ts): an emoji or `lucide:Name` from `CURATED_ICON_NAMES` — the exact set `PageIcon` renders (`CURATED_ICONS` there `satisfies Record<CuratedIconName, …>`, so adding an icon to one side only is a type error) — with colors from `SELECT_COLOR_ORDER`; image URLs are refused over MCP. View service fns (`createDatabaseView`/`updateDatabaseView`/`deleteDatabaseView`, plus the `seedDefaultViews`/`resolveColumnRef` helpers) live in `services/workspace.ts` alongside `updateDatabaseSchemaById`.

**Context-first MCP policy (0039):** `prepare_context` now returns Context Pack v2 (full-corpus BM25 + native metadata + link graph, default 2k budget, exact-revision trust, `selectionReason`) and a 30-minute actor-bound `contextRunId`. Manual/Smart leave it advisory; Strict rejects actual Remnus MCP mutations without the same PAT/OAuth actor's current run. Delete previews remain readable. All 16 write tools accept `contextRunId`; page/database creates and page updates accept optional `knowledge`, and all MCP content writes record machine provenance without granting human review. Scope checks, authorization, and destructive confirmations remain independent.

**Server Actions (`src/lib/actions/`)**
- `knowledge.ts` — authenticated wrappers for page/database/row metadata, exact-revision human review, and workspace context policy. Viewers may read but cannot mutate; only owners/admins change policy. `KnowledgeContextPanel` is mounted below both editors; Portability owns Manual/Smart/Strict settings.
- `comments.ts` — `getComments`/`addComment`/`deleteComment`, thin auth wrappers (own local `assertWorkspaceAccess`, same per-file pattern as `workspace.ts`) around `services/comments.ts`. `getComments` also returns `viewerUserId`/`isOwner` so `PageCommentsPanel` (mounted below both editors, above `KnowledgeContextPanel`) knows client-side whose delete button to show without a second round trip; `deleteComment` re-checks the same rule server-side regardless (own comment, or workspace owner — same permission model the MCP `add_comment` tool's write-side is exempt from, since agents never get a delete tool at all).
- `trash.ts` — `getMyTrash()` (cross-workspace, grouped by workspace — mirrors `agentToken.ts`'s `getUserWorkspacesWithTokens`), `getUserTrashCount()` (sidebar badge), `restoreTrashItem(workspaceId, snapshotId)` (per-workspace `assertWorkspaceAccess`, own local copy per-file convention). Powers `TrashModal.tsx`, reachable from a sidebar button next to AI Agents. **Human-only by design — no MCP counterpart.** See **Trash & Version History**.
- `history.ts` — `getPageHistory(workspaceId, pageId)` (own local `assertWorkspaceAccess`), `restoreVersion(workspaceId, pageId, snapshotId)`. Powers `PageHistoryModal.tsx`, reachable from the "..." menu on both page editors. **Human-only by design — no MCP counterpart.** See **Trash & Version History**.
- `workspace.ts` — Workspace + sidebar item CRUD (all auth-gated via `assertWorkspaceAccess`). Includes `updateWorkspaceIcon(id, icon, iconColor)`, `setWorkspaceHidden(id, hidden)` (sidebar hide/show), `reparentWorkspaceItem(itemId, newParentId, targetWorkspaceId, siblingIdsOrder)` (sidebar drag-to-nest — sets `parent_id`, re-sequences the new sibling group, cycle-guarded; `newParentId: null` → root), and `getPageRelations(workspaceId, pageId)` (thin auth-gated wrapper around the cookie-free `getRelatedPages` service — powers the in-app `PageBacklinksPanel`; re-exports the `RelatedPageRef` type).
- `database.ts` — Database schema + view mutations (`assertDatabaseAccess`). `updateDatabaseSchema` automatically propagates renamed select/multi-select options to all workspace pages to prevent data loss. It also performs **soft type conversions** within the `{select, multi_select, user, multi_user}` family (these four share a "list of tokens" shape, so cell values migrate across them): label↔label / user↔user only change arity; **label → user** matches each option label against workspace member names/emails (case-insensitive, email-local-part fallback) and stores the matched member ids (unmatched dropped); **user → label** resolves member ids back to display names and **auto-adds them as colored options** on the target select column (the type dropdown clears options on switch).
- `page.ts` — Database row CRUD (`assertDatabaseAccess`).
- `auth.ts` — User auth, registration, role management, workspace membership, admin user ops. `inviteToWorkspace` enforces the seat limit; `transferWorkspaceOwnership` moves `billing_owner_id`. `updateMyProfile({ name?, image? })` lets the signed-in user edit their own display name + avatar (avatar uploaded via `/api/upload` `kind=icon`; `image: null` clears it → initials fallback; best-effort Cloudinary cleanup of the replaced avatar). It calls `update()` (`unstable_update`, re-exported from `src/auth.ts`) so the JWT session reflects the new name/avatar **without a re-login** — handled by the `trigger === 'update'` branch in the `jwt` callback (merges `name`/`image` into `token`). Surfaced in `UserSettingsModal` → Account tab (`ProfileSection`).
- `billing.ts` — Stripe billing actions: `createCheckoutSession(tier)`, `createPortalSession()`, `getMySubscription()` (plan + usage meters), `getWorkspaceSeatUsage(workspaceId)`, and **admin-only** `adminSetUserPlan(userId, tier)` — manually activates/changes a user's plan (upserts the `subscriptions` row, no Stripe call); **refuses when `stripeSubscriptionId` is set** so it never fights the webhook self-healing sync. Surfaced in `AdminUserDetailModal`. See **Billing & Plan Limits**.
- `demo.ts` — `loginAsDemo()` — provisions a fresh ephemeral demo account + seeded workspace and signs in. Also opens the visitor's `demo_sessions` row (best-effort, try/catch — analytics must never cost someone their demo); the heartbeat accumulates time onto it.
- `locale.ts` — `setLocale(locale)` — writes `NEXT_LOCALE` cookie.
- `consent.ts` — `setAnalyticsConsent(granted)` — persists the logged-in user's effective capture permission to `user.analytics_consent` ('granted'\|'denied'). Called fire-and-forget by `ConsentProvider` whenever the effective permission changes. See **Activation Funnel Analytics**.
- `agentToken.ts` — MCP token mint / list / revoke. `mintAgentToken` fires the `mcp_token_created` funnel event after a successful PAT insert. `getUserAgentActivity` LEFT JOINs both `agent_tokens` and `oauth_access_tokens` (coalesced names) so OAuth calls appear in the AgentsModal activity feed. `getMyAgentUsage()` — the signed-in user's last-30-day MCP usage `{ calls, bytes }` by `owner_user_id` (bytes → ~tokens at /4; powers the AgentsModal usage row).
- `onboarding.ts` — `getOnboardingProgress()` — new-user onboarding state derived **entirely from real DB rows** (no stored flag): `hasToken` (a real PAT/OAuth token exists), `hasAgentCall` (a real `agent_activity` row exists), `mintTargets`. **Excludes the planted seed token** (`tokenPrefix === 'rmns-demo'`, from `createRichWorkspaceData`) so a freshly-seeded workspace doesn't show every step pre-done; returns empty for `role==='demo'`. Powers `OnboardingGuide`. Since migration `0034` OAuth tool calls land in `agent_activity` too (nullable `token_id` + `oauth_token_id`), so `hasAgentCall` covers both token paths (the old "OAuth first-calls not server-detectable" gap is closed).
- `analytics.ts` — Admin-gated engagement analytics. `getEngagementOverview()` (total/avg session time, DAU/WAU/MAU, new-this-week/month signups, 30-day signup trend, per-user activity map) — **every metric here excludes `role='demo'`** (session aggregates join `users` and filter; signup/storage queries filter) so ephemeral demo visitors don't pollute the admin scores; demo is surfaced separately via `demoActiveSessions` (distinct demo users with a heartbeat in the last 15 min) + `demoTotal` (current demo accounts, ≈ last 6h). Also returns `desktopUsersTotal`/`desktopUsersActive30d` (distinct non-demo users with a `user_sessions.platform = 'tauri'` session, ever / in the last 30 days — migration `0035`), and each `PerUserActivity` carries `usesDesktop: boolean` (same signal, per user — surfaced as a small badge in `AdminUsersTable`). `getUserDetail(userId)` (account + authType + `ownedWorkspaces` count, activity summary — including `activity.usesDesktop` — **storage usage** (`storageBytes` + per-workspace `storageBytes`), **content** (`pages`/`databases`/`records` counts across the user's workspaces), **agents** (`UserAgents`: active PAT+OAuth count, total MCP tool calls + last-call time + `responseBytes` (total response payload bytes → ~tokens served, migration `0034`) across the user's workspaces, and a PAT-token list `AgentTokenSummary[]` with scope/status('active'|'revoked'|'expired')/last-used; OAuth connections collapsed to an `oauthActive` count since they rotate hourly), **subscription** (`tier`/`status`/`source: 'stripe'|'manual'|'none'`/`currentPeriodEnd` — `source` derived: Stripe-managed if `stripeSubscriptionId` set, else `manual` for a non-free tier, else `none`), workspaces with items) for the admin panel. `getActivationFunnel()` reconstructs the signup → connected-an-agent (real PAT `createdBy`/OAuth) → made-first-call (agent_activity; PAT via token join + OAuth via `owner_user_id`, migration `0034`) funnel entirely from DB state (PostHog-independent; the granular OAuth/consent sub-steps stay in PostHog). `getTrafficSources(days=30)` — **admin-gated PostHog read** (via `src/lib/analytics/posthog-query.ts`): where landing (`/`) visitors came from over the last N days, from PostHog `$pageview` + `$referring_domain` (top-of-funnel, ALL visitors, not just signups). Returns `{ channels, domains, campaigns, days, available }` — `channels` is a coarse `TrafficChannel` ('direct'|'organic'|'social'|'referral') rollup (`classifyChannel` buckets each referring domain), `domains` is the per-referring-domain detail (`'$direct'` = no referrer); internal (own-domain) referrers are filtered out. **`campaigns`** is a per-tag breakdown of `?ref=<tag>` (our own simple partner/campaign link convention — e.g. `remnus.com?ref=scoutforge`), falling back to `?utm_source=<tag>` when no `ref` is present — read straight off `$current_url` via HogQL's `extractURLParameter` in a second parallel query, since neither tag was previously broken out as its own queryable property (they only ever reached PostHog as `$set_once` person props at **signup**, via `AttributionCapture`'s `remnus_first_touch` cookie + the `signup` funnel event — so a `ref=`-tagged visitor who never signs up was invisible everywhere; this closes that gap for all visitors). Empty array (not `available:false`) when nobody has landed with a tagged link in the window, or when that one sub-query fails while the domain query still succeeds. `available:false` when the PostHog read creds are missing (local dev/forks) or the domain query fails. `getTrafficTrend()` — companion "how many, over time, from where" read (same admin-gated-PostHog-read shape as `getTrafficSources`): 4 parallel HogQL queries — landing (`/`) `$pageview` visitor counts bucketed via `toStartOfWeek`/`toStartOfMonth`, each run twice: grouped by referring domain (→ `TrafficChannel`, same `classifyChannel` as `getTrafficSources`) and grouped by `?ref=`/`?utm_source=` tag (→ campaign, same `extractURLParameter` pattern as `getTrafficSources`'s `campaigns` query). Returns `{ weekly, monthly, campaignTags, available }` — `weekly`/`monthly` are `TrafficTrendPoint[]` (`{date, visitors, channels: Record<TrafficChannel, number>, campaigns: Record<string, number>}`), 12 buckets oldest→newest, **zero-filled** (PostHog only returns buckets with rows). `visitors` is deliberately the **sum of the 4 channel counts**, not a separate whole-bucket distinct query, so a stacked chart's segments always sum to exactly its bar height — each channel count is itself a sum of per-domain `count(DISTINCT person_id)` (same approximation `getTrafficSources`'s channel chips use). **`campaignTags`** is a FIXED series set (top 6 tags by total visitors over the 12-*month* window, reused for both `weekly` and `monthly` `campaigns` records) so a tag's identity/color/order never shifts when switching granularity — a tag absent from a given week/month is simply 0, not missing. Powers the "Weekly"/"Monthly" tabs via **`TrafficTrendChart`** (own file: `src/components/features/admin/TrafficTrendChart.tsx`, NOT in `AdminCharts.tsx` — big enough to warrant separating) — a from-scratch SVG chart (measured against the card's real width via `ResizeObserver`, not viewBox scaling, so hairlines stay crisp) with: a **bar/line view toggle** (`BarChart3`/`LineChart` icon buttons); a **source selector** (`SourceSelect`, a custom dropdown matching `AdminShell`'s width-picker pattern) choosing among `channel:all` (stacked/multi-line, default) · one specific `channel:<TrafficChannel>` · `campaign:all` · one specific `campaign:<tag>` — picking a single series drops the legend (redundant per the dataviz skill: "a single series needs no legend box") and switches the bar chart from stacked to plain, or adds a ~10%-opacity area wash under the line; a real **left Y-axis + bottom X-axis** (hairline `#52525b`) with 3 gridline ticks (`0`/mid/`niceMax`, `niceCeil()` rounds to a clean 1/2/5×10ⁿ) and X-axis date labels (every 2nd bucket, to avoid crowding); and a **crosshair + floating tooltip** (clamped within the container) that lists every visible series' value at the hovered bucket, per the dataviz skill's "one tooltip, every series" rule (hover hit-zones are transparent full-height `<rect>`s per bucket, on top of the marks). **Two validated categorical palettes** (`dataviz` skill's `validate_palette.js` against the card's `bg-neutral-900`/`#21252b` surface): channels reuse slots 1-4 of the reference 8-hue dark set (`direct`=`#3987e5`, `organic`=`#199e70`, `social`=`#c98500`, `referral`=`#008300` — CVD separation lands in the 8–12 "floor band", legal only with direct labels, hence the mandatory legend/tooltip); campaign tags use slots 5-8 (`#9085e9`/`#e66767`/`#d55181`/`#d95926` — clears CVD cleanly, ΔE ≥ 23) assigned by a tag's fixed position in `campaignTags`, never by on-screen rank, so picking a different source never repaints a tag that stays visible. `viewMode`/`source` selection state is **lifted into `AdminTrafficSources.tsx`** (not local to the chart) so it survives switching to the Sources tab and back. Reuses the `Admin.channelDirect`/`channelOrganicSearch`/`channelSocial`/`channelReferral` i18n keys already used by the Sources tab for channel labels; campaign tags render as raw (untranslated) strings — they're user data, not UI copy. `getDesktopDownloadStats(days=30)` — same admin-gated-PostHog-read shape, groups the client-captured `desktop_download_clicked` event (fired from `DownloadView.tsx`'s download links — `/download` is the only surface with real file-download links; `LandingDownload.tsx` only deep-links to `/download`, so it isn't instrumented) by its `os` property. Returns `{ byOs, totalClicks, totalVisitors, days, available }`; powers `AdminDesktopStats.tsx` in the admin panel's "Desktop / Tauri" section. Exports type-only `PerUserActivity`/`EngagementOverview`/`UserDetail`/`UserSubscription`/`UserContentStats`/`UserAgents`/`AgentTokenSummary`/`ActivationFunnel`/`TrafficSourcesData`/`TrafficChannel`/`TrafficTrendData`/`TrafficTrendPoint`/`DesktopDownloadStats`.
- `src/lib/analytics/posthog-query.ts` — **Server-only** PostHog Query API (HogQL) reader. `runHogQL(query)` POSTs to the PostHog **app** host's `/api/projects/{POSTHOG_PROJECT_ID}/query/` (NOT the `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST` ingest host — region derived from it: `eu.i.` → `eu.posthog.com`, else `us.posthog.com`) with `Authorization: Bearer POSTHOG_API_KEY` (reuses the source-map upload creds from next.config.ts), returns the `results` rows or null on missing creds / failure. `hasPosthogQueryCreds()` reports whether both are set. Powers `getTrafficSources`. **Reuses the existing `POSTHOG_API_KEY` + `POSTHOG_PROJECT_ID` env — no new env; must be available at runtime (not only build) for the traffic card to populate.**
- `demoFeedback.ts` — `submitDemoFeedback({sentiment, comment})` (demo-only, best-effort insert into `demo_feedback`) + admin-gated `getDemoFeedback()` (sentiment counts + recent commented entries). Powers `DemoFeedbackPrompt` + the admin "Demo Feedback" section.
- `account.ts` — GDPR "right to erasure" self-service account deletion. **Email-confirmed two-step flow** (destructive/irreversible, so a click alone isn't enough — see **Account Deletion**): `requestAccountDeletion()` (checks preconditions, mints a 30-min single-use `account_deletion_tokens` row, emails a confirm link — nothing deleted yet) and `confirmAccountDeletion(token)` (called from the emailed link's confirm page; requires the SAME live session that requested it — re-validates the token + preconditions, marks it used, then actually deletes via the shared private `performAccountDeletion(userId)`). Blocked (both steps) for `role==='demo'` (already ephemeral/auto-reaped) and for anyone who's the `billing_owner_id` of a workspace with OTHER members or pending invites (must transfer ownership or remove them first — deleting them would strand those teammates' billing/access). Deletion itself: best-effort cancels any live Stripe subscription (`cancelSubscription()` from `billing.ts`) and best-effort purges every Cloudinary asset the user uploaded (walks `uploaded_assets` — the DB ledger row alone isn't real erasure of the file); nulls `agent_tokens.created_by` first (that column has no `onDelete` action, unlike almost every other user FK, so it would block the user delete for tokens left in a *shared* workspace they don't solely own); deletes every workspace where they're the **sole** member (cascades everything under it); then deletes the `user` row itself (cascades sessions/accounts/remaining `workspace_members`/oauth/`subscriptions`/`user_sessions`/`uploaded_assets`/`account_deletion_tokens`; SET-NULLs `agent_activity.owner_user_id`/`demo_feedback.user_id`/`email_log.user_id`) — then `signOut({redirectTo:'/'})`. Workspaces shared with teammates are left intact; the delete just removes the caller's own membership. i18n: `Errors.accountDelete{DemoBlocked,HasTeammates,NoEmail,EmailUnavailable,EmailFailed,InvalidToken,TokenExpired}` + `UserSettings.dangerZoneTitle`/`deleteAccount*`.
- `mailing.ts` — admin-gated mailing actions: `getMailingOverview` (30-day per-kind counts, unsubscribed/suppressed totals, newsletter audience, recent 50 `email_log` rows, campaign list), `saveCampaign`/`deleteCampaign` (draft-only), `previewCampaignHtml`, `sendTestCampaign`. See **Mailing System**.
- `contact.ts` — **Public, no auth** (the only unauthenticated server action in this list — the `/contact` page has no logged-in caller). `submitContactForm({name, email, message, company, startedAt})` — honeypot + fill-time + per-IP rate-limit spam gating, then emails the team inbox via `sendEmail(kind:'contact')`. See **Contact Form** under **Mailing System**.
- `workspace.ts` also exports `getWorkspaceStorageUsage(workspaceId)` (auth-gated; sums `uploaded_assets.bytes`) — surfaced read-only in `WorkspaceSettings` → `GeneralTab`.
- `sharing.ts` — Public page sharing server actions. `ShareRecord` type: `{ id, slug, pageId, workspaceId, permission: 'read'|'write', width: 'narrow'|'wide'|'full', inSitemap: boolean, createdBy, createdAt }`. Key exports:
  - `createShare(workspaceId, pageId, permission, customSlug?, width?)` — customSlug admin-only; regular users get UUID slug
  - `createShareWithChildren(...)` — same as createShare but also auto-shares all descendant workspace items
  - `updateShare(shareId, workspaceId, { permission?, width?, inSitemap? })` — inSitemap admin-only; **cascades `inSitemap` to all child shared pages automatically**
  - `revokeShare`, `revokeAllSharesInWorkspace`
  - `getSharesByWorkspace`, `getShareBySlug` (no auth), `getShareByPageId`, `countSharedPagesInWorkspace`
  - `updateSharedPageContent(shareId, content)` — requires login + write permission
- `workspace.ts` `createStandalonePage` and `createWorkspaceDatabase` call `autoShareIfParentShared` after creation — if `parentId` has a `shared_pages` record, the new child is **automatically shared** with the same `permission`, `width`, and `inSitemap` values. This is best-effort (never blocks creation).

**Types (`src/lib/types/`)**
- `views.ts` — `DatabaseView` (added `icon` and `iconColor`), `TableViewConfig`, `KanbanViewConfig` (added `hiddenGroups`), `CalendarViewConfig`, `ViewFilter`, `ViewSort`, `OpenBehavior`.
- `properties.ts` — `SelectOption` (`value`, `color`, optional `group` for status), `SELECT_COLORS` (9-color palette), `StatusGroup` (`todo`|`in_progress`|`complete`) + `STATUS_GROUP_ORDER`/`STATUS_GROUP_DEFAULT_COLOR`/`DEFAULT_STATUS_OPTIONS`/`getStatusGroup`, helpers: `getOptionColorByValue`, `getCardBorderAccents` (returns `{color, icon?, iconColor?}[]` per border-line segment; `getCardBorderDots` is a thin `.color`-only wrapper over it, both treat `status` like `select`) / `getCardBgColor`, `formatDateValue`.

**Database column types** — `SchemaColumn.type` ([templates.ts](src/lib/templates.ts)): `text`, `number`, `select`, `multi_select`, `status`, `user`, `multi_user`, `date`, `datetime`, `checkbox`, `url`, `email`, `phone`. All stored as JSON in `pages.properties` — **no migration** for new types.
  - **`status`** — single-select for work tracking. Reuses `options: SelectOption[]` but each option carries a `group` (`todo`/`in_progress`/`complete`). Rendered with a progress-ring glyph (todo = dashed empty, in_progress = half-filled, complete = filled check) via `StatusIcon`/`StatusChip`. The editor dropdowns group options under group headers. Groupable/colorable in kanban + calendar (`defaultKanbanView` prefers a `status` column); rename propagation in `updateDatabaseSchema` treats it like `select`.
  - **`user` / `multi_user`** — store workspace member **user ids** (string / string[]); no `options`. Resolved to avatar + name via `MembersContext` (`MembersProvider`/`useMembers`/`useMember`) — fed by `getWorkspaceMembers(workspaceId)` in the `/db/[id]` + `/db/[id]/[pageId]` routes and provided by `DatabaseView`. Display components in `PropertyTags.tsx` (`UserAvatar`/`UserChip`/`UserTags`); pickers in `InlineCellEditor` (table/kanban/calendar) and `PageEditor` (row sidebar).
- `MembersContext.tsx` — `MembersProvider` + `useMembers()`/`useMember(id)`: the workspace member list (`{id,name,email,image,role}`) for resolving `user`/`multi_user` cells.
- `PropertyTags.tsx` — shared display chips for the new types: `StatusIcon`, `StatusChip`, `UserAvatar`, `UserChip`, `UserTags`, `OptionIcon` (renders a `select`/`multi_select` option's configured `icon` — emoji/`lucide:Name`/upload URL, same format as `PageIcon` — tinted by the option's `color` when it's a lucide icon; renders nothing when `icon` is unset; used by every select/multi_select chip render site: `TableLayout`, `KanbanBoard`, `CalendarView`, `PageEditor`, `InlineCellEditor`).
