- `SelectOption` ([properties.ts](src/lib/types/properties.ts)) has an optional `icon?: string` field (emoji/`lucide:Name`/upload URL) set via a per-option `IconPicker` popover in `PropertiesPanel.tsx` (the schema editor's option chip row) — picking a lucide icon there also syncs the option's `color` to the picker's chosen swatch so the icon and chip background aren't two different colors the user didn't choose together.
- **Inline "create option" while picking a value** — `InlineCellEditor`'s `select`/`multi_select` dropdown (table/kanban cells) and `PageEditor`'s own select/multi_select property UI both take an `onCreateOption`/local handler: a search input filters existing options, and typing a value with no exact match shows "Create '<value>'" — selecting it appends `{value, color:'default'}` to the column schema via `updateDatabaseSchema` (persists + `revalidatePath` picks up the new option) and immediately assigns it to the row. `TableLayout`/`KanbanBoard`/`PageEditor` each implement `handleCreateOption(colId, value)` locally (need `database.id` + `schema`, already in scope); `GroupedTableLayout` inherits it for free since it renders `TableLayout` per group. i18n: `Database.searchOrCreateOption`/`createOptionLabel`.

**Core feature components (`src/components/features/`)**
- `WorkspaceSidebar` — Collapsible workspace tree, drag-and-drop reorder, optimistic mutations, mobile bottom-sheet context menu. **Optimistic delete must roll back (fixed 2026-07-10):** `confirmDelete` removed the item from local state and then `await`ed `deleteWorkspaceItem` inside `startTransition` with **no catch**. A server-side failure was swallowed, so the sidebar asserted a deletion that never happened — the page stayed in the DB, kept rendering as a child block inside its parent (those buttons come from `getSubItems`, not from the markdown), and was still openable. It now restores the pre-delete snapshot, surfaces `Workspace.deleteFailed` (all 8 locales) in an alert, and reports to PostHog via `reportClientError`. It also navigates away (`router.push('/app')`) only **after** the delete succeeds, instead of before awaiting it. Bottom bar includes an "AI Agents" button that opens `AgentsModal`, a "Plan & Billing" row, an "Install app" row (`PwaInstallButton` — web only), and Settings. **Drag-to-nest:** an item dragged onto a **page** has three drop zones — top ~30% (before, reorder line), bottom ~30% (after, reorder line), and the middle (nest **inside** — full-row blue ring + "Nest inside" badge, persisted via `reparentWorkspaceItem` + auto-expands the target). Databases (which can't hold children) only expose before/after. A before/after drop adopts the target's parent level, so dropping next to a root item un-nests and dropping next to a nested item joins that branch.
- `ContextMenu` — **Shared Notion-style right-click menu primitive.** Exports `useContextMenu(onClose?)` (→ `{ open(e, items), close, node, isOpen }`; `open` reads `e.clientX/Y`) + the `MenuItem` type (`action` {id,label,icon?,danger?,hint?,disabled?,onSelect} · `separator` · `label`). Renders via portal, clamps/flips into the viewport, suppresses the native menu, closes on Esc/outside-click/scroll/resize. Wired on: **sidebar items** ([WorkspaceSidebar](src/components/features/WorkspaceSidebar.tsx) — Open/Rename/Duplicate/Copy link/Add page inside/Share/Delete; desktop only, touch keeps the bottom sheet), **DB table rows** ([TableLayout](src/components/features/TableLayout.tsx)), **kanban cards** ([KanbanBoard](src/components/features/KanbanBoard.tsx)), and **calendar cards** ([CalendarView](src/components/features/CalendarView.tsx)) — the latter three share an Open/Open-in-full-page/Copy-link/Duplicate/Delete set (reuse existing `onRowClick`/`onCardClick`/`onDuplicatePage` + `confirmDeleteId`). i18n: `open`/`copyLink` added to both `Workspace` and `Database` namespaces.
- `AgentsModal` — **The AI agent control center.** Global MCP token overview modal opened from the sidebar "AI Agents" button. Lists all active tokens across all user workspaces (grouped by workspace). **Each workspace row shows both PAT (`rmns_`) and OAuth (`oa_`) tokens in one unified list** (`TokenRow` is a discriminated union over `{kind:'pat'|'oauth'}`). **Every row names whose agent it is** (2026-09-25): `getUserWorkspacesWithTokens` returns `owner` per PAT (creator's name/email, `isYou`, current role in that workspace, `active`); the row shows "You" / name · email · role, "Deleted account" for a creator-less PAT, and a red "No longer a member" badge when the creator left (that token is already refused by `agentAccess.ts`; revoking clears it from the list and the quota). OAuth rows are always the viewer's own (the OAuth list is filtered to them). PAT rows show real expiry countdown; OAuth rows show a 🔄 "Auto-renewing" badge (access token = 1h, refresh = 30d, so a per-hour countdown would mislead). Each row's icon is a clickable `AgentTypePicker` (brand icon via `agents/AgentMark`; owner/token-owner can set the agent type, persisted to `agent_name`; its dropdown renders in a `createPortal` with fixed coords so the surrounding `overflow-hidden` card never clips it). OAuth row labels prefer the user's `display_name` (set on the consent screen) over `client_name`. Both revoke via their respective server actions. Header has a **"Connect editor"** button that opens `ConnectModal` (a standalone full-screen modal, z-110) layered on top — no longer inline. Collapsible recent-activity log (last 60 calls — includes OAuth calls since migration `0034`). A **usage row** above it shows the caller's last-30-day consumption ("~X tokens · N calls", from `getMyAgentUsage()`; bytes→tokens at /4 via the shared `formatTokens` in `admin/format.ts`). Fetches via `getUserWorkspacesWithTokens()` + `getUserOAuthTokens()` + `getUserAgentActivity()` + `getMyAgentUsage()`.
- `agents/ConnectModal` — **Standalone full-screen modal** (own overlay, z-110) wrapping `ConnectFlow` in `bare` mode (supplies its own header/chrome). Opened from `AgentsModal`'s "Connect editor" button. Props: `mcpUrl`, `mintTargets`, `onClose`.
- `onboarding/` — **New-user onboarding (MVP).** Drives the activation funnel (signup → first MCP token → first agent call) so the value (connecting an AI agent over MCP) isn't left to self-discovery. `OnboardingGuide` is mounted inside `WorkspaceSidebar` (bottom, above the "AI Agents" button) **only when the `showOnboarding` prop is set** — passed solely by the always-mounted desktop sidebar in `[locale]/layout.tsx`, NOT by the mobile drawer's `WorkspaceSidebar` (`MobileNavWrapper`), so the welcome modal/checklist never double-mount. **Skipped for `role==='demo'`**.
  - `OnboardingGuide` — Orchestrator (client). Self-fetches `getOnboardingProgress()`; renders the welcome modal (first run), the checklist widget, and the shared `ConnectModal` the steps open. Re-checks on tab focus/visibility + gently polls (20s) while connected-but-no-call-yet so the "first call" tick lands without a manual refresh. **State is real DB state**; only `welcome_seen`/`dismissed` bits live in `localStorage` (`remnus_onboarding_welcome_seen` / `remnus_onboarding_dismissed`). `mcpUrl = window.location.origin/api/mcp`; `mintTargets` come straight from the progress result.
  - `WelcomeModal` — One-time first-run modal (portal, z-120). Splits intent into the two real paths — **"Connect my AI agent"** (recommended → opens `ConnectModal`) vs **"Explore on my own"** (dismiss) — surfaces the trust story (scoped tokens · every write logged · revoke anytime) and **frames the pre-seeded "Paint clone" workspace as a sample** so it isn't mistaken for the user's own data.
  - `GettingStartedChecklist` — Persistent sidebar widget. 3 funnel-mirrored steps (account ✓ · connect agent · first call) auto-ticked from `OnboardingProgress`; the first incomplete step gets a "Start" button (opens `ConnectModal`). **Minimize (−)** collapses it into a sidebar-style "Getting started N/3" button (matching the AI Agents / Plan / Settings rows) that re-expands on click — persisted in `localStorage['remnus_onboarding_collapsed']`. A **permanent dismiss** is only offered once every step is done (the celebratory row → `localStorage['remnus_onboarding_dismissed']`). i18n: `Onboarding` namespace.
- `agents/AgentMark` — **Shared agent brand-icon resolver.** Reconciles the 3 id systems (EDITORS / AGENT_OPTIONS / AIMark names) + free-text OAuth `client_name`. Exports `AGENT_MARKS` (canonical selectable agents — `id` is what's persisted in `agent_name`), `resolveAgentMark(hint)` (best-effort substring inference from any free text — incl. mapping `mcp-remote`'s generic registration names like "MCP CLI Proxy"/"MCP CLI Client" → Claude, since the `.mcpb` bundle connects Claude Desktop through that proxy), `markForId(id)` (explicit id → mark), `MarkIcon`, `VscodeMark` (also reused by ConnectFlow), and the `<AgentMark override hint fallback>` component (override id → inferred from override → inferred from hint → Globe/Zap fallback). PAT mint via ConnectFlow now passes the editor id as `agentName`; OAuth tokens get a user-set `agent_name` override (else inferred from `client_name`). The `AgentsModal` `AgentTypePicker` (clickable token icon → dropdown of `AGENT_MARKS` + "Auto-detect") writes via `setAgentTokenAgent` / `setOAuthTokenAgent`.
- `agents/ConnectFlow` — 3-step MCP onboarding (editor → connect → test). `bare` prop renders only the step body (no outer card/header) — used by `ConnectModal`; without it, renders its own bordered card. **OAuth is the primary/recommended path** (token-less: editor runs its own OAuth 2.1 discovery on first 401), PAT is a collapsible "Advanced" section that mints a PAT inline (workspace picker + scope). Per-editor artifact: Claude Code → `claude mcp add` command; Cursor/VS Code → `cursor://`/`vscode:mcp/install` deeplink (URL-only for OAuth, embedded `Authorization` header for PAT); Codex → `~/.codex/config.toml` TOML snippet (`[mcp_servers.remnus]` with `url`; OAuth via `codex mcp login remnus`, PAT via `http_headers = { Authorization = "Bearer …" }`); Windsurf/Continue/Antigravity → JSON config snippet; **Other tool** (`custom`) → raw MCP endpoint URL + standard `mcpServers` JSON config (a catch-all for any MCP client not in the list). The editor list (`EDITORS`) + per-editor `kind` (`command`/`deeplink`/`json`/`toml`/`generic`) + `OAUTH_READY` + `CONFIG_PATHS` and builders all live in `src/lib/mcp/deeplinks.ts` (`buildCursorUrl`/`buildVscodeUrl`/`buildClaudeCmd`/`buildJsonConfig`/`buildCodexToml` — all take `(mcpUrl, token?)`; token omitted = OAuth mode — plus `CODEX_LOGIN_CMD`). **Desktop-only one-click connect:** on `json`/`toml`/`command` editors, `useIsTauri()` gates an additive "connect automatically" block that calls the `agent_connect.rs` Tauri commands (see **Tauri** below) instead of asking for copy-paste — every manual block stays visible underneath as a fallback. `useIsTauri()`-fetched `detect_installed_agents` results also add a small "detected on this device" badge in `StepChoose`/`StepConnect`.
- `WorkspaceSettingsModal` — 4-tab modal shell. **Tab order:** General → Tokens → Members → Sharing (ImportTab accessible from GeneralTab). Takes an `onOpenAgents` prop (closes settings + opens `AgentsModal`). Tabs live in `workspace-settings/`: `GeneralTab` (rename, icon, danger zone — delete warns if shared pages exist), `TokensTab` (**thin redirect** — short blurb + "Open AI Agents center" button that calls `onOpenAgents`; all token/connect management now lives in `AgentsModal`. The old per-workspace token CRUD / onboarding flow — `McpCreateToken`/`McpEditToken`/`McpOnboarding` — was removed), `MembersTab` (invite, member list), `SharingTab` (list + revoke shared pages per workspace; `onNavigateToMembers` prop links the "private sharing" hint to the Members tab), `ImportTab` (Notion ZIP import). Shared types in `workspace-settings/types.ts`. Mobile-responsive: top horizontal tab strip on `< sm`, left nav on `≥ sm`.
- `share/ShareModal.tsx` — Modal for creating/managing a share from sidebar context menu or page ⋯ menu. Features: permission (read/write), width (narrow/wide/full), "include child pages" toggle (cascade), custom slug (admin only), edit existing share (permission + width + `inSitemap` toggle — admin only), revoke. Shows `SEO ✓` badge when `inSitemap = true`.
- `share/SharedPageView.tsx` — Public page renderer. Shows sticky navbar (Remnus logo, breadcrumb, permission badge, save status, Sign in / Get started CTAs for guests, Go to app for logged-in non-members). Uses `share.width` for content container. Accepts `navTree` (shared nav sidebar), `parentSlug` (back button when no navTree), `shareMap` (passed to BlockEditor for child block link resolution), `editable` (Tiptap editable mode — `false` blocks typing but allows click navigation on child blocks).
- `share/SharedPageNav.tsx` — Navigation sidebar for shared page trees. Desktop: sticky 224px left sidebar. Mobile: collapsible "Contents" bar. Auto-expands branch containing current page. Renders `SharedNavItem` tree with `PageIcon`, chevron toggles, active highlight. Props: `navTree`, `currentPageId`, `mobileOnly`/`desktopOnly` (rendered twice for layout separation).
- `TemplatePickerModal` — 2-step item creation from templates (defined in `src/lib/templates.ts`).
- `recurrence/RecurrenceDialog` — The repeat editor: 8 presets (daily / weekdays / weekly / biweekly / monthly-by-date / monthly-by-weekday / yearly / custom) plus a full builder behind "Custom", with a live natural-language summary and a **"this will create N cards in the next 90 days" preview** — the guardrail against casually picking "every day, forever". **Not a radio list, on purpose:** every preset is a tile that renders its own sub-label computed from THIS card's date ("Every month → on the 19th", "Monthly → 3rd Tuesday"), so the user reads the consequence instead of deriving it; the summary sits at the top where it reads as the answer rather than a footnote; and the custom controls are steppers / day circles / segmented switches instead of number fields and `<select>`s. Deliberately NOT folded into `DateRangePicker`: that popover is 288px wide and saves-and-closes on any outside mousedown, which fights a multi-control form.
- `recurrence/RecurrenceScopeDialog` — The "this / this and following / all" question, shared by delete and by rule changes. A dedicated component rather than a three-button `ConfirmDialog` because the honest answer to "what will this do?" depends on the scope AND on how many affected cards already have content — so each option is a tile carrying **its own** live count (`previewScopeImpact` per scope), not one shared line under the group. Delete offers all three scopes and paints them red; a *rule* change offers only `thisAndFollowing`/`all` ("just this one" is meaningless for a rhythm). When dirty cards exist it surfaces a checkbox to include them — preserving is the default so nothing dies on a reflex click, but the choice is explicit rather than silent.
- `recurrence/parts.tsx` — Shared `OptionTile` / `Segmented` / `Stepper` used by both dialogs. Native radios read as a settings list rather than as a decision, and leave nowhere for the sub-label that actually explains the option.
- `recurrence/useRecurrenceControls` — The pick-a-rule → pick-a-scope → apply conversation, as a hook returning `{ openRepeat, requestDelete, isSeriesCard, run, node }` (same idiom as `useContextMenu()`). Every surface that offers recurrence uses it, so the calendar menu, the card's ⋯ menu and the page editor cannot drift apart. `onPlainDelete` lets each caller keep its own existing confirmation for non-series rows.
- `recurrence/SeriesPanel` — The recurrence surface **inside an opened card** (peek + full page), rendered by `PageEditor` between the properties and the body. Shows "part of a repeating series" + the rhythm in words with Edit / Remove-from-series, a muted struck-through note for a detached card, or a dashed "Repeat this task" button when the card has a date but no rule. This is where it matters most: the customer's whole pattern is opening a future occurrence and filling it in, so the question "is this one of many?" has to be answerable without going back to the grid.
- `recurrence/RecurringBadge` — The ⟳ marker, used by `CalendarView`, `KanbanBoard` and `TableLayout`. Carries the rule in words as its tooltip when a rule is supplied (the calendar has the map already); Kanban/Table pass none and fall back to a plain "Repeating" label rather than each loading the map. A detached card keeps a muted struck-through icon instead of losing the marker — "used to follow a series, doesn't any more" is what the user needs when wondering why it stopped moving with the rest.
- `WhatsNewButton` — Sidebar "What's New" row (between `PwaInstallButton` and Settings) + the changelog modal it opens. Entries come from [src/lib/changelog.ts](src/lib/changelog.ts); the panel renders them as a date-grouped timeline with `new`/`improved`/`fixed` chips. **Self-contained on purpose:** the sidebar is mounted twice (desktop shell + `MobileNavWrapper` drawer), so the seen-state lives in the component instead of being threaded through both layouts — it reads/writes the client-readable `remnus_whatsnew_seen` cookie (newest seen entry id) directly via `document.cookie`, matching the per-device `remnus_*` preference cookies in `actions/preferences.ts`. Unread count = entries above that id; with no cookie it falls back to a 7-day recency window so a fresh signup never sees a "24 updates" badge. Opening the panel marks it read (not closing — otherwise navigating away leaves the badge up), while the highlight ring is frozen at open time so it doesn't vanish mid-read. i18n namespace `WhatsNew` (all 8 locales); entry text itself is localized inside `changelog.ts`, not in `messages/`.
- `DatabaseView` — View orchestrator: tabs, filters, sorts, peek modals, URL deep-link (`?v=view_id`). `handleAddRow(initialProperties?, opts?)` — the shared "create a row" handler behind every view's `onCreatePage`; Kanban/Calendar pass `{openAfterCreate: true}` so the new row's peek/full-page editor opens automatically once the real id comes back from the server (via `handlePageClick`), matching how the click-to-open peek already behaves — Table/GroupedTable keep the old behavior (row appears inline, no auto-open) since that view's creation flow expects immediate inline title typing instead. **Side peek drawer** (`openBehavior:'side'`) is resizable: `sidePeekWidth` state (default 772px = the old fixed `max-w-2xl` + 100px, range 420–1100px, persisted to `localStorage['remnus_side_peek_width']`) drives an inline `style={{width}}` only at the `sm` breakpoint (a `useSyncExternalStore`-based `isDesktopViewport` check, same pattern as `TableLayout`'s coarse-pointer detection, avoids a hydration-flash and avoids the inline width fighting the mobile bottom-sheet's `inset-x-0`); a `cursor-col-resize` handle strip on the drawer's left edge drives `handleSidePeekResizeStart`, which toggles a `body.resize-drag-active` class (globals.css) for the drag's duration — a `preventDefault()` on the handle's own `mousedown` alone did not reliably stop the browser starting a text selection once the pointer moved over surrounding page content mid-drag; `resize-drag-active` forces `user-select:none` + `cursor:col-resize` on `body *` for the duration (same technique `BlockSelectionExtension`'s marquee uses via `block-marquee-active`, kept as a separate class since that one also forces `cursor:default` which would fight the resize cursor). **Marquee suppression:** the resize handle sits in the peek editor's left margin, which is a valid block-selection **marquee "empty zone"**, so a resize drag used to spuriously start a rubber-band block-selection (a stray "area picker" box). Fixed with two guards: the handle carries `data-no-block-marquee` and `BlockSelectionExtension`'s `onMouseDown` early-returns when `event.target.closest('[data-no-block-marquee]')`, plus the handler `stopPropagation()`s the mousedown (the marquee listener can be attached as high as `documentElement` when the editor's scroll parent isn't settled at mount, so stopPropagation alone isn't enough → the attribute guard is the reliable one). i18n: `Database.resizePanel`. **Header "New" button** (`handleHeaderNewClick`, replaces a bare `onClick={handleAddRow}` that was accidentally spreading the click `MouseEvent` into the new row's properties) prefills today's date (`formatYYYYMMDD(new Date())`) into the active Calendar view's `dateCol`, or else the first `date`/`datetime` schema column if any — without this the button's new row had nowhere obvious to land (invisible on a Calendar view until opened directly) — and always opens the new row immediately (`{openAfterCreate:true}`), regardless of view type.
- `DatabasePropertiesSidebar` — Shell (~250 lines). Sub-panels in `database-sidebar/`: `PropertiesPanel` (column schema editor), `GroupingLayoutSection` (shared group-by/group visibility/order/color controls for table + kanban), `KanbanLayoutSection` (card props/colors), `CalendarLayoutSection` (date col, view mode, card props), `FiltersSection`, `SortsSection`. Shared utilities (`Checkbox`, `getPropertyIcon`, `selectCls`) in `database-sidebar/shared.tsx`.
- `TableLayout` — Notion-style grid, draggable columns, inline cell editing, row color tinting. Supports an embedded/grouped mode where row drag is disabled and the Toggle Columns control is rendered only once by the parent.
- `GroupedTableLayout` — Vertical table sections grouped by a select/status property. Reuses `TableLayout` per visible group, respects the shared grouping settings (`groupByCol`, `groupOrder`, `hiddenGroups`, `groupColBg`), keeps `Uncategorized` last/non-draggable, and pre-fills the grouped property when creating rows inside a group.
- `KanbanBoard` — Grouped by select column, draggable groups, card color, group bg tint. **Cards are draggable from anywhere on the card** (`draggable={true}` unconditionally on the card's outer div, not just the corner grip button) — a plain click still opens the card since native HTML5 DnD only kicks in after real pointer movement past the mousedown; the corner `GripVertical` button is kept as a visible affordance and its own independent drag start still works too. (Previously gated behind an `isCardDragReady` state only flipped true by mousedown on the grip — removed.)
- `CalendarView` — Monthly/weekly grid, card drag-to-reschedule, card color. **Cards are draggable from anywhere on the card**, same change/reasoning as `KanbanBoard`; also fixed a pre-existing bug in the card's `onDragEnd` that set `draggedCardId` back to the dragged card's own id instead of clearing it to `null`, which left a cancelled (not dropped) drag permanently stuck at `opacity-25`. **`user`/`multi_user` card properties render as `PropertyTags.tsx`'s `UserAvatarStack`** (avatar-only, no name text — calendar cards are tight on space) instead of `UserChip`/`UserTags`: avatars **overlap** GitHub/Notion-style (negative `-ml-1.5`, each with a thin separator `ring` in a card-dark colour), and the viewer's own avatar gets a theme-accent `ring-2 ring-blue-500` (`--color-blue-500` redefined per `[data-theme]` in globals.css → theme-aware for free) and is lifted above its neighbours (`relative z-10`) so the accent ring never clips. The self accent is a **`ring` (box-shadow), NOT a `border`** — a bordered self-avatar (`p-0.5 border-2`) was a few px taller than its siblings and stretched the whole calendar card's height; a ring paints outside the border-box so it never grows the avatar. Hovering the whole avatar group shows one portal-rendered tooltip listing every assignee's avatar + name stacked (not per-avatar tooltips). Needs `currentUserId` — threaded server-side from `getCurrentUser()` in `(app)/db/[id]/page.tsx` → `DatabaseView` → `CalendarView`, and client-side (Tauri keep-alive) from `(app)/layout.tsx` → `AppShell` → `TabHost` → `TabPane` → `DatabaseView`, mirroring the existing `isAdmin` prop's exact path. **Month view uses a rolling 6-week window** anchored on `currentDate`: the grid starts one week before the week containing the anchor (not the 1st of the month), so "today" sits on the 2nd row (current week + one prior week visible, rest looking ahead) instead of opening with stale past weeks on top. Monthly prev/next navigation + current-month highlighting are preserved.
- `InlineCellEditor` — Shared inline editor for all property types (text, number, date, datetime, select, multi_select).
- `StandalonePageEditor` — Title + block editor, auto-save, back button when `parentId` set. Props include `isAdmin` (passed from route). Header: `[SaveStatus] [Refresh] [⋯]` — the ⋯ dropdown contains width selector (Narrow/Wide/Full) and Share button (opens `ShareModal`).
- `PageEditor` — DB row editor: properties panel + block editor, peek-compact layout. Props include `isAdmin`. Header: `[SaveStatus] [⋯]` — the ⋯ dropdown contains width selector + Share button + Duplicate + Delete.
- `PageBacklinksPanel` — Client, self-fetching. Mounted at the bottom of both `StandalonePageEditor` and `PageEditor` (skipped in `PageEditor`'s peek mode). Calls `getPageRelations(workspaceId, pageId)` on mount/pageId-change and renders a collapsible "Backlinks (N)" section listing pages whose body references this one (via the `page_links` graph) — renders nothing while loading or when there are no backlinks (Notion-style "linked mentions", silent when empty). Each row links to `/page/<id>` or `/db/<databaseId>[/<id>]` depending on `RelatedPageRef.type`. i18n: `Page.backlinksTitle` (all 8 locales).
- `graph/LocalGraphPanel` — "Local map" section directly under `PageBacklinksPanel` in both editors (not in peek). **Always starts closed**; only opening it loads `graph/LocalGraphView` (dynamic, `ssr:false`), which fetches `getLocalGraphData` and mounts `GraphCanvas` — so a page load never pays for sigma/WebGL. See **Knowledge Map**.
- `BlockEditor` — Props: `initialContent`, `onChange`, `onImmediateSave?`, `placeholder?`, `workspaceId?`, `parentId?`, `initialSubItems?`, `shareMap?`, `editable?` (default `true`). **Live child-block reconciliation (2026-07-10):** the embedded child-block list is prepended from `initialSubItems` at mount, but the editor is keyed by a stable `page.id`, so a refetch (Yenile button / activity heartbeat / another user's edit) never reached it — only Ctrl+R (a full remount) did, which is why sidebar rename/add/delete of a sub-page left the open parent's body stale. A `useEffect` on `[editor, initialSubItems]` now reconciles the child blocks **in place** (no remount → cursor + unsaved edits survive): rename patches attrs, a new child is inserted (prepended), a deleted child's **owned** block is removed; **link-only** (`linkOnly`) reference blocks are never touched. Guarded on `initialSubItems === undefined` (a still-loading/errored fetch) so a transient empty result can't wipe the list — an empty *array* is trusted and drives removals. `TabPane` passes `subItems` as `undefined` until the fetch succeeds, feeding that guard. Propagation: web re-renders the open page via the action's `revalidatePath`; the Tauri keep-alive tabs get it from `WorkspaceSidebar` calling `useTabNav().refresh()` after rename/delete/reparent (+ the heartbeat for cross-user/other tabs). The `editable` prop maps to Tiptap's `editable` option — `false` prevents typing but allows click events (child block navigation works). A `useEffect` on `[editor, initialSubItems]` syncs child block node attrs (title/icon/iconColor) from `initialSubItems` after mount, keeping display current even if sub-pages were renamed after last save.
- `MobileNavWrapper` — Mobile-only bottom bar: workspace sheet, context-aware + button, user sheet.
- `ViewsBar` — View tabs with inline rename/delete/add; collapses to dropdown on mobile.
- `PageIcon` — Renders icons: emoji string, `lucide:Name`, or `https://…` image URL (as `<img>`). Supports 9 theme colors.
- `IconPicker` — Popover with 3 tabs: emoji, Lucide icon + color, Upload (Cloudinary via `POST /api/upload`). Used for workspace items, DB rows, and workspaces.
- `SaveStatus` — Auto-fading save indicator (idle → saving → saved → error).
- `LanguageSwitcher` — Language dropdown; calls `setLocale()` + `router.refresh()`.
- `AdminUsersTable` — Paginated user table (selectable page size: 10/25/50/100, via a `Rows per page` control that always shows whenever there's at least one row, not only when multiple pages exist) with a leading `#` row-number column (continuous across pages), client-side column sorting (name/email/sign-in/**plan**/last-active/time-spent/**storage**/joined), search + role + sign-in filters, last-active, time-spent & storage columns (from `getEngagementOverview().perUser` — each `PerUserActivity` now carries `storageBytes`), a **Plan** column (the user's `subscriptions` tier — `getAllUsers()` LEFT JOINs `subscriptions` keyed by `ownerUserId`, defaulting to `free` with no row, mirroring `getUserDetail`'s per-user subscription resolution), and clickable rows that open `AdminUserDetailModal`. **No dedicated Role column** — admin status is shown as a small Shield icon in front of the name instead (the role filter dropdown still works, it just isn't mirrored as its own column). **Delete now lives in the modal, not the row** — the table has no per-row destructive action; a small `(You)` marker next to the name replaces the old row-level self badge.
- `admin/AdminUserDetailModal` — Per-user detail modal opened from `AdminUsersTable`. Fetches `getUserDetail(userId)` on open. Sections: account details (incl. inline role promote/demote via `setUserRole`), activity summary (total time, session count, last active, **storage used**), **content** (owned-workspace / pages / databases / records counts), **AI agents (MCP)** (connected-agents count, total tool calls, last-call time, then a list of PAT tokens with scope + last-used + an active/expired/revoked status badge, plus an "N active OAuth connections" line), **subscription plan** (current tier + a Stripe/Manual/Free source badge; tier-picker buttons call `adminSetUserPlan` to manually activate a plan — hidden/disabled when the sub is Stripe-managed, showing a "managed by Stripe" note instead), and the user's workspaces with their items (each showing its own storage total). Replaces the old standalone workspaces table. **Header carries the delete-user control** (moved off the table row 2026-07): a trash icon next to the close button (hidden for the signed-in admin's own account) that expands into an inline Confirm/Cancel, calling the existing `adminDeleteUser` action and closing the modal + `router.refresh()`-ing the list on success.
- `admin/format.ts` — Shared admin formatters: `formatDate`, `formatDuration`, `formatRelative`, `safeDate`.

**Editor (`src/components/features/editor/`)**
- `BlockEditor` — Tiptap editor: StarterKit (with `link` configured: `openOnClick:false`, autolink, linkOnPaste; **`codeBlock: false`** — replaced by `FencedCodeBlock`), `@tiptap/markdown` v3, TaskList, Table, ChildBlock, YoutubeEmbed, ImageBlock, CalloutBlock, BookmarkBlock, FileBlock, SlashCommand, PageMention, EmojiSuggestion, FencedCodeBlock. `editorProps.handleClick` intercepts `<a>` clicks: internal `/…` hrefs navigate via the SPA router (after `onImmediateSave`), external hrefs open in a new tab. **`editorProps.handleDOMEvents.click` must keep `preventDefault()` on internal `a[data-page-link]` clicks** (2026-09-25): a pageLink is a `contenteditable=false` atom, so the browser also followed its href natively on `click` — a full document reload racing the save + SPA push, because `handleClick` only sees the earlier mouseup and its `preventDefault` cannot stop the click. Links inside editable text (the Link mark) are not followed natively, so they need nothing. `editorProps.handlePaste`/`handleDrop` upload **any** pasted/dropped file — images become an `imageBlock` (`kind=image`), everything else a `fileBlock` (`kind=file`) — at the drop position (`handleDrop` is gated on `!moved` so an internal block drag isn't mistaken for a file drop). While each request is in flight a skeleton is shown at the insert point via `UploadPlaceholder` (below), and the insert position is re-read from the placeholder on completion so the block still lands correctly if the user kept typing above it. **Delete-key behavior:** Delete inside an **empty top-level block** deletes that block instead of merging the next one into it — `joinTextblockForward` keeps the current node and pulls the following textblock's content *into* it, so the survivor inherited the **current** node's type (pressing Delete on an empty heading turned the paragraph below into a heading; an empty paragraph above a heading flattened that heading). Restricted to `$from.depth === 1` because removing the lone paragraph inside a `listItem` would leave a schema-invalid empty item; a non-empty block still uses `joinTextblockForward` as before. Use `key={page.id}` to remount on page switch. **Color round-trip:** markdown has no syntax for text/highlight colors, so the default serializer drops them (a colored run reverted on reload). `ColorTextStyle` (`TextStyle.extend`) and `ColorHighlight` (`Highlight.extend`, `multicolor`) override `renderMarkdown` to emit inline HTML (`<span style="color:…">`, `<mark data-color="…" style="background-color:…">`) when a color attr is present — tiptap's own `parseHTML` rules read it back on parse. **Both color marks set `priority: 99` (below the formatting marks at 100) so they serialize INNERMOST** — `~~<span style="color:…">text</span>~~` rather than `<span style="color:…">~~text~~</span>`. The latter (the old behavior — `TextStyle` defaults to priority 101) wraps the formatting delimiters in inline HTML, which marked then parses as one HTML blob, so the `~~`/`**`/`*` never re-tokenize and show up literally on reload. Innermost color keeps the delimiters outside the span so they round-trip. (Pages saved before this fix keep the literal markers until the run is re-formatted; a separate `<p data-indent>` indented-paragraph HTML block has the same unfixed limitation for marks inside it.) The mark CSS in `globals.css` (`.prose-editor strong/em/s { color: inherit }`) must stay `inherit` so bold/italic/strike don't override an explicit text color. **Invalid-doc self-heal:** `@tiptap/markdown` can parse some HTML-bearing / Notion-imported markdown into a schema-invalid doc (a stray top-level `text` node, a `paragraph` nested in a `paragraph`, an empty `listItem`). ProseMirror builds it anyway (`fromJSON` skips validation), then the editor **hard-crashes on the very next transaction** — TrailingNode's `appendTransaction` calls `contentMatchAt` on the invalid doc and throws `Called contentMatchAt on a node with invalid content` (so even starting a marquee selection blows up). A mount `useEffect` checks `doc.type.validContent` (top + descendants) and, when invalid, round-trips through HTML (`setContent(editor.getHTML(), { contentType: 'html', emitUpdate: false })`) — ProseMirror's DOMParser re-normalizes to a valid structure. It heals in memory (no update emitted); a later real edit re-saves clean markdown.
- `UploadPlaceholder` (`UploadPlaceholder.ts`) — Skeleton shown where a pasted/dropped image or file will land, for the duration of the upload. Implemented as a **widget `Decoration`, deliberately NOT a node**: a placeholder node would be part of the document, so it would fire `onUpdate`, serialize into the saved markdown, and get persisted if the upload failed or the user navigated away mid-flight. A decoration lives beside the doc (nothing saved, no update emitted) yet is still position-mapped, so the skeleton follows the content as the user types above it. Exports `UploadPlaceholder` (configured with an i18n'd `uploadingLabel`), `uploadPlaceholderKey` (dispatch `{add:{id,pos,kind,name}}` / `{remove:{id}}` as tr meta) and `findPlaceholderPos(state,id)` (the mapped insert position on completion). The plugin also contributes `class="has-upload-skeleton"` on the editor root via `props.attributes` — the skeleton sits inside an empty paragraph, whose Tiptap placeholder `::before` ("type '/' for commands") otherwise renders straight through it; `globals.css` suppresses the hint under that class. Styles: `.upload-skeleton*` in `globals.css` (theme-aware via the neutral CSS vars; shimmer disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`). i18n: `Editor.uploading`.
- `TableControls` (`TableControls.tsx`) — Floating toolbar shown above the table the caret currently sits in (mounted by `BlockEditor` only when `editable`). Provides the affordances missing after `/table`: **add column / add row** (arrow-insert icons `BetweenVerticalEnd`/`BetweenHorizontalEnd` → `addColumnAfter`/`addRowAfter`), **delete column / delete row** (red grid icons), **cell color** (a `PaintBucket` button → swatch popover; applies `setCellAttribute('backgroundColor', …)` to the caret cell or every cell in the active `CellSelection`; "No color" clears it), **toggle header row**, and **delete table**. Positions against the live table DOM rect (zoom-aware, like `BlockSelectionToolbar`). i18n keys in `Editor`: `tableAddColumn`/`tableAddRow`/`tableDeleteColumn`/`tableDeleteRow`/`tableToggleHeaderRow`/`tableDelete`/`tableCellColor`/`tableCellColorNone`.
- `TableMarkdown` (`TableMarkdown.ts`) — Table persistence + cell coloring + column-width round-trip. `BgTableCell`/`BgTableHeader` extend `@tiptap/extension-table-cell`/`-header` with a `backgroundColor` attribute (renders inline `background-color` style + a `data-bg-color` mirror that `parseHTML` prefers for an exact round-trip; sanitized via the same `safeColor` whitelist as the color marks). `MarkdownTable` extends `Table` and overrides `renderMarkdown`: **GFM tables carry only text + alignment, so a table with a cell background color OR a manual column width (`colwidth`) is serialized as a single-line HTML `<table>` block** (`tableNeedsHtml`; round-trips via `@tiptap/markdown`'s HTML token → `generateHTML`/`generateJSON` → `BgTableCell.parseHTML` for colors + the cell `colwidth="N"` attribute for widths); a **plain** table keeps the clean GFM serialization (`this.parent`, the base extension's `renderMarkdown`). `generateHTML` needs the full extension set, captured at `onCreate` into a module-level ref. BlockEditor wires `MarkdownTable.configure({ resizable: true, lastColumnResizable: false })` + `BgTableCell` + `BgTableHeader` (plus `TableRow`) instead of the raw extensions — so columns are **drag-resizable** (prosemirror-tables `columnResizing`; the last column flexes to absorb), and the resize-handle/`resize-cursor` CSS lives in `globals.css`. Inline cell `style` overrides the (non-`!important`) `td`/`th` background rules in `globals.css`. **Row height is intentionally not draggable** — prosemirror-tables has no row-resize support and rows auto-size to content (Notion-like).
- `CodeBlockExtension` (`FencedCodeBlock`) / `CodeBlockView` — extends `@tiptap/extension-code-block`. (1) Overrides `renderMarkdown` to size the markdown fence to `max(3, longestInnerBacktickRun + 1)` backticks — the default serializer always emits a 3-backtick fence, so a code body containing a ``` run closed the block early on reparse and corrupted the round-trip. (2) Adds a React node view (`CodeBlockView`, `NodeViewContent as="code"`) that collapses long code (> ~320px) behind a localized "Show more"/"Show less" toggle (`Editor.codeShowMore`/`codeShowLess`); auto-expands on focus so editing never lands the caret in a hidden region. Disabled in StarterKit (`codeBlock: false`) and added separately so it owns codeBlock serialization + rendering.
- `YoutubeEmbedExtension` / `YoutubeEmbedView` — Tiptap node (`youtubeEmbed`, atom, draggable) embedding a YouTube video as a responsive 16:9 iframe. Inserted via the `/` "YouTube Video" slash command; the node view shows a URL input until a video id is set (`extractYouTubeId` parses watch / youtu.be / embed / shorts / live URLs or a bare id). Serializes as `<div data-yt-id="…">` for markdown round-trip (same HTML-block approach as ChildBlock).
- **Media blocks** (`ImageBlock`, `CalloutBlock`, `BookmarkBlock`, `FileBlock` — each an `…Extension.ts` atom node + `…View.tsx` node view). All inserted via `/` slash commands and serialize as a **single-line `<div data-*>` HTML block** (round-trip-safe like ChildBlock; text/newlines encoded as `&quot;`/`&#10;`):
  - `ImageBlock` (`div[data-img-src]`) — upload (`POST /api/upload` `kind=image`) or paste image URL; renders `<img>`. Has an `align` attr (left/center/right) with hover toolbar controls. Delete calls `/api/upload/delete` to remove the Cloudinary asset. `workspaceId` option (set in BlockEditor) is sent with uploads for storage accounting.
  - `CalloutBlock` (`div[data-callout-color]`) — emoji icon + theme color (default/blue/green/amber/red, see `CALLOUT_COLORS`) + autosizing **plain-text** body. (Rich-text body intentionally out of scope — see Work Plan task.)
  - `BookmarkBlock` (`div[data-bm-url]`) — link-preview card; Open Graph metadata fetched once via `GET /api/og` and frozen into attributes.
  - `FileBlock` (`div[data-file-url]`) — downloadable attachment (`POST /api/upload` `kind=file`); name + size + download button. Delete calls `/api/upload/delete`; `workspaceId` option sent with uploads.
  - Interactive controls inside these atom node views (URL inputs, the callout textarea, toolbar buttons) only work because each `ReactNodeViewRenderer` is given `{ stopEvent: mediaStopEvent }` (`mediaStopEvent.ts`): it returns true for events whose target is an `input/textarea/select/button/a` (but not `[data-drag-handle]`), so ProseMirror ignores them entirely — including right after insertion when a NodeSelection sits on the fresh node. A React `onMouseDown` is **not** enough (it fires at the React root, after PM's `view.dom` handler already grabbed the NodeSelection). `assetClient.ts` holds the `deleteUploadedAsset(url)` fire-and-forget helper.
- `ChildBlockExtension` — Tiptap node for embedded sub-pages/databases. Serializes as `<div data-cb-id>` — standard HTML block element required because `marked` does not tokenize custom elements. `linkOnly` attr (`data-cb-link="1"`) marks a **reference to an existing page** (block "Link to page") vs an owned embed; link-only blocks delete the block only, never the target page. Options: `workspaceId`, `parentId`, `onImmediateSave`, `shareMap: Record<string,string>|null` — when provided (shared view), child blocks link to `/share/[slug]` if the target page is in the map, otherwise show a lock icon and disable navigation.
- `ChildBlockView` — Node view: icon + title link (calls `onImmediateSave` before nav; disabled in shared view when not shared) + lock icon for unshared children. Drag/delete/duplicate are handled by the global `BlockDragHandle` (gutter); the inline grip and × delete were removed. Reads `shareMap` from extension options via `editor.extensionManager`.
- `PageMentionExtension` / `PageMentionList` — Inline page links: typing `@` opens a searchable picker of existing pages/databases; selecting one inserts a `pageLink` atomic node (not an editable mark).
- `EmojiExtension` (`EmojiSuggestion`) / `EmojiList` / `emojiData.ts` — Inline emoji picker: typing `:` opens a searchable list (curated `EMOJI_LIST` of ~300 `{emoji,name,keywords}` entries, no external dataset dependency), filtered by gemoji-style short name or keyword; selecting one (click/Enter/Tab) replaces `:query` with the plain emoji character — round-trips through markdown as ordinary unicode text, no special node/serialization needed. Same `@tiptap/suggestion` + `tippy` pattern as `PageMention`, distinct `PluginKey('emojiSuggestion')`. i18n: `Editor.emojiPickerEmpty`.
- `PageLinkNode` — Atomic inline node (`pageLink`, `atom:true`) for `@` page links: label is non-editable and a single Backspace removes the whole link. Renders `<a data-page-link href=…>` and serializes to inline HTML so it round-trips via @tiptap/markdown's inline-HTML token handling (re-parses back into the node, not a link mark). Plain typed/pasted URLs remain ordinary editable link marks.
- `PagePickerPanel` / `pagePicker.ts` — Block "Link to page" picker (own search box, tippy-anchored at cursor); `openPagePicker(editor)` inserts a link-only `childBlock`. Arrow/Enter/Escape are handled via a **document capture-phase keydown listener** so navigation works even while ProseMirror still holds focus (the editor keeps focus when the picker is opened from a slash command); the search input is focus-grabbed across a few `requestAnimationFrame`s to win that race.
- `pageLinkData.ts` — Client-side cached index (30 s TTL) of all linkable workspace items via `getAllWorkspaceItems`; `searchPageItems(query)` + `pageLinkHref(item)`. Shared by the `@` mention and the block picker.
- `BubbleMenuBar` — Selection toolbar (Bold/Italic/Strike/Code/H1–H3 + "Turn into"). Uses anchor probe to self-position inside transformed ancestors (peek modals). Hidden when `BlockSelection` has an active selection.
- `BlockSelectionExtension` (`BlockSelectionExtension.ts`) — ProseMirror plugin for Notion-style multi-block selection. State: `{ selected: number[], anchor: number | null }` keyed by `blockSelectionKey`. **Two distinct selection modes coexist (the Notion model):** (1) dragging **over text** = normal native text selection (untouched, so you can still bold/color a run); (2) dragging from an **empty area** = a **rubber-band / marquee (selection-area)** that selects blocks by rectangle intersection — like a desktop file-area drag. The marquee lives in the plugin's `view(editorView)` (not `handleDOMEvents`, because margin mousedowns never reach `.ProseMirror`): it attaches a `mousedown` listener to the editor's nearest scroll ancestor (`findScrollParent`), and **only starts when the mousedown lands in an empty zone** — the left margin (`x < pmRect.left`), right margin (`x > pmRect.right`), or below the last block — within the padded content column (`findColumn` = nearest ancestor wider than the editor) and the editor's vertical span. It draws a `.block-marquee` fixed-position overlay on `<body>` (viewport coords) and, per `mousemove` (rAF-throttled), selects every visual block whose `nodeDOM` rect intersects the marquee (`rectsIntersect` AABB). `document.body.block-marquee-active` suppresses native `user-select` during the drag. **Scroll-aware marquee:** the actual scroll container is resolved **fresh on each marquee mousedown** (`scroller = findScrollParent(editorView.dom)`), NOT once at plugin mount — at mount tiptap's editor DOM often isn't yet placed under the page's `.overflow-auto` wrapper, so `findScrollParent` (which now matches on overflow style ALONE, not `scrollHeight > clientHeight`) would wrongly fall through to `documentElement`. Two consequences are handled: (1) the start anchor is shifted by `scroller.scrollTop/Left − startScroll*` on every update so the marquee stays glued to the **content** beneath it (scrolling mid-drag no longer slides the selection along the viewport); (2) when the pointer sits within `EDGE` (56px) of the container's top/bottom, an rAF loop **auto-scrolls** the container (speed ∝ edge depth, max 22px/frame) and re-runs the marquee each frame, so a drag extends past the visible area. Keyboard/click affordances still apply: shift+click on a block extends the selection (`handleDOMEvents.mousedown`); a plain click inside text clears it; `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` grows/shrinks from the anchor; `Delete`/`Backspace` deletes all selected blocks; `Escape` selects the current block (none selected) or clears (selection active). Visual blocks: top-level non-list nodes + individual `listItem`/`taskItem` nodes. Applies `Decoration.node(..., { class: 'block-selected' })` per selected position.
- `BlockSelectionToolbar` (`BlockSelectionToolbar.tsx`) — Floating toolbar rendered when `BlockSelection` has blocks selected (positioned above the first selected block via `view.coordsAtPos`). **Mirrors the `BubbleMenuBar` formatting set so block selection has the same text-editor affordances**: count badge (`blockSelectionCount`), Bold/Italic/Strike/Code, a text-color + highlight panel (`TEXT_COLORS`/`HIGHLIGHT_COLORS`), a "Turn into" dropdown (8 block types), then Copy/Duplicate/Delete. **Marks apply to the whole block range** via `applyMarks(mutate)`: it builds a TextSelection spanning all selected blocks (inside-first → end-of-last), runs the tiptap chain, then **collapses the text selection and re-stamps the block selection meta in the same transaction** so the block decorations survive (no lingering native highlight, BubbleMenuBar stays hidden). Active state per mark comes from `doc.rangeHasMark`. "Turn into" (`applyBlockType`) shifts positions, so it clears the block selection afterward. Copy (`copySelected`, `Copy` icon) serializes the selected nodes **to markdown** and writes it via `navigator.clipboard.writeText`, then clears the selection; Duplicate (`CopyPlus` icon) inserts each copy after its own original; Delete removes the blocks. **Copy/Delete share one implementation with the keyboard:** `serializeBlockSelectionMarkdown(editor)` and `deleteBlockSelection(view)` are exported from `BlockSelectionExtension` and used by both the toolbar buttons AND a **document-level capture `keydown` listener** in the plugin's `view()` that handles Ctrl/Cmd+C / Ctrl/Cmd+X over a block selection (it's document-level, not `handleDOMEvents.copy`, because the marquee mousedown `preventDefault`s focus — the editor usually isn't focused so the copy/cut events never reach `view.dom`; the listener uses the async clipboard API and is gated on `hasBlockSelection`) and the Delete/Backspace key handler. `deleteBlockSelection` uses `tr.deleteRange` (not `delete`) so an emptied parent (e.g. the last listItem) is removed too — a raw delete left an invalid empty list that crashed the next transaction. i18n: adds `blockCopy` (Copy) to the `Editor` namespace; reuses `bubble*`/`slash*` labels.
- `BlockDragHandle` — Notion-style left gutter handle. Rendered by `BlockEditor` only when `editable`. Tracks the hovered block (via `blockAt` → `posAtCoords` sampled at the pointer's Y) and pins a `GripVertical` button to its left. Targets the **innermost list/task item** when the cursor is inside one (each bullet/checkbox is its own draggable block), otherwise the top-level block. Handle is positioned against the target node's own DOM (`view.nodeDOM`), so it follows list indentation. **Drag** reorders the block by handing ProseMirror a `NodeSelection` slice (`view.dragging = { slice, move: true }`) so its native drop handler performs the move. **Click** opens a menu: Copy · Duplicate · Delete · "Turn into" (**right-clicking anywhere in the editor opens this same menu at the cursor**, suppressing the native browser menu — `contextmenu` listener on `editor.view.dom`, fine-pointer only, skips `input`/`textarea` targets so node-view fields keep their native menu; the grip stays hidden when opened this way via a `menuAnchor` cursor point. Esc/outside-click closes. **Selection-aware `target`** resolved at open time, most-specific first: a non-empty `TextSelection` → `{kind:'range',from,to}` (Cut/Copy/Duplicate/Delete act on the exact range, not the whole block); an active `BlockSelection` marquee → `{kind:'blocks',positions}` (acts on those blocks; Turn-into hidden — the floating block toolbar covers it); else `{kind:'block',pos}` under the cursor. Grip-click always targets the block. `Cut` shows only for range/blocks. Copy serializes via `nodesToCleanMarkdown` (block) / `serializeBlockSelectionMarkdown` (blocks) / `textBetween` (range)) — the last is a **flyout submenu** (opens to the side, flips left near the viewport edge) listing the 8 block types (same set as `BubbleMenuBar`). Hover-tracking listens on `document` with a left-gutter hover zone (not `mouseleave`) so the pointer can travel from block → handle without a dead gap. An **occlusion probe** (`elementFromPoint` on the hovered block) suppresses the handle when a modal/overlay covers the editor, so handles never float over modals regardless of z-index. On **headings** the handle is pushed further left (−48px vs −28px) to clear the `.heading-collapse-btn` chevron (`left:-22px`). The media/embed atoms (`bookmarkBlock`/`imageBlock`/`calloutBlock`/`fileBlock`/`youtubeEmbed`) **defer to the global handle** — their node views no longer render their own `data-drag-handle` grip, so there's one grip that both drags and opens the menu. **"Turn into" is type-aware:** text/list blocks convert among the 8 text types; the **link-type atoms** (`bookmarkBlock`/`imageBlock`/`youtubeEmbed`/`fileBlock` — `MEDIA_TYPES`) convert **among themselves**, carrying the URL across (`mediaUrlOf` → `mediaAttrsFor`, via `setNodeMarkup`; YouTube uses `extractYouTubeId`). `calloutBlock` (atom, not URL-based) shows no "Turn into". **`childBlock` also uses the global handle** — its node view no longer renders an inline grip/× (icon now aligns with surrounding text via `-mx-1 px-1`); the handle's **Delete** runs childBlock-aware logic (link-only → drop reference; owned sub-page → also `deleteWorkspaceItem`, with a confirm dialog — rendered via `createPortal` independently of `handle` so it survives occlusion-hide — when `checkItemHasContent`), and **Duplicate** forces the copy to `linkOnly` (can't clone the page). In shared view the global handle isn't rendered, so childBlocks show just icon + title (+ lock when unshared). i18n keys in `Editor`: `blockHandleTooltip`, `blockDuplicate`, `blockDelete` (+ reuses `bubbleTurnInto`/`slash*` labels). Replaced the old orphaned `BlockTypeMenu` (deleted). **Touch / coarse-pointer mode** (`useSyncExternalStore` on `(hover: none)`): hover-tracking and HTML5 drag don't work on touch, so the handle becomes a tap-driven **`⋮` (MoreVertical)** button anchored to the **caret's block** via `selectionUpdate`/`focus`/`scroll` (not mousemove), the blur-hide is skipped, and `onMouseDown` preventDefault keeps focus so the tap doesn't dismiss it. The menu gains **Move up / Move down** (`moveBlock` — delete+insert sibling swap, since DnD can't reorder on touch) and, on headings, **Collapse/expand** (`toggleHeadingCollapse`) — so the always-on heading chevron is dropped on touch (`globals.css` `@media (hover: none)`: editor gets a `padding-left` gutter for the `⋮`, and `.heading-collapse-btn` is `pointer-events: none`). i18n: `blockMoveUp`/`blockMoveDown`/`blockToggleCollapse`.
- `clipboardMarkdown.ts` — **Shared clipboard copy serializer.** The editor's STORAGE serializer emits atom blocks (callout/image/bookmark/file/youtube/childBlock/pageLink) as `<div data-*>`/`<a data-page-link>` HTML so they round-trip on reload — correct for storage, ugly on the clipboard. `cleanupCopiedMarkdown(md)` post-processes that storage markdown into clipboard-friendly markdown (callout → blockquote, image → `![](…)`, bookmark/file → `[label](url)`, youtube → watch URL, child/pageLink → label text) and **normalizes whitespace** (drops `&nbsp;`-only lines, collapses 3+ newlines to one blank line, trims) so a paste never has doubled blank lines. `fragmentToCleanMarkdown(editor, fragment)` (used by `BlockEditor`'s `clipboardTextSerializer` → native text-selection copy/cut) and `nodesToCleanMarkdown(editor, nodes)` (block selection / single-block copy) both route through it, so **every copy path** — native text selection, marquee block selection (`serializeBlockSelectionMarkdown`), and the drag-handle menu — produces identical clean markdown.
- `SlashCommandMenu` — `/` trigger; reads `workspaceId`/`parentId` from extension manager dynamically, not from closure.
- `SlashCommandList` — Keyboard-navigable command list rendered as three divider-separated groups: basic blocks · media (`MEDIA_IDS`: video/image/callout/bookmark/file) · pages (child blocks). Exports `SLASH_KEYWORDS` (id → synonyms like `h1`/`img`/`todo`); `SlashCommandMenu`'s suggestion filter matches the English label, the id, **and** these keywords so typed shortcuts (`/h1`, `/img`) resolve. Child group: "Link to page" (`child-link`, opens `openPagePicker`), "Page", "Database".

**Marketing (`src/components/marketing/`)**
- `LandingBridgeSwitcher` — Full landing page composition (pure server component, no auth check). Used by `page.tsx`. `LandingKnowledge` is rendered after `LandingWhy` so the OKF/context value is explained before client integrations and tool details.
- `LandingNav` — Sticky header; "Go to app" → `/app` for authed, Sign in / Get started for guests. Nav links (lg+): Integrations, **Resources** (`ResourcesNavDropdown` — see below), Pricing, Download. Includes `<PwaNavButton />` (opens `PwaInstallModal`; icon-only below lg) + `<LandingThemeToggle />` (light/dark) + `<LanguageSwitcher variant="header" />`. `LandingFooter`'s Protocol column links to `/wiki/*`, its Blog column to `/docs/*`. Below the column grid, a bordered strip centers a rounded "Crafted by **Akatron Network**" badge (`Landing.bridgeFooterCraftedBy`, rendered via `t.rich` — the bold name is embedded per-locale so word order stays natural in all 8 languages) with the real Akatron mark (`public/akatron-logo.png`, downloaded from `akatron.net`) linking to `https://akatron.net`; the plain-text Akatron mention that used to live in `bridgeFooterCopyright` was moved out into this badge, leaving that key as just the `© {year} Remnus. Open source.` line.
- `ResourcesNavDropdown` — client popup combining **Wiki** + **Docs** behind one "Resources" nav item (opens on hover with a short close-delay so the pointer can travel into the panel, and toggles on click for keyboard/touch; closes on outside-click/Escape). Panel lists both with an icon (`BookOpen`/`Newspaper`) + one-line description. i18n: `Landing.bridgeNavResources` + `bridgeNavResourcesWikiDesc`/`DocsDesc`; reuses `bridgeNavWiki`/`bridgeNavDocs` as item titles.
- `LandingThemeToggle` — Client component. Sun/Moon button in the marketing nav that toggles `data-theme` between `remnus` (dark) and `catppuccin` (light) + persists via `setTheme`. Tracks the live `<html data-theme>` via `useSyncExternalStore` + MutationObserver (no local state, SSR-safe). Both `LandingBridgeSwitcher` and `MarketingShell` expose the shared `.marketing-site` scope: in Catppuccin, `globals.css` remaps only public marketing routes (including body-portaled modal/banner chrome via `body:has(.marketing-site)`) to an editorial white palette, ink text, alternating near-white section bands, a translucent sticky nav, light-surface preview shadows, and dark monochrome Cursor/Notion marks—leaving the authenticated workspace palette untouched. The blue wash is scoped to `.marketing-hero` (not every marketing page's first section). Pricing-specific light overrides keep every `.pricing-plan-card` white, reserve the outlined shadow for `.pricing-card-featured`, and render `.pricing-comparison-featured` as a near-white column with fine blue edges instead of a gray fill. Existing targeted fixes remain: `.hero-hover-dim` and `hero-ai-tile-light`.
- `LandingHero` / `LandingWhy` / `LandingWhatsInside` / `LandingIntegrations` / `LandingSetup` / `LandingTools` / `LandingPricing` / `LandingDownload` — Landing sections 01–09 + `LandingClosing` + `LandingFooter`. `LandingDownload` ([src/components/marketing/LandingDownload.tsx](src/components/marketing/LandingDownload.tsx)) — dual platform card: phone/tablet PWA install (with real mobile screenshot peeking out of a phone frame) + desktop native app card (desktop screenshot), bridges to `/download#mobile-install` and `/download` respectively. i18n: `Landing.bridgeDownload*` keys. **`LandingTools`'s `TOOLS`/`RESOURCES`/`PROMPTS` arrays mirror the live MCP catalog — FULLY SYNCED 2026-09-23: `TOOLS` = 27 (11 read incl. `get_pages` + 16 write, incl. `bulk_create_pages`/`create_database_view`/`update_database_view`/`delete_database_view`/`add_comment`/`bulk_delete_pages`/`bulk_move_items`/`create_dashboard`/`update_dashboard`), `RESOURCES` = 7 (incl. `remnus://dashboard/catalog`), `PROMPTS` = 7 (`save-memory`+`recall-context` added Faz 4). Nothing enforces this sync — they're only used for the `.length` counts and (for `RESOURCES`/`PROMPTS`) their `.uri`/`.name` chip labels.** Redesigned 2026-07-30: the old three full data tables (every tool/resource/prompt as a table row with scope/desc/returns columns) were replaced with a landing-appropriate summary — a 6-card `FLAGSHIP_TOOLS` grid (curated subset spanning read+write, own `bridgeToolsFlagship*` i18n keys + a lucide icon each) plus compact Resources/Prompts cards showing only a short tagline and a chip cloud of names/URIs, no per-item descriptions. The per-tool/resource/prompt `bridgeToolsDesc*`/`bridgeResourcesDesc*`/`bridgePromptsDesc*` and table-header `bridge*Col*`/`bridgeToolsReturn*` i18n keys were deleted (all 8 locales) since nothing renders them anymore; full per-item descriptions still live on `/wiki/read-tools`, `/wiki/resources`, `/wiki/prompts` (linked from each card). Whenever a tool/resource/prompt is added or removed, bump: the array in `LandingTools.tsx` (and, if it's flagship-worthy, `FLAGSHIP_TOOLS` + a new `bridgeToolsFlagship*` key, all 8 locales), the count strings `Landing.bridgeToolsH2Part1`/`bridgeResourcesH2Part1`/`bridgeToolsFooterText` (all 8 locales), the open-source pricing card `Landing.bridgePricingSelfF3` (all 8 locales), and the root `README.md` (feature bullet + `## MCP Tools` table). `docs/mcp/README.md` + `read-tools.md`/`resources.md`/`prompts.md` are the wiki-side equivalents (these carry the full per-item descriptions now, since the landing page no longer does), and the `docs/blog/remnus-vs-*.md` comparison posts cite the raw counts in prose (27 tools · 11 read · 16 write · 7 resources · 7 prompts — synced 2026-09-23, as are `connect-claude-code-to-remnus-mcp.md` and `what-is-an-mcp-native-workspace.md`) — keep every side in sync. **Hero CTA hierarchy:** the old inline workspace/integrations links and MCP count strip were removed; the left column now leads with the large `HeroDemoButton`, followed by the lower-emphasis `/login` “start free” button. **`LandingSetup` redesigned 2026-07-30** (rendered right after `LandingIntegrations` in `LandingBridgeSwitcher.tsx`, before `LandingWhatsInside` — was previously between `WhatsInside` and `Tools`): the old full numbered section (eyebrow "05 / Setup", "Three steps. No API keys." header, 3-card step grid) was redundant with `LandingIntegrations`, which already pitches the same "connect in under 60s" story via its intro copy and a per-client `SetupGuideModal` trigger. It's now a single compact, unnumbered callout strip (icon + `bridgeSetupStripTitle`/`Body` — leads with the actually-differentiated claim, "no API keys," instead of the generic "3 steps") that reuses the same `SetupGuideModal` for anyone who wants the click-through step-by-step, so the detailed walkthrough is opt-in rather than forced on every visitor. The old `bridgeSetupSnum`/`bridgeSetupCaption`/`bridgeSetupH2*`/`bridgeSetupStep`/`bridgeSetup{1,2,3}{Title,Body}`/`bridgeSetupCode2` i18n keys were deleted (all 8 locales); `bridgeSetupModal*` (used by `SetupGuideModal`) and `bridgeIntSetupLink` (reused as the strip's link label) were untouched. **Section numbering renumbered 2026-07-30** to close the gap this left (Setup's "05" going away made the visible eyebrow sequence jump 04→06, which read as broken): `bridgeToolsSnum` "06/MCP"→**"05/MCP"**, `bridgePricingSnum` "07"→**"06"**, `bridgeDownloadSnum` "08"→**"07"** (all 8 locales) — current sequence is Hero=01, Why=02, Integrations=03, WhatsInside=04, Tools=05, Pricing=06, Download=07 (Setup unnumbered, sits between Integrations and WhatsInside). `bridgeResourcesSnum` ("07/Resources") is dead/unused (nothing renders it — left as-is, not part of the visible sequence). If a numbered section is ever added/removed again, renumber every `bridge*Snum` after it to keep this sequence continuous. **Gotcha fixed 2026-07-30, three passes — settled on a theme-scoped override, not a universal re-tint:** the strip's card originally used a flat `bg-neutral-900` fill, which is the *correct* look in every dark theme — it only falls apart in catppuccin (light), where `--color-neutral-900` (`#f8f9fb`) lands almost exactly on `[data-theme="catppuccin"] .marketing-site main > section:nth-of-type(even) { background: #f8fafc }` (LandingSetup sits at an even position), so the card disappeared into a grey smear with no legible edge. Passes 1–2 tried fixing this by re-tinting the fill/border/divider with teal *unconditionally in the component* (first a faint 6% tint + a decorative radial-gradient glow — still read as "grey"/the glow looked like an accidental cut; then a stronger 10%/28% tint baked into `LandingSetup.tsx`'s inline styles) — but that also changed the dark themes' look, which didn't need fixing and read worse for it (over-colored border). **Final fix:** `LandingSetup.tsx` went back to plain `bg-neutral-900 border-neutral-800` (classes `setup-strip-card`/`setup-strip-divider` added as override hooks, no inline tinting), and the teal-tinted treatment lives *only* as a `[data-theme="catppuccin"] .marketing-site .setup-strip-card`/`.setup-strip-divider` rule in `globals.css` (next to the other catppuccin-only overrides) — so every dark theme keeps its original untouched look, and only catppuccin gets the fill/border tint it actually needed. **Lesson: a fix for one theme's contrast problem belongs in a theme-scoped CSS override (matching the existing `[data-theme="catppuccin"] .marketing-site ...` pattern in `globals.css`), not in the component's shared inline styles** — inline styles apply to every theme at once, so "fixing" one theme there risks visibly regressing the others, exactly as happened here. Note the decorative radial-gradient glow div (`absolute inset-0`, `radial-gradient(480px circle at 6% 30%, color-mix(in oklab, var(--color-opt-teal) 12%, transparent), transparent 70%)`) that pass 2 removed (thinking it was the "cut off" culprit) was restored afterward — it was never actually the problem (the flat neutral-900-vs-section-background collision was), and it's a liked visual detail independent of the contrast fix; it now sits back in `LandingSetup.tsx` on top of the plain `setup-strip-card` base, unaffected by (and compatible with) the catppuccin-only tint override.
- **Landing MCP context demo:** `LandingTools` includes a localized Context v2 walkthrough with the 2k Smart starting budget and honest Smart/Strict behavior. Its counts are illustrative, not a live MCP request or measured savings claim; keys are `Landing.bridgeContextDemo*` in all 8 locales.
- **Landing OKF knowledge section:** `LandingKnowledge.tsx` is the dedicated public product narrative for portable OKF v0.2 interchange, exact-revision human review, Context Pack v2, and Smart/Strict behavior. It links to `/wiki/context-first`; copy is in `Landing.bridgeKnowledge*` across all 8 locales. The current visible section sequence is Hero=01, Why=02, Knowledge=03, Integrations=04, WhatsInside=05, Tools=06, Pricing=07, Download=08; `LandingSetup` stays unnumbered. This current sequence supersedes the older numbering history retained above.
- **Hero integrations link:** below the primary demo and secondary `/login` buttons, `LandingHero` renders a quiet tertiary `#integrations` text link; the existing global smooth-scroll behavior carries visitors directly to `LandingIntegrations`.
- `HeroDemoOverlay` — Client module exporting both `HeroDemoButton` (the persistent primary CTA in `LandingHero`'s left copy column) and `HeroDemoOverlay` (hover-reveal CTA centered over the desktop workspace screenshot). Both use `useActionState(loginAsDemo)` and show a spinner while the server action runs.
- `WhatsInsideViewer` — Client component. Auto-cycling Kanban/Table/Calendar viewer (4 s). All strings passed as props. `footer1` is a `[string, string, string]` tuple (one per panel), rendered via `footer1[active]` (key'd on `active` so it re-triggers `animate-fade-in`) — was a single static string until 2026-07-30, when `LandingWhatsInside` was reframed to tie back to the Hero's "your AI creates pages, designs kanban boards and updates your calendar" promise: each panel now shows a per-view attribution line mixing a human ("you") and a named MCP client (Claude/Cursor) — e.g. Board="Card moved by Claude · 2m ago", Table="Row updated by you · just now", Calendar="Rescheduled by Cursor · 5m ago" (`Landing.bridgeInsideFrameFooter1{Board,Table,Calendar}`, all 8 locales; replaces the old single `bridgeInsideFrameFooter1`). The section's H2/body/caption/callout copy were also reframed the same way (from "Build it any way you want" — generic multi-view flexibility — to "Every view your agent already uses" — same views, human + agent), and the two MCP-syntax chips in the Pages card (`remnus://pages/<id>`, `page.update_content`) got an explanatory label (`bridgeInsidePagesChipLabel`, new key) since they were previously unlabeled decoration a first-time visitor couldn't parse. **Why:** the section sat right after Hero's AI-agent promise but described itself in purely human/Notion-clone terms with no MCP/agent tie-in — a narrative gap between the promise and the payoff.
- `SetupGuideModal` — Client component. MCP connection steps modal with endpoint + auth header snippets. All strings passed as props. Rendered per-client inside each `LandingIntegrations` card AND once inside the compact `LandingSetup` strip (same component, same `bridgeSetupModal*` props, two call sites). Optional `variant?: 'link' | 'button'` prop (default `'link'`) controls the trigger's own styling: `'link'` is the quiet inline mono text link used in the Integrations cards (unchanged); `'button'` is a bordered pill CTA used standalone in `LandingSetup`, where the trigger isn't stacked under body copy so the link variant's built-in `mt-4.5` would misalign it.
- `DownloadView` — Client component for `/download`. Detects OS (`navigator`) — **mobile first: Android before Linux (Android UAs contain "linux") and iOS incl. iPadOS-as-Mac (`maxTouchPoints`)**. Desktop OSes get a smart primary "Download for {os}" button + full platform grid (Windows .exe, macOS Apple Silicon/Intel .dmg, Linux .AppImage/.deb); mobile OSes get a PWA install primary CTA (native `beforeinstallprompt` prompt when available, else an anchor to `#mobile-install`). The **"Phone & tablet — web app" section** (`#mobile-install`) shows an install button (`triggerInstallPrompt` from [src/lib/pwa/installPrompt.ts](src/lib/pwa/installPrompt.ts)), an installed badge when running standalone, and iOS/Android add-to-home-screen instructions. i18n: `Download.pwa*` keys (all 8 locales). Links target `github.com/Ranork/remnus-app/releases/latest/download/<stable-name>` — never needs per-release updates, but only resolves once the draft release is published. Stable-named assets are produced by the "Upload stable-named installers" step in `tauri-release.yml`.
- `MarketingShell` — Layout wrapper wrapping the public marketing pages (`/pricing`, `/contact`, `/download`, `/privacy`, `/security`, `/brand`, `/wiki`, `/docs`) with the unified LandingNav and LandingFooter.
- `ContactSection` — `/contact` page body (server). 3 channel cards (GitHub / email `info@remnus.com` / community-coming-soon) + `<ContactForm />`. `ContactForm` — client component below it, posts to `submitContactForm()`. See **Contact Form** (under **Mailing System**) for the spam-protection design.
- `src/components/docs/` — **Wiki + Docs (blog) presentation.** `WikiShell` (server two-column grid; subtle blue radial glow on an isolated `absolute inset-0 overflow-hidden` layer — kept off the section itself so it never sits on an ancestor of the sticky sidebar, which can break `position: sticky` in some browsers), `WikiSidebar` (client — flat nav tree, `usePathname` active state, mobile collapsible; icons resolved locally from `@/lib/content/manifest`'s `WIKI_PAGES` rather than received as a prop, since Lucide icon components are functions and can't cross the server→client RSC boundary), `WikiToc` (client — "On this page" scrollspy via IntersectionObserver), `WikiArticle` (breadcrumb + title + prose + TOC), `BreadcrumbTrail` (shared visible breadcrumb — deliberately mirrors the `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD built in `src/lib/content/seo.ts`), `DocsProse` (server — renders trusted repo HTML with `.prose-doc`), `BlogCard` (blog index card), `DocsArticleFooter` (prev/next + back), `CompetitorMark.tsx` (`NotionMark`/`AppFlowyMark`/`AffineMark`/`ObsidianMark` — inline-SVG brand marks for "Remnus vs X" comparison posts, each rendered in its own real brand color rather than the generic blue every other `/docs` icon uses; Notion/AFFiNE/Obsidian paths + hex sourced verbatim from Simple Icons' `data/simple-icons.json` (Notion's official `#000000` is swapped for neutral-100 `#cccccc` for dark-bg visibility, the same treatment `AIMark.tsx` gives other monochrome brands; AFFiNE uses its real `#1E96EB`, Obsidian its real `#7C3AED`), AppFlowy's flower mark is pulled verbatim from its own repo assets and keeps its official per-petal colors; used only to identify the product being compared, not to imply affiliation). i18n: `Docs` namespace. **Wiki icons are Lucide component references, not emoji** (`WikiPage.icon`) — see `manifest.ts` below; **blog post icons** (`BlogPost.icon`) can be a Lucide icon OR one of these brand marks, both render as `<Icon size={n} />` so they're interchangeable in `BlogCard`/the article header.
- `HeroSection` / `FeaturesSection` / `PricingSection` / `ContactSection` — Legacy marketing shell components.
- `LandingChip` / `AIMark` — Utility: status pill, AI client SVG marks.
- `mini/` — Static mini previews: `KanbanMini`, `TableMini`, `CalendarMini`, `MarkdownPageMini`, `ViewTab`. Since 2026-07-30, `KanbanMini`/`CalendarMini`/`MarkdownPageMini` render real brand marks via `AIMark` (not bare text) for their one "touched by an agent" item, each in a small colored-circle badge matching `TableMini`'s pre-existing assignee-avatar visual language (also reused: the `EA`/`CK`/`SK` initials + purple/teal/yellow colors are shared across `KanbanMini` and `TableMini` so it reads as the same team across views). **Keep the agent/row/card attribution in sync with `WhatsInsideViewer`'s per-panel `footer1` copy** — Board's card (`agent: 'claude'` on "Inline cell editor") must match `bridgeInsideFrameFooter1Board` ("Card moved by Claude"), Calendar's event (`agent: 'cursor'` on day-22 "Team demo") must match `bridgeInsideFrameFooter1Calendar` ("Rescheduled by Cursor"); `TableMini`'s highlighted row is intentionally attributed to a human (pulsing green "now" badge, not an agent) to match `bridgeInsideFrameFooter1Table` ("Row updated by **you**") — this was a real mismatch before the fix (the row showed a "· claude" tag while the footer said "by you"). The mix (2 panels agent-attributed, 1 human) is deliberate: it's a *Human-Agent Collaborative* workspace, not an agent-only one.

**Other**
- `src/lib/client-auth-store.ts` — In-memory store for pending desktop OAuth tokens. `setPendingClientToken(deviceId, token)` writes; `consumeClientToken(deviceId)` reads + deletes (one-time use, 5-min TTL). Single-process only — swap for a DB table in multi-instance deployments.
- `src/lib/recurrence/rule.ts` — **Recurrence rule model + expansion engine.** An RFC 5545 (RRULE) subset stored as JSON: `freq`/`interval`/`byWeekday`/`monthlyMode`/`byMonthDay`/`bySetPos`/`byMonth`/`skipWeekends`/`end`/`startDate`/`exDates`. Pure functions, no `rrule` dependency (~40 kB saved; the subset is ~250 lines). Key exports: `expandOccurrences(rule, {from,to,limit})` — **always windowed**, because a `{freq:'daily', end:'never'}` rule has no last occurrence; `normalizeRule` (fills every default, returns null on a bad start date); `endRuleBefore` (the THISANDFUTURE split's first half); `countOccurrences` (the "this will create N cards" preview); `parseDateValue`/`buildDateValue` (a series repeats the date value's *shape* — time of day and duration — not its literal end date). Semantics worth knowing: `COUNT` counts **generated** occurrences, so an EXDATE'd one is not backfilled from the future; monthly `dayOfMonth: 31` **skips** short months (RFC behavior — `monthlyMode:'lastDay'` is the explicit clamp opt-in), as does Feb 29 yearly; dates are local wall-clock strings so a 09:00 series stays 09:00 across DST. Both the candidate generator and the outer loop carry step budgets — a rule like `{freq:'yearly', byMonth:2, byMonthDay:30}` never fires and would otherwise spin forever. Covered by `npm run test:recurrence` (26 assertions).
- `src/lib/recurrence/summary.ts` — `formatRuleSummary(rule, t, locale)` → "Every 2 weeks on Tue and Thu · until 12 Dec 2026". Composed from small ICU fragments rather than one message per rule shape (4 frequencies × 3 monthly modes × 3 end conditions × 8 locales is not maintainable). Weekday names come from `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, list joining from `Intl.ListFormat` — no 8 × 7 weekday message keys.
- `src/lib/services/recurrence.ts` — Cookie-free domain service for recurring cards. `materializeSeries` (idempotent; the unique index is the backstop), `topUpDatabaseSeries` (**read-triggered**, which is what makes open-ended series work with no cron at all — including self-hosted Docker), `createSeriesFromPage`, `splitSeriesAt` (THISANDFUTURE), `replaceSeriesRule` (ALL), `deleteOccurrences` (the three-way delete), `detachOccurrence`, `getScopeImpact`, `pruneEmptySeries`. **The load-bearing idea is `findDirtyOccurrenceIds`:** an occurrence someone actually touched (body written, title/properties diverged from the template, sub-page nested, link in/out, `agentEditedAt` set, dragged to a different day than `occurrence_date`) is preserved and detached instead of regenerated away. Unlike Google Calendar — where an occurrence is nearly empty and deleting/recreating it is harmless — a Remnus occurrence is a full page that may already hold a customer's work. Sub-item and link checks are batched (two queries for the whole set), never per card. `deleteOccurrences` RETURNS ids rather than deleting: the action layer owns the tombstone/`purgeReferencesTo`/`removePageLinksFor` cleanup.
- `src/lib/actions/recurrence.ts` — Session-aware wrappers (`assertDatabaseAccess` + realtime publish + `revalidatePath`). `loadRecurrenceState(databaseId, windowEnd)` is the one the calendar calls on every window change: it tops up materialization to the visible window and returns the rule map for card badges. `cleanUpDeletedPages` replays exactly what `deletePage` does for a single row — skipping it would leave orphaned sub-pages and dead child-block buttons.
- `src/lib/changelog.ts` — **Single source of truth for the in-app What's New panel.** Ordered newest-first `ChangelogEntry[]` (stable `YYYY-MM-DD-kebab-topic` id, ship date, `new`/`improved`/`fixed` category, `title` + one-sentence `summary`), plus `localizedText`, `newestEntryId`, `countUnseenEntries` and the `CHANGELOG_SEEN_COOKIE` name. In the repo rather than the DB on purpose: a release note belongs to the commit that shipped the feature, so there is no drift between what shipped and what the panel claims, and no migration/admin CMS. `title`/`summary` are `Record<Locale, string>` — a missing locale is a **`tsc` error**, not a silent English fallback. **Every agent must add an entry for any user-visible change, unprompted** — the writing rules (one sentence, customer language, newest first, never rewrite a shipped id) live in `AI.md` → `## Changelog / What's New` and are echoed in `CLAUDE.md`.
