- `src/lib/dashboard/schema.ts` — **The dashboard spec: zod, versioned, and the ONE source of truth** for the renderer and the MCP write tools alike. Block union (`metric`/`chart`/`database_embed`/`list`/`text`/`links`/`activity`), `validateDashboardSpec` (strict write gate), `parseDashboardSpec` (lenient per-block read), `blockWidth`, and `DASHBOARD_BLOCK_CATALOG` — the agent-facing descriptions, deliberately beside the shapes they describe. See **Dashboards**.
- `src/lib/dashboard/catalog.ts` — The agent-facing block catalog (`renderDashboardCatalog`, served as `remnus://dashboard/catalog`): field signatures derived from the zod schemas with `z.toJSONSchema`, hand-written `NOTES` only for what a JSON schema cannot carry, and `DASHBOARD_TEMPLATES` checked against `validateDashboardSpec` before they are served.
- `src/lib/services/dashboards.ts` — The ONE dashboard write path (MCP tools + web actions): `createDashboardInWorkspace`, `patchDashboard` (add/update/replace/remove/order + title/icon), `DashboardInputError`. Validates only touched blocks, normalizes references, compare-and-swaps the stored spec, returns warnings. See **Dashboards → MCP**.
- `src/lib/services/workspaceDeletion.ts` — `deleteWorkspaceData(workspaceId)`, the ONE whole-workspace delete (series → rows → databases → comments → workspace in one batch). See **Deleting a whole workspace**.
- `src/lib/services/graph.ts` — **The knowledge map's cookie-free service** (P12; code layer P13): `getWorkspaceGraph(workspaceId, {expanded, activityDays, code})`, `getLocalGraph(workspaceId, itemId, depth)`, `findMentions` (exported for `bench:graph`), `getGraphModelStats`. Wire format + route helper in `src/lib/graph/types.ts` (client-safe, no DB import); session/lock wrappers in `src/lib/actions/graph.ts`. See **Knowledge Map**.
- `src/lib/graph/codePaths.ts` — Pure (client-safe) reading of knowledge `sources` as repo paths: `normalizeSourcePath`, `normalizeQueryPath`, `matchSource` (`exact`/`folder`/`inside`), `pathBasename`. Used by the code layer and the file → pages lookup; `npm run test:code-paths`.
- `src/lib/services/codeSources.ts` — `getPagesForResource(workspaceId, resource)`: the items whose knowledge sources cite a file/folder, one batch, matched in memory. Behind `get_related_pages` with `resource`. See **Knowledge Map → Code layer and the agent side (P13)**.
- `src/lib/dashboard/data.ts` — Cookie-free resolution of a spec into rendered block data. One database lookup and one row read for the WHOLE dashboard, filtered per block in memory; workspace scoping is what makes a foreign source unreachable rather than merely refused. Also `getDashboardSpecByItemId`.
- `src/lib/tableFilters.ts` — `applyFilters`/`applySorts`, extracted out of `DatabaseView.tsx` so the dashboard renderer (a **server** component) evaluates the identical rules. Two copies would drift, and a filter that selects different rows in a view and in a dashboard is worse than no filter.
- `src/lib/templates.ts` — 9 item templates for `TemplatePickerModal` (the 9th is **Blank Dashboard**, `category: 'dashboard'`, carrying an empty `spec` — a seeded dashboard template is impossible, since its blocks would name `databaseId`s that do not exist until the user creates them) (the 8th is **Agent Memory** — a `select` Type [Decision/Preference/Gotcha/Fact] + `multi_select` Tags + `date` Date database with a Table + "By Type" kanban view; backs the `save-memory`/`recall-context` prompts; i18n keys `Templates.agentMemory`/`agentMemoryDesc` in all 8 locales, wired into `TemplatePickerModal`'s name/desc maps).
- `src/lib/seed.ts` — `createSeedWorkspace()` (real signups) and `createDemoSeedData()` (demo) via shared `createRichWorkspaceData(userId, name, { includeAgent })`. **`includeAgent` gates the planted agent records:** the demo (`includeAgent: true`, default) ships with a "Claude AI Agent" seed token (`token_prefix = 'rmns-demo'`), a fake `agent_activity` audit history, and per-row "agent edited" badges so it tells the full AI-agent story out of the box; **real signups pass `includeAgent: false`** — same sample Paint-clone content but NO token/activity/badges (a brand-new user hasn't connected an agent, so a planted one would mislead the AI Agents panel + onboarding funnel). Cleanup for already-seeded real accounts: `src/db/cleanup-seed-agent-records.ts` (deletes `rmns-demo` tokens + cascaded activity + clears row stamps for non-demo users; demo accounts untouched; idempotent; run on both local + Turso).
- `src/lib/services/assets.ts` — Cookie-free asset accounting + Cloudinary cleanup. `recordAsset()` (insert into `uploaded_assets`, dedup on `public_id`), `deleteAssetByUrl(url, userId)` (destroy on Cloudinary by stored `public_id`+`resource_type`, delete ledger row; authorized for uploader or workspace member), `getUserStorageBytes`/`getWorkspaceStorageBytes`/`getStorageBytesForUsers`.
- `src/lib/services/workspace.ts` — Cookie-free service layer for MCP tool handlers. Exports: `searchWorkspace` (ranked FTS5 via `services/searchIndex.ts` + migration 0052, falling back to a LIKE-skeleton + GLOB accent-folded scan; one `db.batch` per path), `listWorkspaceItems` (returns `{ items, hasMore, nextCursor }` — cursor-based pagination via `sort_order + id` keyset), `getPageById`, `getAnyPageById` (auto-detects workspace item vs DB row), `getPagesByIds` (batch version of `getAnyPageById` for a specific known id list — `Promise.allSettled`, one bad id doesn't drop the rest; backs the `get_pages` tool), `getDatabasePageById`, `getDatabaseSchema` (schema only, no rows), `queryDatabaseRows` (returns `{ schema, rows, hasMore, nextCursor }` — cursor-based pagination; accepts optional `filters` + `fields` projection; the `filters` `json_each` branch reads from the `->` operator, not `json_extract`, so it doesn't choke on dequoted scalar values — see the `get_pages` tool entry above for the failure mode this fixed), `buildContentOutline` (pure markdown→outline collapse for get_page's outline mode), `getWorkspaceDigest` (compact one-line-per-item workspace map for the digest resource; guards legacy CURRENT_TIMESTAMP-as-text `updatedAt` rows via the shared `isValidDate()` helper), `getChangesSince` (returns `{ changes, hasMore, nextCursor }` — the `get_changes_since` delta-sync tool; merges workspace-item edits + database-row edits (in-memory, `isValidDate()`-guarded, like the digest) with `deleted_items` tombstones (SQL-filtered, always-valid timestamps) into one keyset-paginated, time-ordered feed), `getRelatedPages` (the `get_related_pages` tool; one `db.batch` for the subject, parent candidates, children, links and siblings, plus one batch to resolve link ids — was ~7 sequential queries — parent, children, outgoing links + backlinks from the `page_links` graph, same-database sibling rows; resolves stored target ids across all three id forms, workspace-scoped, dedupes children out of outgoing and the parent out of backlinks), `listWorkspaceMembers` (workspace_members JOIN user; role/email/joinedAt), `queryAuditLog` (agentActivity; tool/status/from/to filters; agentTokens LEFT JOIN for agentName), `createPageInWorkspace`, `updatePageById` (merges properties, never overwrites), `bulkUpdatePages`, `deleteItemFromWorkspace` (recursive cascade for workspace items; writes a `deleted_items` tombstone per deleted node), `recordDeletionTombstone` (exported best-effort tombstone insert, reused by the web-UI delete actions in `actions/workspace.ts`/`actions/page.ts`), `moveItemInWorkspace` (reparent; subtree cycle-check), `createDatabaseInWorkspace` (custom schema; title auto-prepended), `updateDatabaseSchemaById` (add/remove columns; title protected). All insert paths here (and in `actions/workspace.ts`, `actions/page.ts`, `seed.ts`) explicitly stamp `createdAt`/`updatedAt` with `new Date()` — see the createdAt gotcha under **Auth System** for why that matters.
- `src/lib/services/pageLinks.ts` — Cookie-free link-graph service for the `page_links` table. Exports: `extractPageRefs(markdown)` (pure regex extraction of `pageLink`/`childBlock` references — no Tiptap/DOM, usable from server actions, MCP handlers, and tsx scripts), `syncPageLinks(workspaceId, fromId, fromType, content)` (delete+insert re-sync for one source page; skips self-references; **best-effort** — a lost graph row degrades `get_related_pages`, never a save), `removeOutgoingPageLinks(ids)` (drops only rows whose SOURCE is a deleted item — the delete-to-Trash call), `removePageLinksFor(itemId | itemId[])` (drops rows where the item is source OR target; for items gone for good), `stripPageRefs(markdown, ids)` (pure: removes `childBlock` divs pointing at any of `ids`, and **unwraps** inline `pageLink` anchors to their plain label text rather than deleting them — they sit mid-sentence, so removing them would silently rewrite the user's prose), and `purgeReferencesTo(ids)` (rewrites every page that references a deleted item). **Why `purgeReferencesTo` exists:** `removePageLinksFor` only drops the *graph rows* (which feed the backlinks panel + `get_related_pages`) — it never touches the markdown that actually renders the embedded sub-page button, so deleting a page left a dead button behind in its parent. It **must run before** `removePageLinksFor` for the same ids, since it finds the referencing pages *through* those very rows. **Since 2026-09-25 it no longer runs at delete time** for anything that goes to the Trash: the three trash-writing delete paths — `deleteWorkspaceItemRecursive` ([actions/workspace.ts](src/lib/actions/workspace.ts)), `deletePage` ([actions/page.ts](src/lib/actions/page.ts)), and `deleteWorkspaceItemAndDescendants` / `deleteItemFromWorkspace` / `bulkDeleteItemsFromWorkspace` ([services/workspace.ts](src/lib/services/workspace.ts), MCP) — call `removeOutgoingPageLinks` once for the whole subtree/batch, and `releaseTrashedReferences` ([services/snapshots.ts](src/lib/services/snapshots.ts)) runs the purge when `purgeExpiredSnapshots` or `enforceWorkspaceSnapshotByteCaps` drops the trash copy (skipping any id that exists again). The recurrence cleanup writes no trash copy and still purges at once. A deleted **database** passes THREE id forms — its workspace-item id (childBlock `data-cb-id`), its `databases.id` (a `/db/<id>` href), and every one of its row ids — because links can target any of them. See the `page_links` row in **Database Tables** for the write-path call sites.
- `src/components/providers/QueryProvider.tsx` — TanStack Query provider (staleTime 60s, gcTime 5min).
- **Browser-style tabs (Tauri desktop only)** — Notion-like top tab strip with **real keep-alive** (switching tabs preserves each tab's full in-memory state — open modals, scroll, unsaved edits — like browser tabs; no reload). **Only rendered inside the Tauri shell** (web is unchanged — users open OS browser tabs there). Gated via `src/lib/hooks/useIsTauri.ts` (`'__TAURI_INTERNALS__' in window` check, SSR-safe). **How keep-alive works:** the App Router unmounts a route on navigation, so content can't survive a tab switch through it. Instead, in Tauri the `(app)` content routes (`/db/<id>`, `/db/<id>/<pageId>`, `/page/<itemId>`) **render `null`** (detected server-side via the `remnus_platform` cookie set by `/tauri-app`; see `src/lib/server/platform.ts`) and the client **`TabHost`** owns the content area, mounting one pane per open tab (only the active one shown, the rest `display:none` but kept mounted). **Lazy + suspend:** a tab's pane mounts on first activation and stays live for `SUSPEND_AFTER_MS` (30 min) after going inactive; past that it's **suspended** (pane unmounted to free memory, dimmed in the strip) and re-mounts fresh when reactivated. Tabs restored from a previous session start suspended (lazy) until first clicked. Navigation still uses the normal `router.push` (navigate-in-place within the active tab = browser-like); keep-alive is purely TabHost keying panes by `tab.id` independent of the router.
  - `src/components/providers/TabsContext.tsx` — `TabsProvider` (client) holding `tabs: Tab[]` (`{id, href, title, icon, iconColor}`; `href` normalized to `/page/<id>` · `/db/<id>` · `/db/<id>/<pageId>` · `/admin`[`/*`]) + an **explicit `activeIdState`**. `useTabs()` returns `null` outside the provider (web no-op). **Duplicate tabs of the same page are allowed** (browser model) so active can't be derived from the path; the exposed `activeId` is `activeIdState` when it still points at a live tab, else falls back to the first tab matching the current path (drift-proof). Model = **"open in active tab"**: single navigation makes the active tab follow in place; `openInNewTab` always **appends a fresh tab** (Ctrl/Cmd+ or middle-click). All state writes use **functional `setTabs` updaters + a deterministic `auto:<path>` id** for auto-created tabs, so React Strict Mode's double-invoke (dev) can't duplicate or drop tabs (fixes the earlier "tabs vanish / reappear" bugs). The pathname-reconcile effect only **navigates the active tab in place** (never appends except when there is no active tab — e.g. after `closeAll`). Meta for `/page` & `/db` tabs resolves **live** from the `items` prop (renames reflect); DB-row (`/db/x/y`) titles come from `document.title` via a `<title>` `MutationObserver`. **`isTabbable()` also matches `/admin` and `/admin/*`** (fixed section, so `resolveMeta` short-circuits it to a static `{title: t('Workspace.adminLink'), icon: 'lucide:Shield', iconColor: 'blue'}` regardless of subpath e.g. `/admin/mailing`) — **not** a keep-alive pane (see `isKeepAlivePane`/`TabHost` below), it exists purely so opening the admin dashboard gets its own real tab instead of leaving the previously-active tab looking "stuck" highlighted in the strip while unrelated content renders underneath it (the bug this fixed: before, `isTabbable` only matched `/page`\|`/db`, so the reconcile effect's `if (!isTabbable(norm)) return;` guard skipped `/admin` entirely and left the old tab's `activeIdState` untouched — it stayed "active" in `TabBar` even though the visible content had moved on). A **global capture-phase `auxclick`/`click` listener** intercepts middle-click and Ctrl/Cmd+click on any internal `<a href>` (sidebar, in-editor page links, modal links) → `openInNewTab`. Persisted to a **single GLOBAL** `localStorage['remnus_tabs']` key (`{tabs, activeId}`; legacy per-workspace `remnus_tabs_<id>` buckets are migrated+merged on first load, then deleted; deleted items pruned on load). The provider is **NOT keyed by workspace** (one browser-style strip across all workspaces — keying it would reload a different bucket on cross-workspace nav). **Suspend bookkeeping (added for keep-alive):** `lastActiveAt: Record<id,number>` (stamped when a tab becomes/leaves active) + a `now` state advanced on a 60s interval feed a `suspendedIds: Set<string>` memo (exposed in the context value) — tabs with no stamp (never opened this session) or inactive past 30 min. Also exports **`isKeepAlivePane(href)`** (`/^\/(page|db)\//` — true for the two content-route prefixes, false for `/admin`; shared by `TabHost`'s pane filter below) and **`useTabNav()`** → `{ refresh, navigate }`: on web `refresh`=`router.refresh`; in Tauri, for a keep-alive tab it **invalidates only the active pane's TanStack queries** (the content is client-fetched, so `router.refresh` — which refetches the now-`null` server route — wouldn't update it), leaving other kept-alive panes untouched; for a non-keep-alive active tab (`/admin`) it falls through to `router.refresh()` exactly like the no-tab case, since there's no query cache to invalidate. Editors call `useTabNav().refresh()` for their manual refresh buttons.
  - `src/components/features/TabBar.tsx` — the strip, rendered **inside the `TauriTitlebar` row** (no separate row → saves vertical space). Per tab: `PageIcon` + title + hover × close; left-click activates, middle-click closes, right-click menu (Close / Close others / Close all), HTML5 drag-to-reorder. **Overflow chevrons** (`ChevronLeft`/`Right`) appear when tabs exceed the available width (scrolls the strip; active tab auto-scrolled into view). Trailing `+` opens a new tab. **Suspended/not-yet-loaded tabs render dimmed** (`opacity-50` via `suspendedIds`). i18n keys in `Layout`: `tabNewTooltip`/`tabClose`/`tabCloseOthers`/`tabCloseAll`/`tabUntitled`.
  - `src/components/providers/TabHost.tsx` — **keep-alive content host** (Tauri only; renders null on web via `useTabs()===null`). Renders a memoized `<Pane>` per **keep-alive** tab (`tabs.filter(isKeepAlivePane)` — a tabbable-but-not-kept-alive tab like `/admin` is excluded here, so `TabPane` never sees its href and never has to fall through to its generic error `PaneFallback`; the empty-state spinner also checks the filtered list so an admin-only tab set doesn't spin forever), mounts `TabPane` unless suspended; active shown, rest `display:none`. Wrapped by `AppShell` in a container shown only on `/db|/page` routes in Tauri. Also runs `useActivePaneAutoRefresh` — mirrors `useWorkspaceEvents` but, on the heartbeat `CHANGE_EVENT` while idle, invalidates **only the active pane's** queries (so a background refresh never wipes a hidden kept-alive pane's state).
  - `src/components/features/tabs/TabPane.tsx` — renders a tab's content from its `href` (the client-side equivalent of the `(app)` content route pages): parses `/page/<id>` · `/db/<id>` · `/db/<id>/<pageId>` and fetches via the existing server actions through TanStack Query, then renders `StandalonePageEditor` / `DatabaseView` / `MembersProvider > PageEditor`. Sub-panes keyed by id so an in-place nav within the same tab remounts with fresh state. (Never invoked with `/admin` — `TabHost` filters those out before mounting a pane.)
  - `src/components/features/tabs/keys.ts` — shared TanStack query keys (`tabKeys`) + `invalidateTabHref(qc, href)` (used by `TabPane` and `useTabNav`).
  - `src/lib/server/platform.ts` — `isTauriRequest()` (reads the `remnus_platform` cookie); the 3 `(app)` content `page.tsx` files `return null` when true. Cookie set in `src/app/[locale]/tauri-app/page.tsx`; name in `src/lib/constants/cookies.ts` (`PLATFORM_COOKIE`).
  - `src/components/features/TauriTitlebar.tsx` — renders `<TabBar />` first, then a `flex-1 min-w-[60px]` window-drag region, then the window controls. (`TabBar` returns null on web / outside the provider, so the web titlebar is unchanged.)
  - `src/components/features/editor/ChildBlockView.tsx` — child-block link (`<button>`) honors Ctrl/Cmd+click and middle-click → `tabs.openInNewTab` (saves first), so in-page sub-page links open in new tabs too.
  - `src/components/AppShell.tsx` — takes `items` + `activeWorkspaceId` + `isAdmin`; always wraps the shell in `TabsProvider` (toggles `enabled={isTauri}`). Renders both `{children}` (server route) and `<TabHost>` with stable tree shape, toggling only `display`: on `/db|/page` routes in Tauri (`isKeepAlivePath`) it hides `{children}` and shows `TabHost`; otherwise (web, or a tabbable-but-not-kept-alive in-app route like `/admin`) it shows `{children}` and hides `TabHost` — `/admin` gets a real, correctly-highlighted tab (via `TabsContext`) but its content always renders through the normal server-rendered route, never through a keep-alive pane. (Tabs render via `TauriTitlebar`, not a standalone row.) Also owns the desktop workspace-sidebar show/hide control; persistence is local-only via `src/lib/sidebarVisibility.ts` (`localStorage['remnus_sidebar_visible']`), while mobile keeps using `MobileNavWrapper`.
- `src/components/providers/PostHogProvider.tsx` — Client-side PostHog wrapper. `posthog.init` runs on **first client render** (not module load) so it can be gated on the server-resolved `consentRequired`/`initialConsent` props: EU/EEA/UK visitors with no stored consent init with `opt_out_capturing_by_default: true` + `persistence: 'memory'` (zero cookies/events until they accept); everyone else inits capture-on.
- `src/components/providers/PostHogPageView.tsx` — Client-side pageview capturer for App Router path transitions.
- `src/components/providers/PostHogIdentify.tsx` — Identity-only provider (identify on login, reset on logout). **Owns no opt-in/opt-out logic** — capture state is entirely `ConsentProvider`'s job (so identify never overrides a consent decision); admins are skipped.
- `src/components/providers/ConsentContext.tsx` — **Single source of truth for analytics capture.** `ConsentProvider` props: `consentRequired`, `initialConsent`, `userRole?`. Effective capture = NOT admin AND (consent not required OR `consent === 'accepted'`); applies `opt_in_capturing`/`opt_out_capturing` + `persistence` on every change. Exposes `useConsent()` → `{ consentRequired, consent, accept(), reject() }`; accept/reject write the `cookie-consent` cookie (12 mo) + update state. Mounted in `[locale]/layout.tsx` (both logged-out and logged-in branches).
- `src/lib/consent.ts` — Geo-aware consent helpers: `CONSENT_COOKIE`, `CONSENT_COOKIE_MAX_AGE`, `isConsentRequired(country)` (EU/EEA + UK ISO-3166 set; opt-in required), `parseConsent(value)`. Geo comes from Vercel's `x-vercel-ip-country` header (read in `[locale]/layout.tsx`); missing header (local dev, Tauri/Capacitor, non-Vercel) ⇒ not required (capture-on default).
- `src/lib/analytics/server.ts` — **Server-only** PostHog (`posthog-node`) capture for the activation funnel (the middle steps happen server-side with no browser). Singleton client on `globalThis` (`flushAt:1`, `flushInterval:0`; every capture `await`s `flush()` — serverless freezes between invocations). `captureServer({event, userId, allowed, role?, properties?, setOnce?})` — `allowed:true` ⇒ identified on `userId`; `allowed:false` ⇒ anonymous (random distinctId, PII stripped, `$process_person_profile:false`). **admin + demo never captured.** Consent resolvers: `isCaptureAllowedFromRequest()` (cookie+geo, for request scopes — signup/token mint) and `isCaptureAllowedForUser(userId)` (reads `user.analytics_consent`, for cookie-less scopes — MCP/OAuth). `captureAgentCall(ownerUserId, tool, workspaceId)` = fire-and-forget `agent_call` helper used by `logActivity`. See **Activation Funnel Analytics**.
- `src/components/features/CookieConsentBanner.tsx` — Bottom consent bar (`useConsent`), shown only while `consent === null`. EU/EEA/UK ⇒ **Accept/Reject**; elsewhere ⇒ informational notice + single "Got it" dismiss. Strings from the `Consent` i18n namespace; links to `/privacy`. Also nudges the accessibility widget's `offsetY` up (`window.AccessibilityPreferenceWidget.configure()`) to clear its own full-width bar while visible, reverting to the corner default once a choice is stored (see `layout.tsx` above).
- `src/components/providers/ActivityTracker.tsx` — Engagement heartbeat. Mounted for authenticated users in `[locale]/layout.tsx`; POSTs `/api/activity/ping` every 30s while the tab is visible (pauses when hidden), feeding `user_sessions`.
- `src/components/providers/LastPathTracker.tsx` — Resume-where-you-left-off. Mounted for authenticated users in `(app)/layout.tsx`; on every navigation to a `/page`/`/db`/`/graph` route it writes that path to the `remnus_last_path` cookie (1-year, `SameSite=Lax`). The `/app` gateway reads it to restore the last page on relaunch. Cookie name lives in `src/lib/constants/cookies.ts` (`LAST_PATH_COOKIE`) — shared because a constant exported from a `'use client'` file becomes a client reference when imported server-side.
- `src/lib/server/lastPath.ts` — **Server-only.** `lastPathOwnerTag(userId)` + `readOwnedLastPath(cookieValue, userId)`. The cookie is written as `<ownerTag>:<encodedPath>`, where the tag is an **HMAC of the user id keyed with `AUTH_SECRET`** (not the raw id, so a cookie left in a shared browser doesn't disclose the previous account's user id, and can't be forged). **Why (fixed 2026-07-10):** `remnus_last_path` survives sign-out, so the next user to sign in on the same browser inherited it. Validating that they *can open* the path was NOT enough — an **admin** can open every workspace (`getAllWorkspaceItems()` returns all of them), and two members of a shared workspace can each open the other's pages, so the previous user's location validated cleanly and resumed under the new account. `/app` now honors the cookie only when the tag matches the signed-in user; untagged (legacy) values are ignored. `LastPathTracker` takes the tag as an `ownerTag` prop from `(app)/layout.tsx`. (The content routes themselves were never vulnerable — `getStandalonePageByItemId`/`getSubItems` call `assertWorkspaceAccess`.)
- `src/components/providers/DebugConsole.tsx` — In-app debug console + global error reporting. Mounted in the **root** `app/layout.tsx` `<body>` (covers every route/platform). Lazy-loads `eruda` (floating console overlay) only when a debug flag is on — `?debug=1` URL param (persisted to `localStorage['remnus-debug']`; `?debug=0` clears it) or the Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+D keyboard toggle — so normal users never download it. Works on web + Tauri + Capacitor (unlike native DevTools which is desktop-only). Always installs `window` `error`/`unhandledrejection` listeners that forward uncaught errors to PostHog (`captureException`, best-effort/guarded on `posthog.__loaded`). Native desktop DevTools (right-click → Inspect) is also enabled in **release** Tauri builds via the `devtools` feature in `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`.
- `src/components/providers/AccessibilityWidgetTheme.tsx` — Reskins the vendored accessibility widget's floating trigger button (see `layout.tsx` above) to match Remnus's flat neutral palette instead of its hardcoded teal glass-morphism default. Mounted alongside the widget's `<Script>` in the **root** `app/layout.tsx`. The widget's official `--a11y-*` custom properties only theme its settings panel, not the trigger; since its shadow root is `mode: "open"`, this injects a `<style>` directly into `[data-accessibility-preference-widget]`'s `shadowRoot` using `var(--color-neutral-900/800)` + `var(--foreground)` (theme-adaptive across all 5 `data-theme` presets) with `!important` to beat the vendor rules. The widget fully destroys/recreates its host element (and shadow root) on every mount/unmount across excluded routes, so this re-injects on its `accessibility-preference-widget:mounted` `document` event rather than once.
- `src/components/providers/AttributionCapture.tsx` — First-touch channel attribution. Mounted in the logged-out `[locale]/layout.tsx` branch. On the first landing with a `?ref=` param (e.g. `ref=scoutforge`), UTM params, or a referrer, write-once stashes them in a `remnus_first_touch` cookie (90 days). The server-side `signup` funnel event reads this cookie, pins it as `$set_once` PostHog person props (`initial_ref/utm_source/medium/campaign/referrer`) **and persists it onto the `user` row** (`signup_*` columns, migration `0031`) — so the PostHog signup funnel breaks down by channel (HN/PH/Reddit/X/a partner `ref` tag). (The admin **landing-traffic** card measures ALL landing visitors, not just signups — it reads PostHog `$referring_domain` via `getTrafficSources`, independent of these signup columns.) See **Activation Funnel Analytics**.
- `src/components/features/DemoFeedbackPrompt.tsx` — Demo-only in-app feedback prompt (bottom-right toast). Mounted for `role==='demo'` in `(app)/layout.tsx`. Fires ~3 min into the session (localStorage-guarded, shown once): 3-emoji sentiment + optional comment → `submitDemoFeedback`, then a thanks screen with a "Create free account" CTA (`logout()` → `/login`). i18n: `DemoFeedback` namespace.
- `src/lib/import/notion-parser.ts` — **Isomorphic** ZIP parser for Notion exports (runs in browser AND server — uses JSZip + `'uint8array'`, never Node `Buffer`/`'nodebuffer'`). `parseNotionExport(input: Buffer | ArrayBuffer | Uint8Array)` handles the double-ZIP structure, builds a typed tree (`NotionSpace[]`), parses CSV databases with `_all.csv` files, infers column types (text/number/date/select/multi_select), matches database row MD pages to their CSV rows, strips child-page `.md`/`.csv` links that point into a page's own sub-folder (those sub-pages are embedded as child blocks — avoids duplicating their titles as bold text), converts other dead relative links to bold, strips the leading `# Title` H1 each page/row is exported with (the app renders the title in the header — avoids a duplicate heading), strips a database row's leading "Prop: value" block (one line per column, which Notion duplicates into the row body — the app shows these in the properties panel), and skips empty "Untitled" pages (Notion artifacts with a heading-only body and no children). Returns `{ spaces, stats, zip }`. Client-import helpers: `getImageBlobFromZip(zip, path)` (read an image as a Blob for direct upload), `materializeItems(items, imageMap)` (walk the tree → serializable `ImportItem[]`, substituting/stripping `__NOTION_IMG__` placeholders). Used directly by `ImportTab` (client-side parse — see `api/import/notion/route.ts`).
- `src/db/` — Drizzle `schema.ts`, `index.ts` (WAL + PRAGMAs on startup), migrations.
- `src/lib/metadata.ts` — Shared OG/Twitter metadata constants (`METADATA_BASE_URL`, `DEFAULT_OG_IMAGE`, `DEFAULT_TWITTER_IMAGE`). Imported by all public page metadata exports to keep OG image URL consistent.
- `messages/` — Translation files (`en.json` source of truth, 39 namespaces (`LandingNext`, the /landing-next marketing draft, exists only in en/tr so far — the other locales fall back to English), 8 locales: en/tr/hi/es/fr/de/zh/ru). Non-default locales deep-merge over `en.json` for graceful fallback (see `src/i18n/request.ts`).
- `src/lib/content/` — **File-driven content layer for the public Wiki + Docs (blog)** (`import 'server-only'`). `manifest.ts` = source-of-truth metadata (`WIKI_PAGES` from `docs/mcp/*.md`; `BLOG_POSTS` from `docs/blog/*.md` + per-post `date`/`description`) — **no `import 'server-only'` on this one file specifically**, so `WikiSidebar` (client) can import it directly for icon lookup. `WikiPage.icon` is a **Lucide component reference** (not emoji) — function values can't cross the server→client RSC boundary, so anything passed as a prop into a client component carries icons by `slug` lookup instead (see `WikiSidebar`). `BlogPost.icon` is typed `PostIcon = LucideIcon | ComponentType<{size?, className?}>`, so a comparison post ("Remnus vs X") can use that competitor's real brand mark from `CompetitorMark.tsx` instead of a generic Lucide icon — both `BlogCard` and the article header render it the same way (`<Icon size={n} />`), and both consumers are server components so the RSC-boundary restriction above doesn't apply here. `index.ts` = loaders (`getWikiNav`/`getWikiPage`/`getAllBlogPosts`/`getBlogPost`/`getAdjacentPosts` — the latter two also return `lastModified: string | null`, the file's real last-git-commit date via `git log -1 --format=%aI`, build-time only, `null` on a shallow clone/missing history rather than a guessed date) + `renderMarkdown` (via **`marked`**: adds slugified h2/h3 `id`s for anchors + TOC, rewrites relative `x.md#a`→`/wiki/x#a` (plus one subfolder level: `playbooks/x.md`, `../x.md`) and legacy `/share/docs/mcp|/share/blog`→`/wiki|/docs`, strips the leading `# H1`) + `truncateAtWord` (word-boundary-safe meta-description truncation). `seo.ts` = per-page `Metadata` + JSON-LD builders — `TechArticle`/`BlogPosting` (with `mainEntityOfPage`, `author`, git-derived `dateModified`), `BreadcrumbList` (`wikiBreadcrumbJsonLd`/`blogBreadcrumbJsonLd`, mirrors the visible `BreadcrumbTrail`), and `blogIndexJsonLd` (`Blog` type listing every post, on `/docs`). **No DB.** See **Public Docs: Wiki + Blog**.

### Public Docs: Wiki + Blog

The MCP reference docs and the blog are **SEO-first marketing sections**, NOT shared pages. Content is **file-driven**: markdown bodies in `docs/mcp/*.md` (reference) + `docs/blog/*.md` (posts), with title/order/icon (+ blog `date`/`description`) in `src/lib/content/manifest.ts`. Rendered server-side to HTML by `src/lib/content/` (`marked`) and wrapped in `MarketingShell`. **Server-rendered per request, not build-time static** — see the `generateStaticParams` gotcha below; the HTML is still fully formed and crawlable either way, this only affects whether it's pre-baked at build time or rendered on each request.

- **Wiki** (`/wiki`, `/wiki/[...slug]`) — hierarchical reference with a **persistent left sidebar** (`WikiShell` + `WikiSidebar`) and a right-rail **"On this page" TOC** (`WikiToc`). Built from `docs/mcp/*.md`.
- **Docs** (`/docs`, `/docs/[slug]`) — **blog**: index of `BlogCard`s → centered article layout. Built from `docs/blog/*.md`.
- **OKF/context public content:** `docs/mcp/context-first.md` is registered as `/wiki/context-first`; `docs/blog/okf-context-engine-for-ai-agents.md` is registered as `/docs/okf-context-engine-for-ai-agents`. Both entries live in `src/lib/content/manifest.ts`, so sitemap, `/llms.txt`, metadata, JSON-LD, navigation, and breadcrumbs include them automatically.
- **Styling:** `.prose-doc` in `globals.css` (theme-aware via neutral CSS vars; separate from the Tiptap `.prose-editor`).
- **SEO / AI-crawler visibility** (the whole point of pulling this out of the shared-page system — fully server-rendered HTML, no JS execution required to read it):
  - Per-page `generateMetadata` (unique title/description, canonical, OG `article` + Twitter card) + JSON-LD: `TechArticle` (wiki) / `BlogPosting` (blog), both with `mainEntityOfPage`, `author`, and a **git-derived `dateModified`** (`getFileLastModified` in `src/lib/content/index.ts` — `git log -1 --format=%aI -- <path>`). Since these routes render per request rather than at build time (see gotcha below), `git log` usually has no repo to read in the deployed serverless runtime and this resolves to `null`/omitted, same as the shallow-clone case — a soft-fail, not a bug to chase.
  - **`BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD** on every wiki/docs page (`wikiBreadcrumbJsonLd`/`blogBreadcrumbJsonLd`/`blogIndexBreadcrumbJsonLd` in `seo.ts`), matched by a **visible** `BreadcrumbTrail` component — structured data should mirror what's actually on the page.
  - **`Blog` JSON-LD** on `/docs` (`blogIndexJsonLd`) listing every post, so a crawler/agent gets the full picture from the index alone.
  - `sitemap.ts` lists every `/wiki/*` + `/docs/*` URL from the manifest (and filters legacy `docs/mcp`/`blog` shared-page rows out).
  - `robots.ts` explicitly names AI crawlers (`GPTBot`, `ChatGPT-User`, `OAI-SearchBot`, `ClaudeBot`, `Claude-Web`, `anthropic-ai`, `Google-Extended`, `PerplexityBot`, `CCBot`, `Bytespider`, `meta-externalagent`, `Applebot-Extended`) alongside `*`, mirroring the same allow/disallow rules — the `*` rule already permits them, naming them removes ambiguity and gives a single place to adjust their access independently later.
  - **`/llms.txt`** (llmstxt.org convention) — a plain-markdown map of `/wiki` + `/docs` (+ a few marketing links) for LLMs/agents, generated from the same manifest so it can't drift from the real pages. Served by `src/app/api/llms/route.ts` (`force-static`), exposed at the clean `/llms.txt` path via a `rewrites()` entry in `next.config.ts` (afterFiles bucket — **middleware/proxy always sees the pre-rewrite path**, so `/llms.txt` itself — not `/api/llms` — is what must be whitelisted: added to `proxy.ts`'s matcher exclusion AND `auth.config.ts`'s `isPublicAsset`, same two-layer pattern as `manifest.json`/`sw.js`).
  - **Raw markdown twin** — every wiki page is also served at `/wiki/<slug>.md` (`/wiki.md` for the overview) by `src/app/api/wiki-md/[[...slug]]/route.ts`, rewritten (`/wiki/:slug*\\.md`) and allowlisted exactly like `/llms.txt`. The `proxy.ts` matcher exclusion is **required**, not defense in depth: `/wiki` is already public in `auth.config.ts`, but the intl middleware would rewrite `/wiki/x.md` to `/<locale>/wiki/x.md` and 404. HTML pages advertise it with `<link rel="alternate" type="text/markdown">` (`wikiMetadata`); `/llms.txt` says so in one line.
  - Public access (the `/wiki`/`/docs` pages themselves) via `auth.config.ts` `isPublicMarketingRoute`.
- **`generateStaticParams` gotcha (do not re-add):** `wiki/[...slug]/page.tsx` and `docs/[slug]/page.tsx` used to declare `generateStaticParams()` to prerender every page at build time. `src/app/[locale]/layout.tsx` — the shared providers layout wrapping *every* route under `[locale]`, including these — unconditionally calls `headers()` and `cookies()` on every render (for geo/consent detection). A route that both declares `generateStaticParams()` and sits under a layout that unconditionally reads `headers()`/`cookies()` builds fine (`npm run build` shows no error) but **500s at actual request time** in production with `DYNAMIC_SERVER_USAGE` — Next only surfaces the conflict at runtime, not at build time, which is why this shipped unnoticed and needed a Vercel MCP runtime-error lookup to catch. Fixed by removing `generateStaticParams` from both files, so they render dynamically like every other route under `[locale]`. **Do not add `generateStaticParams` back to any route nested under `[locale]/layout.tsx`** unless that layout's `headers()`/`cookies()` calls are made conditional first.
- **Migration from shared pages:** the old `/share/docs/mcp/*` + `/share/blog/*` were DB-seeded shared pages. Those URLs now **301-redirect** to `/wiki/*` + `/docs/*` (`next.config.ts` `redirects()`, runs before middleware). The old seed scripts (`scripts/seed-mcp-docs.ts`, `seed-blog.ts`) and the `db:seed` npm step were **removed**; `build` no longer seeds. Existing prod `/share/*` rows are left in place (harmless — the redirects cover their URLs; filtered from sitemap).
- **i18n:** content bodies stay **English** (one source `.md` each); only chrome (`Docs` namespace: nav heading, TOC label, reading time, prev/next, back, breadcrumb labels) is localized across 8 locales. Nav labels live in `Landing` (`bridgeNavWiki`, `bridgeNavDocs`, `bridgeNavResources` + its two item descriptions).
- **Adding content:** drop a `.md` in `docs/mcp/` or `docs/blog/` and add an entry to `WIKI_PAGES`/`BLOG_POSTS` in `manifest.ts` (icon = a Lucide component reference, or for a "Remnus vs X" comparison post, that competitor's brand mark from `src/components/docs/CompetitorMark.tsx` if one exists there). No DB, no migration, no rebuild-of-seed — `/llms.txt` and the sitemap pick it up automatically.

**SEO checklist — every future wiki page / blog post MUST follow this, so the AI-crawler/SEO setup above never regresses:**

1. **The manifest entry is the single source of truth.** Metadata, JSON-LD, `sitemap.ts`, `/llms.txt`, and the breadcrumb trail all key off `WIKI_PAGES`/`BLOG_POSTS` — a page missing from the manifest is invisible to all of them even if the `.md` file exists on disk.
2. **Blog posts:** set an accurate `date` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, publish date) and write the `description` by hand — it's used verbatim in metadata, OG tags, and JSON-LD (not auto-derived, unlike wiki pages).
3. **Wiki pages:** the description is auto-derived from the markdown's first real paragraph (`firstParagraph` in `src/lib/content/index.ts`) and truncated at 160 chars on a word boundary — write a clear, self-contained opening paragraph, since it becomes the meta/OG/JSON-LD description verbatim.
4. **Start every `.md` file with a single `# Title` line matching the manifest `title`** — it's stripped before rendering (the page renders its own H1 from the manifest), but `stripLeadingH1` expects to find it there.
5. **Never hand-add JSON-LD, breadcrumbs, `dateModified`, sitemap entries, or `/llms.txt` lines.** All of it is generated automatically from the manifest + git history (`getFileLastModified`) — manually adding any of it duplicates or drifts out of sync with the real page.
6. **Wiki cross-references:** link to other wiki pages as relative `foo.md` (or `foo.md#anchor`), matching the existing docs — `rewriteLinks` converts these to `/wiki/foo` automatically. One folder level is supported (`playbooks/game.md` → `/wiki/playbooks/game`, and `../foo.md` from inside that folder); a page in a subfolder reaches a sibling through the folder (`../playbooks/api-service.md`), since a bare `api-service.md` would resolve to `/wiki/api-service`. Do not hardcode `/wiki/...` URLs in the markdown source.
7. **Before calling it done:** run `npx tsc --noEmit` (catches an invalid Lucide icon import), then spot-check the new page's `<head>` (title/description/canonical) and confirm it appears in both `/sitemap.xml` and `/llms.txt`.

### Common Commands

- **Start Dev Server:** `npm run dev`
- **Generate Migrations:** `npx drizzle-kit generate`
- **Apply Migrations:** `npx tsx src/db/migrate.ts`
- **Test workspace deletion:** `DATABASE_URL="file:local.db" npm run test:workspace-deletion` — `deleteWorkspaceData` leaves no database, row, series or comment behind (and a bare delete still would). Local only, cleans up after itself.
- **Test trash links:** `DATABASE_URL="file:local.db" npm run test:trash-links` — a delete to the Trash rewrites no other page (subtree, bulk pages, bulk rows), a restore re-syncs `page_links`, links are stripped only once the trash copy is gone for good (never for a live id), and the map never lists a node as both orphan and hub. Local only, cleans up after itself.
- **Knowledge map bench:** `DATABASE_URL="file:local.db" npm run bench:graph` — mention thresholds on `docs/` + a synthetic 5k-node workspace (payload, timings, activity query plan, and since P13 the code layer + `get_related_pages` resource lookup); local only, deletes what it seeds.
- **Test code paths:** `npm run test:code-paths` — how knowledge sources are read as repo paths and matched against a file an agent asks about (pure, no DB).
- **Test access requests:** `DATABASE_URL="file:local.db" npm run test:access` — the `npx remnus join` request lifecycle (see **Project Install** §5). Seeds users/workspace/requests and cleans up after itself, so it refuses to run against anything but a `file:` database
- **Test agent access:** `DATABASE_URL="file:local.db" npm run test:agent-access` — removed member → PAT/OAuth refused and revoked, viewer → read (request time and stored scope), other people's and other workspaces' tokens untouched, admin and creator-less PATs keep their scope. Local only, cleans up after itself.
- **Test email templates:** `npx tsx scripts/test-emails.ts` — sends every template to `MAIL_TEST_EMAIL`, or dumps HTML previews to `email-previews/` when the mail env is unset
- **Desktop dev (Tauri):** `npm run tauri:dev` — requires Rust + Visual C++ Build Tools
- **Desktop build:** `npm run tauri:build`
- **Generate Tauri icons:** `npm run tauri:icon` — run once after Rust is installed
- **Android open:** `npm run cap:open:android` — opens Android Studio
- **Android run:** `npm run cap:android`
- **iOS open (macOS only):** `npm run cap:open:ios`
- **Sync Capacitor:** `npm run cap:sync` — call after changing `capacitor.config.ts`
- **Build Claude Desktop bundle:** `npm run mcpb:build` — installs the bundled proxy + packs `mcpb-build/remnus.mcpb` (see **Claude Desktop (.mcpb bundle)**)

### Cross-Platform Architecture

**Strategy:** Cloud-first. All three platforms (web, desktop, mobile) load `remnus.com`. No separate API or local server required.

```
 remnus.com (Vercel)
       │
 ┌─────┼───────┐
 │     │       │
Web  Tauri  Capacitor
     Shell   Shell
    (Rust)  (iOS/Android)
```

**PWA** — `public/manifest.json` + Workbox service worker. Enables "Install App" in browsers and is the foundation for offline support. Disabled in `development` mode (`NODE_ENV`). Manifest declares the real icon set (`public/icons/icon-{192,512}.png` + `icon-maskable-{192,512}.png`, generated from `logo-square-dark.png` — maskable = logo at 70% on the `#1d1f23` bg for the safe zone) and `id`/`scope`. Mobile install UX lives on `/download` (`DownloadView` `#mobile-install` section + smart mobile primary CTA); `PwaInstallCapture` in `[locale]/layout.tsx` stashes the one-shot `beforeinstallprompt` event into `src/lib/pwa/installPrompt.ts` so the CTA works after client-side navigation. See the Technology Stack **PWA** entry for the middleware-bypass gotcha (`manifest.json`/`sw.js`/`workbox-*` excluded from `proxy.ts` matcher + whitelisted in `auth.config.ts`).

**Claude Desktop (`.mcpb` bundle)** (`mcpb/`) — one-click MCP install for Claude Desktop. `.mcpb` only packages a **local stdio** server, but Remnus's MCP is **remote HTTP** — so the bundle ships a thin launcher (`mcpb/server/index.js`) that runs the bundled [`mcp-remote`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/mcp-remote) proxy against `${user_config.server_url}` (default `https://www.remnus.com/api/mcp` — **must be the `www` canonical host**: the apex `remnus.com` 307-redirects to `www`, so the OAuth protected-resource metadata reports `www`, and `mcp-remote` fatally rejects a resource-indicator mismatch if handed the apex). `mcp-remote` bridges stdio ⇆ the remote Streamable-HTTP server and runs the **OAuth 2.1 + PKCE** browser flow on the first 401 (dynamic client registration against `/api/oauth/*`; the register endpoint already allows `localhost` redirect URIs). The launcher passes `--static-oauth-client-metadata '{"client_name":"Claude"}'` so the registered client is named "Claude" (mcp-remote otherwise registers a generic "MCP CLI Proxy") — this makes the connection show the Claude brand icon in `AgentsModal` out of the box. **No token to paste — real one-click + OAuth sign-in.**
- Files: `mcpb/manifest.json` (`manifest_version 0.2`, `server.type: "node"`, `user_config.server_url` for self-hosters), `mcpb/server/{index.js,package.json}` (deps: `mcp-remote`), `mcpb/icon.png` (from `public/logo-square-dark.png`), `mcpb/.mcpbignore`.
- **Windows multi-instance sign-in race (fixed in bundle 1.0.1 — do not regress).** Recent Claude Desktop builds start the server **more than once simultaneously** (desktop chat + the shared pool that Cowork/Code sessions use + a short-lived "era probe"). While no token is on disk, every instance 401s and tries to open `mcp-remote`'s OAuth callback server on the **same** port — the port is `3335 + md5(serverUrl)[0..4] % 45816` (16819 for the prod www URL) and, once a client is registered, is **pinned** to the port baked into the registered `redirect_uri`, so there is no free-port fallback. `mcp-remote` guards this with a lockfile but **explicitly skips that check on win32** (`process.platform === 'win32' ? null : await checkLockfile(...)` in `coordination.ts`), so the losers die of an unhandled `EADDRINUSE` and the window that lost shows "Unable to connect to extension server" forever. `mcpb/server/index.js` restores the missing coordination: an atomic `wx` lock at `<MCP_REMOTE_CONFIG_DIR|~/.mcp-auth>/remnus-launch-<md5(url)>.lock` elects one instance to run the browser flow while the others poll for `<hash>_tokens.json` (scanning every `mcp-remote-*` version dir) and only then start, never touching the port. Stale locks are reclaimed by pid-liveness + age. **The follower's poll timer must not be `unref()`'d** — nothing else holds the event loop open before the proxy starts, so an unref'd timer exits 0 and the host reads that as a crashed server. Token on disk ⇒ the whole path is skipped (`mcp-remote` only binds the port on a 401).
- Launcher diagnostics land in `~/.remnus-mcpb.log` (mirrored to stderr); Claude Desktop's own log is `%APPDATA%/Claude/logs/mcp-server-Remnus.log`.
- Build: `npm run mcpb:build` (= `mcpb:install` + `mcpb:pack` via `npx @anthropic-ai/mcpb`) → `mcpb-build/remnus.mcpb` (~1.5 MB; `mcp-remote` + deps bundled so it's self-contained, no `npx` at runtime). Build outputs + `mcpb/server/node_modules/` + `*.pem` are git-ignored.
- **Signing:** `mcpb sign --self-signed` produces a valid PKCS#7 trailer but Claude Desktop still shows an **"unverified publisher"** warning for self-signed (and local `mcpb verify` can't validate it — node-forge pkcs7 verify + self-signed not in the OS trust store). **Production trust needs a CA-issued code-signing certificate** (`mcpb sign --cert cert.pem --key key.pem`), best run in release CI (Linux). Tracked as follow-up.
- **Distribution (follow-up):** not yet surfaced in the UI — wire a "Download for Claude Desktop" link (e.g. `/download` or `AgentsModal`) + attach `remnus.mcpb` to the GitHub release once signing is finalized.
- **Install test is manual** (needs the Claude Desktop GUI): double-click / drag `remnus.mcpb` into Settings → Extensions → sign in via OAuth → run the test prompt.

**Tauri** (`src-tauri/`) — Rust shell wrapping a system WebView.
- Dev: `build.devUrl = "http://localhost:3000"` signals CLI to wait; `setup()` hook navigates to `localhost:3000/tauri-app` via `window.eval` (`#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`).
- Prod: loads `https://remnus.com/tauri-app` (set via `app.windows[0].url`).
- Entry point `/tauri-app` sets `localStorage.platform=tauri` and detects OS locale before redirecting to `/app`.
- Features: system tray (single icon, built programmatically — **no** `trayIcon` config in `tauri.conf.json`), global shortcuts, notifications, deep-link (`remnus://` scheme).
- **Single instance:** `tauri-plugin-single-instance` (with `deep-link` feature) is registered **as the first plugin** in `lib.rs`. Because the app hides to tray instead of quitting, re-launching the binary would otherwise spawn duplicate processes/windows. The plugin's callback runs in the already-running primary instance — it focuses the existing window (`focus_main_window`) and the second process exits. On Windows/Linux the second instance receives `remnus://` deep-link URLs via `argv`, which the callback forwards to `handle_deep_link_url` (shared with the macOS `on_open_url` handler). Deep-link navigation uses the typed `WebviewWindow::navigate(Url)` API (never `eval` string interpolation) to avoid JS code injection from a crafted token.
- **Close to tray:** `CloseRequested` event is intercepted in `lib.rs`; window hides instead of quitting. Tray left-click or "Show Window" menu item restores it; "Quit Remnus" exits.
- **Downloads (reworked 2026-07-10 — downloads now go to the system browser):** the main window registers a `WebviewWindowBuilder::on_download` handler (`lib.rs`). On `Requested` with an **http(s)** URL it hands the URL to the system browser (`opener.open_url`) and **returns `false` to cancel the in-app download** — the browser has a real download UI (progress, history, working "show in folder"), which the desktop shell could not reliably provide. Only **`blob:`/`data:`** URLs keep the in-app path (they're owned by the WebView process and are meaningless to another program): those still redirect into the user-chosen download folder (kept in `DownloadConfig` managed state; persisted to `download-dir.txt` in the app config dir, loaded on `setup`) and, on `Finished`, emit a `download-finished` event (`{success, path, name}`) that `DownloadToast` renders.
  - **Desktop `FileBlock` downloads open in the SYSTEM BROWSER** (`FileBlockView`, gated on `useIsTauri()`): it mints a short-lived signed link via `createSignedDownloadUrl` ([src/lib/actions/download.ts](src/lib/actions/download.ts)) and `openUrl`s it, so the browser's own download UI + "show in folder" work. **Why the browser, and why via OUR proxy not Cloudinary:** the in-app "show in folder" (`reveal_download`) is **blocked by the Tauri ACL** — the webview loads a remote origin (`remnus.com`), and custom commands aren't exposed to remote origins by default, so it failed with `Command reveal_download not allowed by ACL` and "show in folder" silently did nothing. And the browser can only be handed a *public* URL, but **Cloudinary cannot name a `raw` asset** (`.html`/`.zip`/`.csv`): measured 2026-07-10, `fl_attachment:dashboard` → `filename="dashboard.png"` for an **image** (Cloudinary appends the delivery *format*) but a raw asset has no format → the file saved **with no extension at all**, and `fl_attachment:dashboard.html` → **HTTP 400** (a dot in the flag is rejected). Only our proxy sets `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="…"` correctly. The proxy needs the session cookie the system browser doesn't share, so the link carries a **short-lived HMAC signature** (`AUTH_SECRET`-keyed, 5-min TTL — `src/lib/server/downloadLink.ts`) authorizing just that one request. **Web downloads are unchanged** (cookie'd `blob` fetch). All frontend + edge middleware, so it reaches the already-installed desktop build via the Vercel deploy — **no binary release needed**.
  - **`/api/upload/download`** authorizes by **signature OR session**: `verifySignedDownload` first (the cookie-less browser case), else `auth()` + 401. `auth()`, not `getCurrentUser()` — the latter `redirect()`s to `/login` (a 307 to HTML), wrong for a `fetch`-consuming API route. **Whitelisted** in `auth.config.ts` (`isDownloadProxy`) so the cookie-less signed request reaches the route; the route still only proxies `res.cloudinary.com` (SSRF guard), and unsigned+cookie-less → 401. Verified 2026-07-10 on a clean dev server: signed → 200 + correct `Content-Disposition`; unsigned/tampered/expired → 401. (An earlier apparent "Turbopack wedges on first request" while debugging this was a dev-cache artifact of editing/deleting a module the route imported mid-session — not a runtime bug.)
  - **`reveal_download` was also broken on Windows** (only relevant to the legacy blob path now): `Command::arg()` applies MSVC escaping, turning `/select,C:\a b\f.txt` into `"/select,C:\a b\f.txt"`, which Explorer parses as one malformed switch — it opens nothing yet `spawn()` reports success. Fixed with `raw_arg(format!("/select,\"{}\"", path))` + `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` + `is_file()`/no-quote validation. Rust-side, so it needs a desktop release. The frontend `DownloadToast` ([src/components/features/DownloadToast.tsx](src/components/features/DownloadToast.tsx), mounted in `(app)/layout.tsx`, Tauri-only) listens for it and shows a toast with a "Show in folder" button → `reveal_download` command. `reveal_download` tries `tauri-plugin-opener`'s `reveal_item_in_dir` (selects the file) and **falls back to `open_path` on the containing folder** when reveal fails (SHOpenFolderAndSelectItems on Windows can return ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND even when the file exists / fail to build an item-id list — without the fallback "Show in folder" silently no-op'd). The download-folder picker + WebView zoom controls live in the **Desktop tab of `UserSettingsModal`** ([src/components/features/workspace-settings/DesktopTab.tsx](src/components/features/workspace-settings/DesktopTab.tsx) — only shown when running in Tauri; the old standalone `DesktopSettingsModal` + its sidebar Monitor button were removed). Three Rust commands back the picker — `get_download_dir` / `pick_download_dir` (native folder dialog via `tauri-plugin-dialog`, Rust-side so no JS capability needed) / `reset_download_dir`. Zoom helpers (`applyDesktopZoom`/`initDesktopZoom`/`getSavedZoom`) live in [src/lib/desktop/zoom.ts](src/lib/desktop/zoom.ts). New Rust dep: `tauri-plugin-dialog`. i18n: `Workspace.download*`/`Workspace.zoom*` keys + `UserSettings.tabDesktop` (all 8 locales).
- **Agent auto-connect (desktop-only one-click MCP connect):** `src-tauri/src/agent_connect.rs` (new module, wired into `lib.rs`'s `invoke_handler!` — no new capability entries needed; custom app commands aren't gated by `capabilities/*.json`, only plugin commands are). Four commands: `detect_installed_agents` (checks `~/.cursor`/`~/.codex`/`~/.codeium/windsurf`/`~/.continue`/`~/.gemini` folder existence + resolves `claude`/`code` on PATH — a "detected on this device" proxy, not a install guarantee); `write_agent_config(editor, mcp_url, token?)` (upserts ONLY the `remnus` entry into the user's real `cursor`/`windsurf`/`continue`/`antigravity`/`codex` config file — JSON via `serde_json` with `preserve_order` (else every write would alphabetically reorder the whole file), TOML via `toml_edit::DocumentMut` (preserves comments/formatting/other keys, unlike the plain `toml` crate) — a parse failure on an existing file is a hard error, never silently replaced with an empty one; writes a best-effort `.bak` first); `run_claude_connect(mcp_url, token?)` (`async fn` + `tauri::async_runtime::spawn_blocking`, shells `claude mcp add …` directly — resolves `.cmd`/`.bat` npm shims on Windows via `cmd /C` since `CreateProcessW` can't exec them directly, and sets `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` so no console flashes); `install_remnus_skill()` (writes the bundled Claude Code skill to the user-level `~/.claude/skills/remnus/SKILL.md`, best-effort `.bak` first — see below). New Cargo deps: `serde` (derive), `serde_json` (`preserve_order` feature), `toml_edit`. Frontend: `ConnectFlow.tsx` (`src/components/features/agents/`) gates a new "Otomatik bağlan" block on `useIsTauri()` + the editor's `kind` (json/toml/command — deeplink editors like cursor/vscode are already one click, so skip it) — purely additive, every manual/copy-paste block stays visible below as fallback. Detected editors also get a small badge in `StepChoose`. See also the separate **discovery surface**: `src/components/features/agent-detect/` (`AgentDetectGuide`/`AgentDetectModal`/`AgentDetectNotice`) — mounted in `WorkspaceSidebar` alongside `OnboardingGuide` (same `showOnboarding` gate) — runs `detect_installed_agents` once per Tauri session and, once the onboarding `WelcomeModal` has already been resolved (avoids stacking on a brand-new user's first launch), shows a one-time "we found these on your device" modal; dismissing it reveals a persistent, independently-dismissible sidebar pill (localStorage: `remnus_agent_detect_modal_seen` / `remnus_agent_detect_notice_dismissed`).
- **Remnus Claude Code skill (`skills/remnus/SKILL.md`):** a hand-written Anthropic Skill teaching an agent how to use the Remnus MCP tools well (data model, tool/resource/prompt selection, the merge-not-replace `update_page` gotcha, destructive-op confirm flow, pagination, batch-editing). Embedded into the Tauri binary at compile time via `include_str!("../../skills/remnus/SKILL.md")` in `agent_connect.rs` — no app-resource bundling, no network fetch, always matches the exact app build. Installed as a **user-level** skill (`~/.claude/skills/remnus/SKILL.md`, not project-scoped) since Remnus is an external workspace the agent connects to over MCP, not something scoped to one repo. Surfaced **only for Claude Code** (`editor === 'claude'`) in `ConnectFlow.tsx`'s auto-connect block, as an on-by-default checkbox next to "Connect automatically" — the only client here with an Anthropic Skills concept; other editors don't get the checkbox. Best-effort: a failed skill install never blocks or masks the (more important) MCP connection succeeding — `handleAutoConnect` catches it separately and just falls back to the plain success message. i18n: `Workspace.connectInstallSkillLabel`/`connectInstallSkillHint`/`connectAutoSuccessWithSkill` (all 8 locales). **Keep `SKILL.md` in sync** with the live MCP tool/resource/prompt catalog whenever those change (same sync obligation as `LandingTools.tsx` / `docs/mcp/*.md` — see the `LandingTools` entry above).
- **Desktop OAuth flow (polling / device-authorization):** Tauri login view generates a UUID `device_id` → opens `<public app URL>/client-login?device_id=<uuid>` in the system browser via `@tauri-apps/plugin-opener` (`window.location.origin` for localhost dev, otherwise `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`) → user logs in (Google or GitHub) → browser POSTs to `/api/auth/client-bridge?device_id=<uuid>` which stores a 5-min JWT → browser shows "Close this tab" page → Tauri WebView polls `/api/auth/client-poll?device_id=<uuid>` every 2 s → on `{ ready: true, token }`, WebView navigates to `/api/auth/client-activate?token=…` → session cookie set → redirect to `/app`.
- Release automation: `npm run release:patch` keeps `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, and the `remnus-app` entry in `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` on the same version, creates an annotated `v*` tag, and pushes the branch + tag. Use `-- --dry-run` first. Release CI: `.github/workflows/tauri-release.yml` triggers on the tag, builds Windows, macOS (Intel + Apple Silicon), and Linux installers, then publishes updater artifacts including `latest.json`.
- **Requires:** Rust stable + Visual C++ Build Tools (Windows) / Xcode CLT (macOS)
- Icons: generated from `public/logo-square-dark.png` via `npm run tauri:icon` (after Rust install)

**Capacitor** (`capacitor.config.ts`, `android/`) — native WebView wrapper for iOS and Android.
- Loads `https://remnus.com` via `server.url` — no static export needed
- Plugins active: `SplashScreen`, `StatusBar`, `PushNotifications`, `Haptics`, `App`, `Keyboard`
- Dark theme colors (`#1d1f23`) set in `android/app/src/main/res/values/colors.xml`
- `android/` is committed to git (native project); `ios/` added on macOS via `npx cap add ios`
- **Requires:** Android Studio (Android) / Xcode on macOS (iOS)

## Portable Knowledge: OKF

Remnus keeps SQLite/Turso as the canonical store and exposes **Open Knowledge Format (OKF) v0.2** as an interchange adapter. Workspace Settings has a **Portability** tab (`PortabilityTab`) for export and Knowledge Health, while **Import** includes the local-preview-first `OkfImport` flow.

- `GET /api/export/okf?workspaceId=<id>` creates a ZIP with one Markdown concept per standalone page, database, and database row; a root `index.md`; `log.md`; `VALIDATION.txt`; and `remnus-manifest.json`. Internal Remnus links become standard bundle-absolute Markdown links. Database schema/views and stable IDs live under the namespaced `x-remnus` frontmatter extension. Tokens, member emails, audit records, and embedded remote assets are excluded.
- `GET /api/export/okf?workspaceId=<id>&mode=report` returns conformance plus the heuristic Knowledge Health report. Health uses the existing `page_links` graph and imported OKF lifecycle/trust fields; it is explicitly not a security or factual-accuracy certification.
- MCP turns the OKF profile into live agent value through Context Pack v2: full-corpus BM25 (case/accent-folded, plus the agent's optional `keywords`) + canonical metadata + link graph, a 1k–16k compact-JSON budget (Smart default 2k), exact-revision review semantics, stale/deprecated reranking, selection explanations, and `contextRunId`. Policy is Manual/Smart/Strict; only Strict is server-enforced, and only for Remnus MCP writes. `npm run bench:context` is synthetic regression evidence, never a customer claim.
- `src/lib/okf/` owns the versioned types, safe frontmatter round-trip, paths, snapshot adapter, exporter/validator, internal-link rewriting, and health analyzer. Native metadata is canonical and exported into OKF; unknown imported blocks survive. Imported `human:*` is `external-human-asserted`; only `knowledge_reviews` may export local `human:remnus` verification.
- `parseOkfBundle` performs the browser-side dry run. `POST /api/import/okf` repeats all trust-boundary checks, creates a separate workspace and localized Knowledge Concepts database, preserves raw frontmatter, reconnects concept links after row creation, and rolls back the newly-created workspace on failure. ZIP traversal/symlinks/case collisions, oversized archives/payloads, active HTML/script URLs, and executable Attested Computation behavior are blocked or kept inert. Import is capped at 1,000 concepts; export at 5,000 concepts/25 MB snapshot text.
- v0.1 bundles are accepted best-effort and normalized to the v0.2 Remnus profile. Unknown versions remain previewable with a warning. The current compatibility claim is experimental and version-pinned; do not call it Google-certified, an industry standard, or universally lossless.
- Focused regressions: `npm run test:okf` and `npm run bench:context`. The latter reports synthetic Precision/MRR/token behavior and must not be quoted as production savings.
